The `WindowHeading` (and the display headings) are deliberately NOT keyed on the route identity (no remount `key`) so they persist across window/page switches — that is what lets the boot sweep replay on navigation and keeps an in-progress inline edit alive (an external windowId/server switch mid-edit CANCELS the stale edit instead, since committing the typed name onto the newly-navigated window would rename the wrong one). (`260714-uco1`, `260715-zs1y`)

### Center-heading stable anchor + nav affordances

Four navigation affordances around the top-bar page heading — the anchor, the hierarchy ▾, the history ◀ ▶ pair (left-cluster chrome, § Left cluster), and their `Go:` palette parity — no new routes and no backend surface: (`260714-uco1`, `260731-oiho`)

- **Stable left anchor.** The outer center cell stays `justify-center` in the grid; an INNER container `flex items-center justify-start min-w-0 sm:min-w-[28ch]` reserves a `sm:`-gated min-width with LEFT-aligned content, so the heading's left edge stops drifting as the instance name length changes (min-width over fixed-width+truncate — long names grow rightward, accepted). Applies uniformly across all four modes; below `sm` the min-width is absent (375px). The `28ch` value is monospace-exact, visually tuned. The inline-rename `<input>` is `text-left` so the name does not jump on edit entry (§ Window Heading → edit).
- **Hierarchy ▾ dropdown** (`HierarchyDropdown`, reuses `BreadcrumbDropdown`). A bare-`▾` sibling of the rename button, bound LEFT of the prefix (reads `▾ Window: name`), listing EXACTLY the current page's ANCESTOR chain — no lateral jumps. Terminal: `tmux Server: {server}` (→ `/{server}`) then `Host` (→ `/`); board & server: `Host` only; **solo Host: not rendered** (the root has no ancestors). `hidden sm:inline-flex` — below `sm` it rides with the hidden prefix span (the hamburger/sidebar covers mobile nav). Being a sibling of the rename button, clicking it never enters inline edit. Navigation routes through the typed `useNavigate()` (params validated). It is intentionally redundant with the left breadcrumb.
- **Browser-history ◀ ▶ arrows** (`HistoryNav`). Bordered `rk-glint` icon buttons on the shared fixed-size token (`TOP_BAR_BUTTON`, § Right cluster → shared button-size token), rendered in the **LEFT cluster** immediately right of the hamburger (before the brand crumb on Host, which has no hamburger) on ALL FOUR modes (history is global) — the macOS ordering, and the reason the center box needs no width-compensation furniture (§ Left cluster). The pair sits in its own `shrink-0` group so the arrows never collapse under breadcrumb pressure. **`hidden lg:flex`** — the pair is a rung of the left cluster's degradation ladder (§ Mid-width overlap degradation): below `lg` the rigid left cluster (toggle + arrows + the nav's `sm:min-w-[150px]` floor) exceeds its equal-`1fr` side track against a 28ch-capped long-name heading (worst case needs ~832px) and the overflowing nav would paint over the centered prefix — the exact overlap class the `260715-q8ey` invariant forbids (`top-bar-overlap.spec.ts`); browser back gestures plus the palette's `Go: Back` / `Go: Forward` carry coverage at narrow widths. They call TanStack Router's `useRouter().history.back()` / `.forward()` — BROWSER HISTORY, explicitly NOT sibling-window cycling. Forward is always-active (browser-chrome style — `canGoForward` is not reliably exposed, so a dim/disabled state is omitted; a no-forward click is a harmless no-op). **Within-server window hops retrace**: user-initiated window switches **push** history entries (`navigateToWindow` — see § URL as Resumable Bookmark → optimistic navigate), so Back/Forward step through every within-server hop, not only server boundaries. No dedup — `w1→w5→w1` yields three entries. History Back/Forward bypasses `navigateToWindow` (raw `router.history.back()/forward()`), so no slide transition plays on arrow navigation; the landed window's tmux is realigned for free by the deep-link intent effect (§ URL as Resumable Bookmark, contract 1 — its `hasAlignedToUrlRef` guard re-arms on every `windowParam` change). (`260715-m4xy`)
- **`Go:` palette parity** (Constitution V) via the pure `lib/palette-nav.ts` builder `buildNavActions(mode, server, handlers)` (+ colocated `palette-nav.test.ts`), mirroring `palette-move.ts`/`palette-view.ts`. Returns the two history actions (`Go: Back`/`Go: Forward`, ALWAYS) followed by the route-appropriate ancestor entries: `Go: tmux Server` when `mode === "terminal" && server`, then `Go: Host` **unconditionally** (the Host root is an ancestor of every palette-bearing mode — only the `terminal` extra differs). `NavMode` is `"terminal" | "board" | "server"` — the **palette-bearing subset of `TopBarMode`**: the root Host route mounts no palette, so there is no `host` mode (`TopBarMode` keeps its `"host"` member; only `NavMode` narrows). **Dual-wired** (§ Dual-mount duplication): `app.tsx`'s `navActions` `useMemo` folds it into the AppShell `paletteActions` composition with `mode = windowParam ? "terminal" : "server"`; `board-page.tsx`'s `boardRouteActions` wires its own `buildNavActions("board", "", …)` call (§ Boards Command Palette) — the board route does not render AppShell, so the trio would otherwise be missing on `/board/$name` despite the top-bar arrows rendering there. Both pass `router.history.back/forward` + `navigate(...)` bodies. No new app-level keybindings (browser-native Alt+←/→ already cover history). (`260714-uco1`, `260719-i996-nav-actions-board-wiring`)

**Board** (`/board/$name`, `mode="board"`): the left cluster is hamburger + brand + a **counts/hint span ONLY** (`BoardModeInfo` — `{N} pane[s] · {M} server[s] · ⌘[⌘] cycle`, no leading `›` because the hint is not a crumb). The board name + ▾ switcher live in the center heading (`Board: <board>` display + `<BoardSwitcher>`; § center cell); there is no left `Board ▸` home button (the brand crumb is the home affordance, and a left `Board` word would duplicate the centered type word — move-don't-copy). Right cluster: the L1 **merged split control** (wired to the focused tile's window via `focusedPane`) + the board-only **autofit toggle** (the one in-bar L2 survivor) + the L3 always-block. **Kill lives in the chevron menu** (`menuOnly` — § Right cluster): the menu's Kill row is a **confirm-gated window-kill** of the focused tile's window, opening the kill dialog with a default-focused `Unpin instead` escape (§ Board Kill Confirm Dialog); unpin lives on the tile-header pin-glyph + palette (§ Close pane / Kill / § Board Tile Header / § Board Autofit Toggle). The L3 block carries the connection dot, which shows an **AND over the board's attached servers** (green iff ≥1 entry AND every distinct attached server's `isConnectedByServer` slice is connected; `board-page.tsx` derives `boardConnected` and passes it). (`260702-6m46`, `260704-pr0p`, `260704-9o7k`, `260715-6jwn`, `260718-co9z`)

**Mobile (`< sm`)** — the brand collapses to the bare icon and intermediate crumbs (server link + session, plus their `›` separators, wrapped in `hidden sm:flex` spans) are hidden, leaving brand icon + the centered heading. The leaf is the **center-cell heading in EVERY mode**: Terminal → `WindowHeading` (the mobile leaf AND primary rename affordance, `coarse:min-h-[30px]` touch target), tmux Server → the `tmux Server: <server>` display heading, Board → the `Board: <board>` display heading, Host → the solo `Host`. **The two-tone `PageType:` prefix is hidden below `sm`** (`hidden sm:inline`) so mobile keeps just the name — EXCEPT the solo `Host` word (a name-equivalent, not a prefix), which stays visible at all breakpoints. The `▾` window/board switcher stays visible on mobile with its `+ New Window` / board-switch action. Long names truncate in the center cell (`max-w-[16ch]` on mobile, `sm:max-w-[28ch]`) — never wrap or squeeze neighbors. The topbar stays a single line at 375px (the documented invariant in `fab/project/context.md`). (`260704-pr0p`)

**Mid-width overlap degradation.** Between the `sm` breakpoint (640px) and ~900px (the common sidebars-open / half-screen browser-pane case), a deterministic **degradation ladder** (class-only in `top-bar.tsx`, no API/routing/backend change) keeps the left breadcrumb from painting over the centered heading: (`260715-q8ey`)

0. **History arrows hide below `lg`.** `HistoryNav` is `hidden lg:flex` — the ladder's widest rung, and the only one above the 640–900px band. The pair is rigid (`shrink-0` fixed-size buttons), so below `lg` the left cluster's floor (toggle + arrows + the nav's `sm:min-w-[150px]`) exceeds its equal-`1fr` side track against a 28ch-capped long-name heading — worst case needs ~832px — and the overflowing nav would paint over the centered prefix, the exact overlap this ladder exists to prevent. Coverage survives via browser back gestures and the palette's `Go: Back` / `Go: Forward`. (`260731-oiho`)
1. **Crumbs truncate first.** The two left-nav crumb wrapper spans (server-link crumb + session crumb) each gained `min-w-0` so the `truncate max-w-[16ch]` already present on their inner anchor / `BreadcrumbDropdown` trigger finally engages. **`min-w-0`-chain rule**: a nested flex item defaults to `min-width: auto`, which blocks `truncate` from shrinking the item until an explicit `min-w-0` is added at that level — the crumb text had been overflowing the nav box because the intervening wrapper span, not the anchor, was the blocked link in the chain. `BreadcrumbSeparator` and the brand chip stay `shrink-0` — the protected identity floor inside the nav; the hamburger sits outside the nav and carries its own `shrink-0`.
2. **Server crumb hides below `md`.** The server-link crumb wrapper is `hidden md:flex … min-w-0` — the first element to give way, because the hierarchy ▾ inside the heading prefix already lists `tmux Server: {server}` → `Host`, so the left server crumb is redundant navigation at cramped widths. The **session crumb stays at `sm+`** (it owns the session switcher + `+ New Session` action). (`260714-uco1`)
3. **Nav clips at its explicit floor.** The `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">` carries an explicit breakpoint-aware floor `min-w-[46px] sm:min-w-[150px]` plus `overflow-hidden`. The floor budgets only nav content — the hamburger sits outside the nav and carries its own `shrink-0` + min sizes: below `sm` the floor guarantees just the bare brand icon; at `sm+` also a session-crumb sliver. The left cell's TOTAL floor (hamburger + gap + nav floor) is what holds the left budget. **Clip-backstop rule**: `overflow-hidden` converts any residual overflow past the shrunk floor into a clean clip at the nav's right edge instead of paint-over overlap — overlap becomes structurally impossible, not merely unlikely. The `min-w-[46px] sm:min-w-[150px]` values hold across the full Playwright sweep (375 / 640 / 700 / 768 / 1024px) with the 30px coarse hamburger, no clip or wrap regression — the top-bar-overlap spec is the harness. (A clipped child keeps a layout box wider than its clipping parent, which is expected; the meaningful proofs are the no-intersection assertion + computed `overflow:hidden`, not a layout-box comparison.)

**Center protection.** The center `auto` grid column is protected by holding its bounded **content floor**, NOT by a magic pixel min-width. The center cell's OUTER wrapper (`flex items-center justify-center`) carries no `min-w-0`, so the `auto` column can not be squeezed below the heading's content. That floor is *bounded* because the heading name spans carry `max-w-[16ch] sm:max-w-[28ch] truncate` (WindowHeading button + PageHeadingDisplay) and the history ◀ ▶ / hierarchy ▾ / window & board ▾ controls are fixed-width `shrink-0`. The inner box's **`sm:min-w-[28ch]` stable anchor stays at `sm:` (NOT `md:`)** — the right cluster absorbs squeeze instead. Net effect: squeeze lands on the left (and right) `1fr` columns; the center heading is never compressed into overlap across the 640–900px band. (`260715-q8ey`)

**Right-cluster degradation.** The right cluster is registry-driven and its `1fr` grid track is squeezable via `min-w-0` on the cell, so under width pressure controls collapse into a chevron menu instead of clipping (full mechanics in § Right cluster → overflow chevron menu below). It uses an explicit fit computation + move-to-menu, NOT `overflow-hidden` — a partial `overflow-hidden` on the right cluster would clip the *wrong* end (flex overflow spills toward inline-end, dropping the L3 always-block / the chevron first). The cell carries `min-w-0` but deliberately NO `overflow-hidden` (the only content that can exceed a very-narrow track is the always-present exempt chevron, which must stay visible/clickable per R6). Regression coverage lives in `tests/e2e/top-bar-overlap.spec.ts` (+ `.spec.md`): at ~700×800 with long window + session names, nav-box ∩ heading-box is empty and crumbs are ellipsised; server crumb toggles across `md`; 375px stays single-line; 1024px+ keeps the anchor intact. The width-sweep spec `tests/e2e/top-bar-overflow.spec.ts` (+ `.spec.md`) is the right-cluster's regression proof (see § Right cluster). (`260715-q8ey`, `260715-h1ck`)

**Right cluster** (`flex items-center justify-end gap-3 min-w-0`) — a **strict 3-level cumulative pyramid**, growing LEFTWARD from a stable always-block pinned right, so no shared button ever changes screen position between pages. The pyramid is consumed by a priority+ overflow menu (§ Right cluster → overflow chevron menu below): the L1→L2→L3 order encodes DROP priority (L1 drops first, L3 last) and the whole cluster is registry-driven, not hardcoded JSX. The tiers: (`260704-9o7k`, `260715-h1ck`)

| Level | Gate | Buttons (left → right) |
|---|---|---|
| **L1** — Open terminal-only + the merged split control terminal+board (`260715-6jwn`, `260722-6d0f`, `260731-oiho`) | Open: `mode === "terminal" && currentWindow && has open targets`; split: `currentWindow` (terminal) OR `mode === "board" && focusedPane` (board) | **OpenButton (Open ▾ — terminal-only, § Open split-button)** · **SplitControl (the ONE merged split chip — primary click = horizontal/side-by-side, ▾ = both directions, § Split control)** |
| **L2** — board-only survivor | `mode === "board" && onToggleAutofit` | **BoardAutofitToggle (board-only — the only in-bar L2 control; Aa, fixed-width, and ✕/Kill are `menuOnly`)** |
| **L3** — always (all four modes) | none | **UpdateChip (⬆ v… — leading, conditional: hidden unless a qualifying, un-dismissed, non-dev update is pending — `260713-4zap`)** · **RefreshButton (⟳)** |

**Terminal-mode end state: Open · Split(▾) · Refresh · chevron** (+ UpdateChip when a qualifying update exists) — three sticky/destructive controls were demoted to chevron-menu rows in `260731-oiho` (§ `menuOnly` demotions).

**Shared button-size token.** Every top-bar icon control is a FIXED square — 28×28 on fine pointers, 30×30 on coarse — from one shared constant set hosted in `top-bar-overflow-menu.tsx` beside `MENU_ROW_*`. Decomposed like `MENU_ROW_*` so state-driven callsites compose around the geometry rather than re-declaring a drifted subset: `TOP_BAR_BUTTON_BASE` (geometry only — the fixed square, rounded border box, centering, `shrink-0`), `TOP_BAR_BUTTON_REST` (resting/hover colors), `TOP_BAR_BUTTON` (`rk-glint` + base + rest — the default for a plain icon button), plus two HEIGHT-only tokens for content-width chips that must share the axis without a fixed width: `TOP_BAR_BUTTON_H` (`h-[28px] coarse:h-[30px]`) for self-bordered chips (UpdateChip) and `TOP_BAR_SEGMENT_H` (`h-[26px] coarse:h-[28px]`) for segments INSIDE a bordered chip wrapper (SplitControl / OpenButton / ViewSwitcher segment groups — the wrapper's border adds 2px, so 26+2 = 28 fine and 28+2 = 30 coarse make the chip's TOTAL box identical to the squares). **A fixed size, not a floor** — a `min-w-*`/`min-h-*` floor lets a box stretch with its content, so sibling controls in one cluster render at visibly different sizes; the fixed square makes that drift structurally impossible rather than correcting it per callsite (§ Design Decisions → Shared top-bar button token). Consumers: the hamburger (§ Hamburger), the HistoryNav arrows, SplitControl/OpenButton/ViewSwitcher segments, FixedWidthToggle, the TerminalFontControl trigger, ClosePaneButton, RefreshButton, BoardAutofitToggle, the `TerminalFontMenuRow` stepper buttons, the overflow chevron trigger, and the version row's check-⟳; the brand crumb normalizes to the same height axis (`min-h-[28px] coarse:min-h-[30px]`, § Brand root crumb). (`260731-oiho`)

Each control carries the shared `rk-glint` chip styling over that token. There is no per-control `hidden sm:flex` breakpoint cliff: controls do not vanish below `sm`; under width pressure they overflow into the chevron menu at any width (§ Right cluster → overflow chevron menu). L1's absence on non-terminal pages just widens the gap to the breadcrumb; the L3 always-block keeps its fixed right edge (the brand anchor lives in the left nav as the root crumb). Mode deltas: (`260702-6m46`, `260704-9o7k`, `260715-h1ck`)

- **The bar carries page-scoped controls; app-global chrome lives elsewhere.** The right cluster's membership (bar or chevron menu) is a role split, not a space budget: split/close/font/fixed-width/autofit/view-switcher/Open act on THIS page, so they sit beside the page heading. Theme, Help, notifications, the connection dot, and the resting version readout are app-global and live in the **sidebar footer** (§ Sidebar footer global-chrome row) and the **settings dialog** (§ Settings Dialog → Notifications row). The overflow pyramid solves *space*; this split is about *conceptual grouping*. (`260724-6j1v`)
- **L3 order**: the conditional **UpdateChip leads**, then **Refresh** — `UpdateChip → Refresh`, with the always-present **overflow chevron** as the right-most element in every mode (§ Right cluster → overflow chevron menu). The chip renders only when a qualifying, un-dismissed, non-`dev` update is pending, so its absence just tightens the gap (§ Update Notification). (`260713-4zap`, `260724-6j1v`)
- **RefreshButton** is L3 — renders in every mode (a reload is meaningful everywhere): plain click `window.location.reload()`, Shift+click `forceReload()`. See § Refresh button.
- **`fixed-width` is `menuOnly`** (terminal-gated, `hidden: !currentWindow`) — its "Fixed width" `menuitemcheckbox` row is the only reachable form. The 900px `maxWidth` wrapper lives in AppShell (`app.tsx`), which renders both `terminal` AND `server`, so **the tmux Server page keeps the constraint and the `View: Fixed Width` palette access** — the entry's row is simply absent there (the effect is a no-op on Board/Host, which never read `fixedWidth`). See § Command Palette View actions.
- **`terminal-font` (Aa) is `menuOnly`**, gated `mode === "terminal" || mode === "board"` (a terminal-bearing surface: the single window or a board pane) — reachable as the menu's inline `−`/`+` stepper row, the settings dialog, and the palette's `Increase/Decrease/Reset terminal font`.
- **`close-pane` (✕) is `menuOnly` and mode-branched** (one component): the terminal row is a real close-pane (`closePane`) of the current window's active pane; the board row reads **Kill** and routes through `onRequestKill` to open the **kill confirm dialog** whose confirmed action is a window-kill of the focused tile's window (with an `Unpin instead` escape), never a direct `closePane`. Unpin lives on the tile header + palette, not here. See § Close pane / Kill / § Board Kill Confirm Dialog. (`260715-6jwn`, `260718-co9z`, `260731-oiho`)
- **BoardAutofitToggle is board-only and stays IN-BAR** — the L2 tier's only in-bar member, gated `mode === "board" && onToggleAutofit`. It is a per-board *working* control, not a sticky per-device preference, which is why it was left out of the `menuOnly` demotion set. Mirrors the `FixedWidthToggle` vocabulary (`aria-pressed`, `rk-glint`, the shared size token) and flips the per-board desktop autofit preference. See § Board Autofit Toggle. (`260708-738w`, `260731-oiho`)

**`menuOnly` demotions — the reduced bar (`260731-oiho`).** Three entries carry `menuOnly: true` on top of the pre-existing `view-switcher`: **`fixed-width`** and **`terminal-font`** (sticky per-device preferences that don't earn permanent bar slots — both already live in the settings dialog and the palette) and **`close-pane`** (destructive, and it sat one slot from Refresh — a misclick trap). Their bar forms are excluded from the visible row, the measurement probe, and the fit budget; their menu rows always render. The **coverage invariant** (Constitution V) holds without new palette entries — the existing `Pane: Close`, `View: Fixed Width (900px)` / `View: Full Width`, and `Increase`/`Decrease`/`Reset terminal font` actions already cover all three, and board Kill keeps its `board-kill-focused` palette entry. Board mode demotes uniformly: the Kill row keeps the `onRequestKill` dialog path. `autofit` is deliberately exempt (above). Reverting any demotion is deleting one flag (the `260722-n2n4` mechanism).

**Overflow chevron menu (priority+ degradation).** The pyramid above is consumed by a browser-toolbar-style priority+ overflow menu: as the right `1fr` track shrinks, controls drop into a dropdown behind an always-visible down-chevron rather than clipping or vanishing. Registry + measurement live in `top-bar.tsx`; the chevron/menu component is `components/top-bar-overflow-menu.tsx`; the fit math is `lib/top-bar-overflow.ts` (+ colocated `.test.ts`). (`260715-h1ck`)

- **Ordered registry drives the cluster.** The right cluster is built from an ordered `RegistryEntry[]` (`{ id, tier — implicit in position, modes: TopBarMode[], hidden?, menuOnly?, menuGroup: MenuGroup, barRender: () => ReactNode, menuRender: () => ReactNode }`), one entry per control — **`view-switcher` LEADS the list** (terminal-only, `menuOnly`); then the pyramid in drop order: L1 open · split; `fixed-width` · `terminal-font` (both `menuOnly`) · autofit; `close-pane` (`menuOnly`); L3 update-chip · refresh. The `open` entry is the **first L1 fit candidate** (immediately after the `menuOnly` `view-switcher`, so first to drop) — earliest-drop placement keeps it out of the L1/L2/L3 pyramid-drop invariants the overflow e2e asserts (§ Open split-button). One ordered source drives BOTH the bar (first-that-fit render `barRender` as icon buttons) and the menu (overflowed + `menuOnly` entries render `menuRender` as labeled rows), so bar↔menu can never drift. Optional gate fields filter each render: (a) **`hidden`** (an entry with `hidden` true renders NOWHERE — not bar, not menu, not the measurement probe) — `view-switcher` `hidden: !(mode==="terminal" && currentWindow && onSelectView && availableViews && availableViews.length > 1)`; `open` `hidden` when not terminal mode, no `currentWindow`, or zero available open targets (a control with an empty menu is a dead control — § Open split-button); `split` `hidden: mode === "board" ? !focusedPane : !currentWindow` (one expression covering both wirings); `fixed-width` `hidden: !currentWindow`; autofit `hidden: !(mode==="board" && onToggleAutofit)`; `close-pane` `hidden: mode === "terminal" && !currentWindow` (the board row stays, disabled, on an empty board); `update-chip` `hidden: !showChip` (kept out of the candidate set entirely so it never reserves an empty slot); (b) **`menuOnly`** (below) — the entry NEVER renders in-bar and its `menuRender()` rows ALWAYS render in the menu (subject to `hidden`). `hidden` keeps its "renders nowhere" priority over `menuOnly`. A third field, **`menuGroup`**, names the menu section the entry's rows render under (§ Menu density + section labels) — it is pure row-placement data and does not gate anything. Gating is 100% registry data — no per-item `hidden sm:flex` empty-flex-item gap hack. (`260717-6anu`, `260722-n2n4`, `260722-6d0f`, `260731-oiho`)

- **`menuOnly` — never-in-bar placement (`260722-n2n4`).** An optional `menuOnly?: boolean` on `RegistryEntry`: a `menuOnly: true` entry is excluded from the visible in-bar row, the hidden measurement probe, AND the width-fit computation (it contributes ZERO pixels to the fit budget), while its `menuRender()` rows ALWAYS render in the chevron menu (when the entry passes its `modes`/`hidden` gates), in registry (pyramid) order alongside the overflowed fit candidates. It is a generic registry capability, not a view-switcher special case — any control can opt in (§ Design Decisions → `menuOnly` is a registry capability). **Four entries set it**: `view-switcher` (the chat lens is not fully functional, so the `[tty|web|chat]` pill is kept out of the navbar entirely and reached only via its `View:` menu rows, `Cmd/Ctrl+.`, the palette, and `?view=` deep links) plus `fixed-width`, `terminal-font`, and `close-pane` (§ Right cluster → `menuOnly` demotions). Each demoted control's in-bar component stays intact but unreachable, so reverting any one of them is removing its flag. (`260722-n2n4`, `260731-oiho`)

- **Drop order = fit candidates consumed from the LEFT; surviving buttons never shift.** The fit candidates are the registry entries minus the `menuOnly` ones (`fitCandidates = candidates.filter((e) => !e.menuOnly)`). Overflow eats them leftmost first — **L1 (`open` first to yield when present, else `split`), then the L2 survivor (`autofit`, board only), then L3** — so the surviving in-bar set is a SUFFIX of the fit-candidate order and every survivor keeps its exact screen position (the pyramid invariant). Implementation: the fit function reverses the measured widths (L3-end first), `computeVisibleCount` greedily fits from the tail, and the returned N trailing fit candidates stay in-bar while the leading `fitCandidates.length − N` overflow into the menu. `visibleItems = fitCandidates.slice(splitAt)`; **`overflowItems = candidates.filter((e) => !visibleIds.has(e.id))`** — candidates-minus-visible, so the `menuOnly` `view-switcher` (never in `visibleItems`) interleaves at its registry position and the `View:` rows lead the menu, all in one expression that preserves registry order for free and cannot double-count (§ Design Decisions → Overflow list is candidates-minus-visible). (`260717-6anu`, `260722-n2n4`)

- **Exempt items never overflow**: the exempt set is exactly one item — the **overflow chevron itself** — distinct from the `menuOnly` entries, which are not exempt but simply out of the fit entirely (always in the menu, never fit candidates). The chevron renders alone in the **trailing block** (`trailingRef`) as the bar's right-most element in every mode, and its measured width is the only thing RESERVED before fit candidates are placed. So `reserved = trailing + RIGHT_GAP_PX` (the trailing block plus one `gap-3` joining it to the last fit candidate). No `menuOnly` entry contributes probe width, so the fit budget is unaffected by which lenses the window offers or by the demoted controls' bar forms. (`260717-6anu`, `260722-n2n4`, `260724-6j1v`, `260731-oiho`)

- **Attention badge**: when an attention-bearing item is overflowed — today only the UpdateChip, i.e. `showChip && overflowItems` contains `update-chip` (`updateOverflowed`) — the chevron carries a small accent-green dot so the update signal isn't lost when the chip is hidden into the menu. No badge when the chip is in-bar or no qualifying update.

- **Always-present menu + fixed version row.** The chevron renders even when nothing overflows, because the menu ALWAYS ends with a fixed `RunKit v{version}` row (§ Steady-state version surfaces → surface 5): click copies the displayed version + toasts; plain `RunKit` when `daemonVersion` is null. When a qualifying update is pending AND the UpdateChip is overflowed, that row FLIPS to the update surface (`RunKit v{current} → v{latest} ⬆`, click = `updateNow()`) and no separate UpdateChip menu row is added (the `update-chip` entry's `menuRender` returns `null` by design). The chip and the version-row update surface share the `useUpdateClick()` hook so the updating-state + failure toast can't drift.

- **Menu-row representations** (`menuRender` closures): view-switcher → **one `View: {label}` `role="menuitemradio"` row per available view** (`View: Terminal`/`View: Web`/`View: Chat`) via `ViewSwitcherMenuRows` in tty-first `orderViews()` order, active row inverse-video accent-green + **`aria-checked`** (the single-select radio state — `aria-pressed` is invalid on a `menuitemradio`), click = `onSelectView(view)` + close (§ ViewSwitcher chip → menu-rows form); these are the switcher's ONLY rendering (`menuOnly`) and lead the menu as `view-switcher` is the first registry entry; open → **one `Open: {label}` row per available target** via `OpenMenuRows` (mirrors `ViewSwitcherMenuRows`, § Open split-button); **`split` → TWO rows, "Split horizontal" then "Split vertical"** (the merged entry's `menuRender` emits both `SplitMenuRow`s in both the terminal and board branches, horizontal first to match the primary — menu rows stay one-action-per-row even though the bar consolidated them into one chip); fixed-width → "Fixed width" `role="menuitemcheckbox"` (checked=`fixedWidth`); terminal-font (Aa) → an inline `−`/`+` stepper `role="group"` row operating on `terminalFontSize` within `TERMINAL_FONT_BOUNDS` (does NOT reopen the Aa popover); autofit → "Autofit panes" checkbox row; close-pane → **"Close pane" on terminal, "Kill" on board** (the board row carries the `onRequestKill` dialog path, § Board Kill Confirm Dialog — verb discipline: the row never reads "Close pane" on a board), honoring the same disabled conditions; refresh → "Refresh page" row (Shift+click force-reload preserved; the "page" word disambiguates from the `Status: Refresh` palette action, `260715-jykd`).

- **Leading-glyph parity — every mirrored row leads with its in-bar button's glyph** (§ Shared top-bar control glyphs). A menu row that mirrors an in-bar icon control carries that control's exact glyph as its FIRST child, riding the row's existing `flex items-center gap-2` (`MENU_ROW_CLASS` / `POPOVER_ROW_CLASS` are untouched — no row-class change was needed). This completes the "bar↔menu can never drift" contract *visually*, not just behaviorally, and teaches the icon↔action mapping in both directions. Coverage is the full mirrored set: the two `SplitControl` popover direction rows, `SplitMenuRow` ×2, `ClosePaneMenuRow` (✕), `RefreshMenuRow` (rotate-cw), `FixedWidthMenuRow` (arrows), `AutofitMenuRow` (frame-with-columns), `TerminalFontMenuRow` (the "Aa" text glyph), alongside the pre-existing `OpenMenuRows` / `OpenTargetRow` glyphs (§ Open split-button → Row icons). **`ViewSwitcherMenuRows` carries no glyph** — its in-bar form is a text pill, so there is nothing to mirror; the command palette, breadcrumb switcher dropdowns, the desktop titlebar host-switcher menu, and the bottom-bar Fn-keys menu are likewise outside this rule. **On a toggle row the leading icon is IDENTITY and the trailing ✓ is STATE** (the macOS menu pattern): `FixedWidthMenuRow` and `AutofitMenuRow` render the glyph's *static* identity variant — contract/inward arrows and the unfilled frame — which does not flip across ON/OFF, leaving the trailing ✓ as the sole state marker (their in-bar buttons keep their live state-driven variants). Every glyph is `aria-hidden`, so row accessible names are unchanged and the e2e specs selecting by role/name (`top-bar-overflow`, `top-bar-refresh`, `board-autofit`, `board-close-and-unpin`, `tooltips`) pass unmodified. (`260801-3q1z`)

- **Row density + View / Window / App section labels.** Menu rows run at the chrome's compact scale — `MENU_ROW_BASE` is `w-full text-left flex items-center gap-2 px-2.5 py-1.5 text-xs` — and every other row style in `top-bar-overflow-menu.tsx` matches it (the update-surface row, the version copy button, the `TerminalFontMenuRow` wrapper), as do the external `MENU_ROW_*` consumers (`ViewSwitcherMenuRows`, `OpenMenuRows`) which inherit the scale for free. Rows are **grouped under thin uppercase section labels** in the fixed order **View → Window → App**: each registry entry names its section via `menuGroup: "view" | "window" | "app"` (`OverflowMenuRow` carries it as `group`), and `TopBarOverflowMenu` partitions `MENU_SECTIONS` over the rows, rendering only non-empty sections — so the partition preserves registry (pyramid) order *within* each section with zero re-sorting and the `View:` switcher rows keep leading the menu. Membership: **View** = view-switcher, fixed-width, terminal-font, autofit; **Window** = open, split, close-pane/Kill; **App** = update-chip, refresh, and the always-present version row (which rides at the App section's tail, so the App section always renders). Each section past the first is preceded by the established divider (`border-t border-border my-1`) and its label is an `aria-hidden` decoration (`px-2.5 pt-1.5 pb-0.5 text-[10px] uppercase tracking-wider text-text-secondary select-none`) — the a11y tree keeps a flat `role="menu"` of rows rather than gaining group semantics. **No labels render when the menu holds only the version row** (nothing overflowed and no `menuOnly` rows — server/host modes at wide widths), keeping the minimal one-row form intact. Right-aligned shortcut hints on rows were considered and left out of scope. (`260731-oiho`)

- **Menu a11y mirrors `BreadcrumbDropdown`**: `role="menu"` panel / `role="menuitem"`/`menuitemcheckbox` rows, `position: fixed` anchored to the trigger rect (top just below, right-aligned — no ancestor `overflow-hidden` clips it, no new dependency), Escape closes + refocuses the chevron, ArrowUp/ArrowDown move focus, outside `mousedown` closes, glued to a moving trigger via scroll/resize reflow. **Row-click close keys on the target's ROLE, not the row wrapper** (review S1): a click landing on a `role="menuitem"`/`menuitemcheckbox` closes the panel (single-shot action), but the font stepper's `−`/`+` are plain buttons inside a `role="group"`, so repeated stepping does NOT close the menu.

- **Measurement mechanism** (in `top-bar.tsx`, collapse-first, no hysteresis): the right cell is given `min-w-0` so its `1fr` grid track is squeezable (the left/center floors make the track width grid-determined — no feedback loop, no oscillation). **The cell must FILL the `1fr` track — `flex … justify-end … min-w-0`, NOT `justify-self-end`** (M1, load-bearing): `justify-self-end` content-sizes the box, which both deadlocks the fit (with collapse-first `visibleCount=0` the box measures only the exempt block → budget < 0 forever) and never fires the ResizeObserver on window resize (a content-sized box's width doesn't track the viewport); default grid stretch fills the track and `justify-end` right-aligns content inside it. A single `ResizeObserver` observes the cell + a hidden off-screen `inert`/`aria-hidden` **probe row** (renders every FIT candidate's `barRender` so real widths are always known — buttons vary by UpdateChip/`coarse:` sizing, nothing hardcoded; **the `menuOnly` ViewSwitcher is NOT in this probe row**, index-aligned with the widths array) + the trailing chevron block (the only reserved node), so reserved/candidate width changes (e.g. the UpdateChip's `⬆ v{latest}` label width changing) re-fit too even though they don't resize the outer cell (S2). The measure effect's dep key is the fit-candidate id list plus `updateKey`/`showChip` — it carries no view-lens deps, because the `menuOnly` pill is not probed and its segment/active-lens changes cannot affect the fit. The pure `computeVisibleCount(availableWidth, itemWidths, reservedWidth, gap)` (front-consuming greedy fit, `gap` charged between rendered items only, clamp `[0, len]`, 0 for a non-positive budget) is unit-tested in isolation. Collapse-first: `visibleCount` starts at 0 (`useState(0)`) and is set in a `useLayoutEffect` before paint, so no flash of overflowing buttons. The cell carries `min-w-0` but deliberately **NO `overflow-hidden`** — the only content that can exceed a very-narrow track is the always-present exempt chevron, which must stay visible/clickable (R6); `.app-shell`/`header` clip any horizontal page overflow. (`260717-6anu`)

- **Keyboard path (Constitution V)**: the menu is a pointer affordance; the command palette exposes every affected action (the menu is itself keyboard-operable per its a11y contract). Coverage: `lib/top-bar-overflow.test.ts` (fit edge cases), `top-bar.test.tsx` / `update-chip.test.tsx` (chevron presence, menu open, version-row states, a working menu action), `view-switcher.test.tsx` (the `ViewSwitcherMenuRows` per-view rows in `orderViews()` order, active marking + aria, click callback, single-view `null`), and the width-sweep e2e `tests/e2e/top-bar-overflow.spec.ts` (+ `.spec.md`) — 1280→…→375px asserting no overlap, monotonic drop over the FIT candidates (L1 `split` first to yield — L2 is empty on terminal now that Aa/fixed-width/✕ are `menuOnly`, and the mobile leaf keeps the L3 Refresh in-bar at 375px: the asserted contract is the pyramid ORDER, not an all-controls-overflow cliff), the never-in-bar `menuOnly` contract for the three demoted controls, menu = exactly the dropped controls + the always-present rows + the section labels + version row, exempt items always visible, a menu action firing, and (on a multi-view window) the `View:` `menuitemradio` rows present at every width — including wide — with no `view-toggle` in the DOM and a `View:` row activation switching the lens. The e2e is the regression proof for the M1 fit-wiring (jsdom's zero widths mean the Vitest layer can't catch fit defects). (`260717-6anu`, `260722-n2n4`)

**Connection dot — a sidebar-footer readout, not a bar element.** The dot lives in the sidebar footer (§ Sidebar footer global-chrome row) with its markup, aria, and version-folding `dotTitle` intact; the bar carries no `[role="status"]` element and `TopBarProps`/`TopBarSlot` carry no `isConnected`. (`260724-6j1v`)

**A dot reads Connected only when the state socket is connected AND the page's relevant subscription has ACKED.** A bare socket connection is not enough — the subscription must have received its `ack` (the fresh snapshot). Per-caller derivation, each passed straight into `<Sidebar isConnected={…}>`: (`260716-qf3j`)

- **Terminal / tmux Server**: `AppShell`'s `dotConnected` — the current server's slice `isConnected` (`isConnectedByServer.get(server)`), true iff the socket is up AND that server's `subscribe` acked (its slice flips Connected on `ack`, and back on the socket-level disconnect debounce); in the chat lens it is the chat subscription's health instead (§ Connection dot = chat stream health in chat mode). Reconnect nuance (nice-to-have, `A-011`): on socket re-open a previously-acked server flips Connected before its fresh re-ack lands (ms-scale window).
- **Board**: `boardConnected` — AND over the board's attached servers (green iff ≥1 entry AND every distinct attached server's slice is connected, i.e. subscribed-and-acked).
- **Host `/`**: no dot at all — the Host page mounts no sidebar, so the indicator is simply absent (§ Design Decisions → Global chrome lives in the sidebar footer). The same holds for any state that unmounts the sidebar: a collapsed desktop sidebar (`Cmd+\`) and a closed mobile drawer.

**Help — a footer anchor, palette-mirrored.** The help affordance is the sidebar-footer `?` anchor (§ Sidebar footer global-chrome row) over the shared `HELP_URL` (`https://shll.ai/run-kit`, `components/global-chrome.tsx`). The palette counterpart is the `Help: Documentation` action (`id: "help-documentation"`), registered in **both** palettes over that same constant — `app.tsx`'s `configActions` next to `Help: Keyboard Shortcuts`, and `board-page.tsx`'s `boardRouteActions` — so the two can never drift (the Host page mounts no palette, and its sidebar-less layout carries no footer either, leaving the URL reachable there only by direct navigation). (`260704-hmd0`, `260724-6j1v`)

**Split control** (`SplitControl` in `top-bar.tsx`): ONE split-button chip carrying both directions — the L1 registry `split` entry. A bordered chip wrapper (`inline-flex items-stretch rounded border border-border overflow-hidden`) holds two `TOP_BAR_SEGMENT_H` segments, the `OpenButton` precedent so the pair reads as ONE control at cluster scale:

**One direction vocabulary across every surface** — the chip, its ▾ menu, the overflow rows, both command palettes (`Window: Split …` in `app.tsx`, `Board: Split Focused Pane …` in `board-page.tsx`), and the shortcuts overlay's tmux rows (`api/keybindings.go`'s whitelist labels) all name directions the way tmux's own flags do: **horizontal** = `horizontal: true` = `split-window -h` = new pane BESIDE (side-by-side, vertical divider); **vertical** = `horizontal: false` = `split-window -v` = new pane BELOW (stacked). Horizontal is the default and is listed first wherever both appear. (`260806-2x2h`)

- **Primary segment** — split HORIZONTAL, the default: `<SplitHorizontalGlyph />` (§ Shared top-bar control glyphs), `aria-label` / `Tip` label "Split horizontally", `execute(true)` → `splitWindow(server, windowId, true, cwd)`, spinner (`LogoSpinner`) while pending — the glyph shows only at rest.
- **▾ segment** — `aria-haspopup="menu"` / `aria-expanded`, opening a small `role="menu" aria-label="Split direction"` dropdown listing `Split horizontal` FIRST and `Split vertical` second (the complete option set, split-button convention — not default-only), each row **leading with its direction glyph** (`SplitHorizontalGlyph` / `SplitVerticalGlyph` — the same definitions the bar renders; § Right cluster → leading-glyph parity). The rows show the plain direction glyph even while a split is pending — pending is carried by the row's `disabled` dim; only the primary segment swaps to a spinner. Outside `mousedown` and Escape close; Escape refocuses the ▾. The primary is a **fixed** horizontal split, not last-used.

Backed by `splitWindow(server, windowId, horizontal, cwd)` from `api/client.ts` → `POST /api/windows/{windowId}/split` with `{ "horizontal": bool }`, via `useOptimisticAction` with a toast on error. Custom square-split glyphs from the shared module (§ Shared top-bar control glyphs). Best-effort — tmux may reject if the pane is too small. **Rendered on terminal AND board mode.** Terminal: `server`/`windowId` come from `currentWindow` (`server` from `useSessionContext()`). Board: the same component wired to the slot's `focusedPane` `{server, windowId, cwd}` — behavior parity with the pre-merge per-direction buttons. Window IDs are server-unique, so targeting a pinned window's ID works even though it lives in a hidden `_rk-pin-*` session; the split lands relative to that window's active pane and renders live inside the tile. Hidden when the board has no `focusedPane` (and on terminal without a `currentWindow`) — no `hidden sm:*` cliff, the entry overflows into the chevron menu under width pressure like every other candidate. The board keybindings that call `executeSplit` directly (`board-page.tsx`) are independent of this control. The overflow menu keeps both directions as separate one-action rows, horizontal first (§ Right cluster → menu-row representations). (`260715-6jwn`, `260731-oiho`, `260806-2x2h`)

### Shared top-bar control glyphs (`top-bar-icons.tsx`)

**One glyph definition per mirrored top-bar control, consumed by BOTH the in-bar button form and its chevron-menu / SplitControl-popover rows** — so bar↔menu visual parity is structural rather than copy-paste. The module exports seven components: `SplitVerticalGlyph`, `SplitHorizontalGlyph`, `ClosePaneGlyph`, `RefreshGlyph`, `FixedWidthGlyph({expanded?})`, `AutofitGlyph({filled?})`, and `TerminalFontGlyph`. It is the sibling of `open-app-icons.tsx` (§ Open split-button → Row icons) and follows that precedent's conventions: **~14px rendered size, `currentColor` stroke/fill** (so the glyph rides the row hover treatments and `rk-glint` accent-green flips for free), **`aria-hidden="true"` decoration** (the row/button's accessible name stays its text label or `aria-label`), **`shrink-0`**, and a kebab-case **`data-icon` attribute as the test seam** (`split-vertical`, `split-horizontal`, `close-pane`, `refresh`, `fixed-width`, `autofit`, `terminal-font`) — monochrome paths are otherwise indistinguishable to queries. (`260801-3q1z`)

**A viewBox/strokeWidth/join-parameterized local wrapper, not `open-app-icons.tsx`'s fixed `Glyph`.** `ControlGlyph({name, viewBox, strokeWidth, join, children})` defaults to the 24-viewBox / strokeWidth-2 / round-caps-and-joins shape the split, close, and refresh originals carry, and each stateful-toggle glyph overrides it: fixed-width is 14-viewBox strokeWidth 1.5 with **round caps only** (`join={false}` omits `strokeLinejoin` entirely), autofit is 14-viewBox strokeWidth 1.5 with caps *and* joins. Preserving each original's exact attributes is what makes the in-bar refactor zero-visual-change (§ Design Decisions → A dedicated `top-bar-icons.tsx`, and per-glyph exact SVG attributes).

**Stateful toggles expose a variant prop so one definition serves both forms.** `FixedWidthGlyph` takes `expanded` (outward/expand arrows when true, inward/contract when false) and `AutofitGlyph` takes `filled` (three 0.35-opacity `currentColor` pane rects over the frame when true). **Both default to `false` — the static identity variant.** The in-bar buttons pass live state (`expanded={fixedWidth}` / `filled={autofit}`, preserving their pre-existing state-driven flip); the menu rows render the bare default, so the leading icon never flips and the trailing ✓ stays the sole state marker (§ Right cluster → leading-glyph parity).

**`TerminalFontGlyph` is a text span, not an SVG** — an `aria-hidden` `<span data-icon="terminal-font">Aa</span>` in a fixed `w-[14px] shrink-0 inline-flex items-center justify-center font-semibold leading-none` box, matching the in-bar `TerminalFontControl` trigger where "Aa" reads as "text size". Font size inherits from the host (`text-xs` on both the trigger and the menu rows), so the glyph matches whichever surface renders it. The fixed 14px box is a sub-pixel (~0.4px) squeeze on the natural monospace width of "Aa" at `text-xs` — centered and unclipped, the one place the refactor is not byte-identical to the original markup.

**In-bar consumers**: `SplitControl`'s primary segment, `ClosePaneButton`, `RefreshButton`, `FixedWidthToggle`, `BoardAutofitToggle`, and the `TerminalFontControl` trigger — all render the shared components with their inline SVGs deleted. **Menu/popover consumers**: the two `SplitControl` popover rows plus `SplitMenuRow` ×2, `ClosePaneMenuRow`, `RefreshMenuRow`, `FixedWidthMenuRow`, `AutofitMenuRow`, `TerminalFontMenuRow`. Two inline rotate-cw copies remain outside the module's reach and are **deletion candidates** for a follow-up: `top-bar-overflow-menu.tsx`'s "Check for updates" button (byte-identical to `RefreshGlyph`, and it lives in this very menu) and `sidebar/status-panel.tsx`'s pane-header refresh (a sidebar control outside the top-bar vocabulary — a shared home would need a module neither `top-bar-icons.tsx` nor `sidebar/icons.tsx` owns today).

**Tests.** `top-bar.test.tsx` asserts, via the `data-icon` seam: every terminal-mode menu row leads with its glyph while row accessible names stay unchanged; both SplitControl popover rows lead with direction glyphs and the primary segment shares the `split-horizontal` definition; the in-bar Refresh renders the shared refresh glyph; `FixedWidthMenuRow` keeps a **static** identity glyph across ON/OFF with the trailing ✓ as the sole state marker; and on board mode `Autofit panes` leads with the UNFILLED identity glyph even when autofit is ON while `Kill` leads with the close glyph. No new e2e spec — the five existing specs that reference these controls pass unmodified (glyphs are `aria-hidden`, so no `.spec.ts` edits and hence no `.spec.md` companion updates).

### Open split-button (`open-button.tsx`)

**A Conductor-style "Open ▾" split-button in the top-bar right cluster that opens the active pane's folder in an editor/app** — the FIRST L1 registry candidate (immediately after `view-switcher`; § Right cluster), **Terminal mode only in v1** (the folder is the active pane's cwd, already derived server-side; server/board routes have no unambiguous folder). `OpenButton` is a two-segment control: a **primary segment** re-runs the last-used target, and a **chevron** opens the full menu. `OpenMenuRows` is the overflow representation (one `Open: {label}` row per target, mirroring `ViewSwitcherMenuRows`; § Right cluster overflow menu-row representations). (`260722-6d0f-navbar-open-in-app`)

**Two mechanisms, chosen by local/remote branch** (pure `buildOpenTargets({hostname, sshHost, sshUser, hostApps, path})` in `lib/open-in-app.ts`, which derives `local` and the effective deeplink host internally):

- **Deeplink targets** — from the static `DEEPLINK_APPS` const (`lib/open-in-app.ts`): VS Code · Cursor · Windsurf, each a `(host, path) => "{scheme}://vscode-remote/ssh-remote+${host}${path}"` template (vscode/cursor/windsurf schemes over the shared `ssh-remote` grammar). No API, no detection — a plain frontend const (the templates live in run-kit's frontend only; architecture.md § Design Decisions → Open-in-app). Shown on **any remote** client (all three unconditionally — client installs are unknowable, a dead scheme no-ops on click); the section's visibility is not gated on `RK_SSH_HOST`. Activation is a plain user-gesture `window.location.href = url` navigation — the browser shows its own "Open <app>?" confirm.
- **Host targets** — from `GET /api/open-apps` (the `wt` registry). Shown as the sole section on a **local** client, and as an explicitly labeled **"on host"** escape hatch on a remote client. Activation calls `openInApp(server, path, app)` (`POST /api/open`) with an error toast on failure. Hidden when the registry is empty.

**Deeplink host resolution** (pure `resolveDeeplinkHost({sshHost, sshUser, hostname})`): (1) `RK_SSH_HOST` set → use it **verbatim, never `user@`-prefixed** (an alias carries user/port/key from the client's `~/.ssh/config`); (2) unset AND remote → derive `${sshUser}@${location.hostname}` (the name the browser reached the host by — SSH-reachable on a tailnet — plus the server-derived username from `GET /api/health`; the `user@` prefix is **omitted when `sshUser` is empty**, leaving the bare hostname, and the hostname is used as-given with no reachability guessing); (3) local → empty.

**Local/remote branch** keys on `isLocalHostname(location.hostname)` (∈ `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`[::1]`). **Section visibility composes to zero-target hiding**: the deeplink section shows for **any remote client** (no `sshHost` gate), the host section is hidden when the registry is empty — when no targets remain the whole control renders nothing (registry `hidden`; a control with an empty menu is a dead control). A local client with an empty registry still renders nothing.

**Split-button behavior + last-used preference.** The primary segment re-runs the **last-used target**, persisted to `localStorage["runkit-open-last-used"]` (`LAST_USED_OPEN_TARGET_KEY`, the `runkit-*` preference convention — same shape as `runkit-terminal-font-size` / `runkit-last-pinned-board`, best-effort try/catch-noop so private mode / quota never throw). With **no stored preference** (or a stored target that is no longer available), the primary click **opens the menu instead**. Clicking a menu target runs it AND persists its id as last-used, then closes the menu. Target ids are **kind-qualified** (`deeplink:vscode` / `host:vscode`) so deeplink and host entries for the same editor never collide.

**Primary segment last-used glyph.** When `lastUsed` resolves non-null (`resolveLastUsedTarget(targets, readLastUsedOpenTarget())`), the primary segment renders `<OpenTargetIcon target={lastUsed} />` (`open-app-icons.tsx`, § Row icons) before the kept "Open" text, so the segment shows WHICH app a primary click launches. The glyph is `aria-hidden` `currentColor` decoration — it rides the segment's `text-text-secondary hover:text-text-primary` flip for free (a small `gap` spaces it from the text) and stays out of the accessible name. **No glyph when `lastUsed` is null** (nothing stored, or the stored id no longer resolves against live targets — e.g. a cleared SSH host removed the deeplink section); the segment then looks text-only, exactly as the chevron-opens-instead case above. The accessible name is unchanged either way (`Open in {label}` when resolved, `Open in app` otherwise). Scope is the primary segment ONLY — the dropdown/overflow rows already carry their glyphs (§ Row icons). (`260723-l317`)

**Data ownership — an invalidatable external store, TopBar props untouched.** A `useOpenTargets(enabled)` hook (`hooks/use-open-targets.ts`) reads `OpenContext` `{sshHost, sshUser, hostApps}` from a small **module-level external store** consumed via React's `useSyncExternalStore` (a listener `Set` + `getSnapshot`), shared by every consumer. The bundle — `getHealth()` (`sshHost` + `sshUser`) + `getOpenApps()` — is fetched **once** and held; the context is then **static BETWEEN settings commits** (no client polling, no per-render refetch — the registry does not change on its own). The one runtime seam that changes it is the Settings dialog's successful SSH-host commit, which calls the exported **`invalidateOpenContext()`**: it bumps an epoch, drops the cache, and — when consumers are mounted — eagerly refetches the bundle and notifies subscribers so they re-render with the fresh `sshHost` **without a page reload**; with zero mounted subscribers it plain-drops the cache so the next `useOpenTargets(true)` mount fetches fresh (§ Design Decisions → Open control owns its data via an invalidatable external store; § Settings-commit invalidation seam). `enabled` gates the fetch; data still returns when another consumer already populated the store; both halves stay individually **fail-silent** (a failing health read ⇒ `sshHost`/`sshUser: ""`, deeplink section hidden, never a thrown error). `resetOpenTargetsCacheForTest()` resets the store cold between tests. The store feeds BOTH the TopBar registry entry (TopBar calls the hook itself) and the palette builder; no new TopBar props or slot-context fields are threaded through, keeping the e2e-asserted chrome surface stable. (`260723-l317`)

**Row icons** (`open-app-icons.tsx`, `OpenTargetIcon({target})`). Each Open menu row leads with a **monochrome `currentColor` inline SVG glyph** (~14px, `aria-hidden`, no image fetches, no icon dependency) — full-color brand marks would clash with the terminal aesthetic, and `currentColor` lets the existing row hover treatments (accent-green flips) apply to the glyph for free. Resolution: a `BRAND_ICONS` id map first (BOTH `vscode` — the deeplink id — and `code` — the wt host registry id — key to the VS Code glyph; plus `cursor`, `windsurf`), then a `KIND_ICONS` generic fallback by `kind` (editor → code-brackets, terminal → `>_` prompt, file-manager → folder — covers `ghostty_macos`/`terminal_app`/`finder` and any future entry), then a neutral open-in-app glyph for anything kindless. Deeplink targets resolve with implicit kind `editor`; host targets carry `appKind` from the wt registry `kind`. Each glyph carries a `data-icon` attribute naming the resolution (`vscode`/`cursor`/`windsurf`/`editor`/`terminal`/`file-manager`/`app`) — the test seam, since monochrome paths are otherwise indistinguishable to queries. Icons render on **both** row renderings (`OpenTargetRow` in the split-button dropdown AND `OpenMenuRows` in the overflow chevron menu); **command-palette rows stay text-only** (no icon affordance today).

**Keyboard parity (Constitution V).** Every available open target is a command-palette entry via the pure `buildOpenActions(targets, run)` (`lib/palette-open.ts`, the `lib/palette-*.ts` pattern), composed into `paletteActions` in `app.tsx`: `Open: <label>`, with host targets suffixed ` (on host)` ONLY on a remote client (disambiguating deeplink/host label collisions — a local view has no collision). The **per-target** entries carry no chord — the target set is data-driven and varies per deployment, so no static chord can name one; palette reachability satisfies §V, documented in the module's registration comment per the code-review rule.

**The last-used behavior does get a chord: ⇧⌘O / Shift+Ctrl+O (`open-last-used`).** Naming the *behavior* ("re-run the last-used target") rather than an app sidesteps the data-driven objection, giving the split-button's primary segment its keyboard twin. The builtin binding is `scope: "terminal"` and its handler is registered in `app.tsx` only when `windowParam` is set (the Open control is terminal-route-only; the board twin mounts no handler and the chord falls through). The handler resolves `resolveLastUsedTarget(openTargets, readLastUsedOpenTarget())` over the same live target set the palette uses and runs it through the shared `useRunOpenTarget().runTarget`, so chord, palette entry, and primary segment share one execution path and one last-used write. With nothing stored (or a stale id) the chord surfaces the info toast **"No last-used app yet — pick one from Open ▾ or the palette"** rather than trying to pop a mouse menu. The pure `buildOpenLastUsedAction(lastUsed, onRun)` adds the matching palette entry `Open: Last used (<label>)`, **hidden when no target resolves** (the dynamic suffix needs a target to name — the boundary-hidden convention of Move up/down); its id `open-last-used` doubles as the registry actionId, so `withShortcutHints` decorates it automatically. (`260801-sm6g`)

**The `Open:` palette family carries one NON-target entry: `Open: PR #{n}`.** The same `openActions` memo in `app.tsx` spreads `buildOpenPrAction(prUrl, prNumber, onOpen)` (`lib/palette-open.ts`, same pure-builder pattern) **after** the `buildOpenActions` targets, so all `Open:`-prefixed rows group together. The builder returns `[]` unless `prUrl` is set and otherwise one `{ id: "open-pr", label: "Open: PR #{n}" }` — the real number from `currentWindow.prNumber`, falling back to a bare `Open: PR` when `prNumber` is absent (a `prNumber != null` guard, stricter than the truthiness check the row flyout card's PR anchor uses). The id is unqualified, so it cannot collide with the kind-qualified `deeplink:*`/`host:*` target ids. Visibility is exactly "the current window has a PR": `currentWindow` is null off the terminal route, and no `prUrl` → no entry (mirroring the sidebar `PrLinkRow`'s absence, § `PR` (L3) register). Selection is **client-side only** — `onSelect` delegates the url to the caller's `window.open(url, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")` (the `Help: Documentation` pattern), keeping `window.open` out of the pure lib. Because `prUrl` is a public GitHub URL, the behavior is identical for a local and a remote viewer; there is no host spoke (no `POST /api/open`, no exec) and hence no ` (on host)` label variant. It is deliberately **not an `OpenTarget`**: the palette↔menu mirror covers Open *targets*, so the top-bar Open split-button menu carries no PR row. Its keyboard path is the palette entry itself — no chord — documented in the registration comment per the code-review shortcut rule. This is the keyboard counterpart to the pointer-only sidebar `PrLinkRow` anchor. (`260727-w2d8-palette-open-pr`)

**Tests.** Vitest: `open-in-app.test.ts` (`resolveDeeplinkHost` chain — alias verbatim / derived `user@host` / empty-user bare host / localhost empty — the remote-implies-deeplinks gate, url grammar, last-used, cwd fallback, `appKind` on host targets), `open-button.test.tsx` (primary/last-used vs menu, deeplink navigation vs host POST + toast, per-row icons in both renderings via `data-icon`, `code` host id → VS Code glyph, generic fallback for an unknown id by kind, AND the primary-segment last-used glyph: present with `data-icon` when a stored target resolves / absent when localStorage is empty or the stored id is stale, accessible names unchanged), `palette-open.test.ts` (the `(on host)` suffix rule; `buildOpenLastUsedAction` — the `Open: Last used (<label>)` label + `open-last-used` id when a target resolves, `[]` when none does, and `onSelect` delegating to `onRun`; and for `buildOpenPrAction` — the numbered `Open: PR #123123` label, the bare `Open: PR` fallback when `prNumber` is absent, `[]` when `prUrl` is absent, and `onSelect` delegating the url to `onOpen`), `use-open-targets.test.tsx` (store invalidation — a mounted consumer receives a changed `/api/health` `sshHost` after `invalidateOpenContext()` without remount; zero-subscriber invalidation drops the cache so the next mount fetches fresh; `sshUser` carried; empty on health failure), `settings-dialog.test.tsx` (the SSH-host commit calls `invalidateOpenContext()` once on success / not at all on a rejected `setSSHHost`, the `@/hooks/use-open-targets` module mocked to observe the seam). **The remote-gate and resolution-chain coverage lives in Vitest, not e2e** — the e2e client is always `localhost`, so `location.hostname` cannot be faked against the test server. E2e `open-in-app.spec.ts` (+ sibling `.spec.md`) stubs `GET /api/open-apps` via `page.route` to assert stubbed-registry presence + menu + palette entries + icon presence (`data-icon`) in host rows; it asserts the primary segment by **accessible name** (`Open in app` / `Open in {label}`), so the `aria-hidden` primary glyph does not affect it. The **default localhost e2e environment yields zero targets** (localhost + empty registry), so the button is absent by default — the spec also asserts that absence, and the existing top-bar chrome specs (`top-bar-overflow`, `top-bar-overlap`, `window-heading`, `mobile-layout`) keep passing unmodified. (`260722-6d0f-navbar-open-in-app`, `260722-fc3b-ssh-host-fallback-open-logos`, `260723-l317`, `260727-w2d8-palette-open-pr`)

**Close pane / Kill** (`ClosePaneMenuRow` in `top-bar.tsx`): a **chevron-menu row**, not a bar chip — the `close-pane` registry entry is `menuOnly` (§ Right cluster → `menuOnly` demotions: a destructive control sitting one slot from Refresh was a misclick trap). **Mode-branched** between a terminal close-pane and a board window-kill. Props: `server?`, `windowId?`, `disabled?: boolean`, `onRequestKill?: () => void` (board routing), `label = "Close pane"`. **The label is coupled to the actual click behavior** — `effectiveLabel = onRequestKill ? label : "Close pane"` — so a row can never read "Kill" while firing a plain close-pane. (`260718-co9z`, `260731-oiho`)
- **Terminal mode**: the optimistic close-pane path — `closePane(server, windowId)` via `useOptimisticAction`, `disabled` while `isPending`. `POST /api/windows/{windowId}/close-pane` → `tmux.KillActivePane`. The row renders only when `currentWindow` exists; also "Pane: Close" in the palette.
- **Board mode**: when `onRequestKill` is present (registered by `BoardPage`) the row calls it INSTEAD of `closePane`, opening the **kill confirm dialog** (§ Board Kill Confirm Dialog) rather than firing immediately; the confirmed action is a window-kill (`killWindow`), not a close-pane, with an `Unpin instead` escape. Its label reads "Kill". `disabled={!focusedPane}` so the row is disabled on an empty board. The confirm gate + escalation exist because a board pane's blast radius — the whole window everywhere, including its home session — is otherwise illegible. (`260715-6jwn`, `260718-co9z`)
- **Self-heal seam**: post-kill board refetch rides `executeKillWindow`'s own `onSettled` in `board-page.tsx` (§ Board Kill Confirm Dialog), not a callback on this row. The self-heal covers the no-`board-changed`-on-kill gap: killing a window collapses its single-window pin-session with no `board-changed` SSE event — fired only by pin/unpin/reorder — so the refetch lets `getBoard` drop the vanished tile and an emptied board fall out of `GET /api/boards`.

The **in-bar ✕ chip** (`ClosePaneButton` — `<ClosePaneGlyph />` from the shared glyph module, the same definition `ClosePaneMenuRow` leads with, on the shared `TOP_BAR_BUTTON` token; `LogoSpinner` while pending) survives in the codebase intact but unreachable under the `menuOnly` flag, so a revert is deleting that flag (the `260722-n2n4` mechanism); it is a **deletion candidate** for a later change that confirms the demotion sticks. Unpin is not on the close/Kill affordance at all — it lives on the tile-header pin-glyph button (§ Board Tile Header) and the `Board: Unpin Focused Pane` palette action (§ Boards Command Palette), both driven by the shared `unpinFocused` handler in `board-page.tsx` (`unpin(entries[focusedIndex].server, .windowId, name)`, `POST /api/boards/{name}/unpin`). (`260715-h1ck`)

**Board autofit toggle** (`BoardAutofitToggle` in `top-bar.tsx`): the L2 tier's **board-only** toggle and its only IN-BAR member, gated on `mode === "board" && onToggleAutofit` (it never renders on the Terminal route). Props: `autofit: boolean`, `onToggle: () => void`. Mirrors the `FixedWidthToggle` vocabulary — `aria-label="Toggle board autofit"`, `aria-pressed={autofit}`, shared `rk-glint` glint hover over the `TOP_BAR_BUTTON_BASE` size token, `title` reflecting on/off ("Autofit on (panes fill the row)" / "Autofit off (fixed pane widths)"). It renders `<AutofitGlyph filled={autofit} />` (§ Shared top-bar control glyphs) — the frame-with-columns glyph gaining filled panes when on — the same definition `AutofitMenuRow` leads with, though the menu row renders the *unfilled* identity default so its trailing ✓ stays the sole state marker. Clicking it flips the per-board desktop autofit preference; the palette counterpart `Board: Toggle Autofit (on/off)` flips the SAME state (Constitution V parity). It is wired from `BoardPage` through the top-bar slot context (`autofit` + `onToggleAutofit`) → `RootTopBar` (`app.tsx`) → `TopBar` props (tolerant-empty: absent `onToggleAutofit` ⇒ no toggle). The behavior it controls (equal-share flex layout, hidden resize handles, non-destructive stored-width round-trip) lives in § Board Autofit Toggle. (`260708-738w`)

**Refresh button** (`RefreshButton` in `top-bar.tsx`): part of the **L3 always-block** — a reload is meaningful on every page, and it is the block's trailing fit candidate (after the conditional `UpdateChip`, before the exempt chevron). It is a registry candidate (`id: "refresh"`) — it renders in-bar in all four modes when it fits and overflows into the chevron menu as a "Refresh page" row otherwise. A **plain `<button type="button">`** whose `onClick` does a **full page reload** — plain click `window.location.reload()`; **Shift+click force-reloads** via the exported `forceReload()` helper (a `fetch(location.href, { cache: "reload" })` that overwrites the document's HTTP cache entry, then `reload()` in `.finally` so it fires even offline — Chrome Shift+reload semantics. The fetch races a 3s timeout (`Promise.race`, then `clearTimeout` in `.finally`) so a **stalled** socket that never resolves *or* rejects still reloads — `.catch` alone covers rejection but not an indefinite hang). `aria-label` "Refresh page", `title` "Refresh page (Shift+click: force reload)", carrying the shared fixed-size `TOP_BAR_BUTTON` token (§ Right cluster → shared button-size token) and `<RefreshGlyph />` from the shared glyph module (lucide `rotate-cw` — a circular arrow + top-right arrowhead, 14×14 `aria-hidden`, `stroke="currentColor" strokeWidth="2"`, round caps/joins) — the same definition `RefreshMenuRow` leads with, matching the sibling split/close glyphs (§ Shared top-bar control glyphs). It is the in-app recovery affordance for degraded terminal views (stale relay / wedged xterm / SSE drift) where browser chrome is out of reach (PWA/standalone/kiosk). Deliberately **NO spinner / `useOptimisticAction` / `isPending` / `LogoSpinner`, NO `disabled` state, and NO confirmation** (unlike Split / Close) — the page unloads synchronously so a spinner would never meaningfully render, and the reload is non-destructive by design (constitution II/VI: all client state re-derives on load; tmux sessions are unaffected). The same action is reachable from the command palette as "View: Refresh Page" (see § Boards Command Palette / § Command Palette View actions below). It is a full reload rather than a targeted relay-reconnect (§ Design Decisions → Refresh is a full page reload). (`260703-p29w`, `260704-9o7k`, `260715-h1ck`)

**Toolbar button color convention**: All toolbar buttons (top bar and bottom bar) use `text-text-secondary` as their default foreground color. Active toggle states (Ctrl/Alt modifiers when armed, FixedWidthToggle when active) use `text-accent` with accent background. Hover state uses `hover:border-text-secondary` (border highlight). This convention applies to: compose button, fixed-width toggle, the split control, the close/Kill affordance, the sidebar-footer icon cluster (help/theme/gear — borderless, so hover is a color flip only), Esc, Tab, Ctrl, Alt, Fn trigger, arrow pad, and ⌘K.

### Window Heading (`WindowHeading` in `top-bar.tsx`)

The centered, highlighted, editable window heading — the single most important identity on the Terminal route (the tmux window name, e.g. `riff-gallant-jackal`). It gives the window name the prominence of a document title (mental model: browser-tab / editor title bar) and makes rename lowest-friction direct manipulation. Lives in the top bar's center grid cell (§ Chrome (Top Bar) center cell); renders only when `mode === "terminal" && currentWindow`. It is the terminal instance of the universal `PageType: name` heading — a static prefix span sits OUTSIDE the rename button, and the name is driven by the shared boot-sweep primitive (`useBootSweep`/`SweepCells`, § Hover-Animation Vocabulary → boot sweep). **The prefix is a static `Window:` in every lens** (§ Heading is a static `Window:`): `WindowHeading` takes a `prefix` prop, but the sole call site passes the CONSTANT `WINDOW_PREFIX` — the heading does not vary by lens (the `ViewSwitcher` shows the lens — its `menuOnly` `View:` menu rows in the chevron menu). (`260704-pr0p`, `260714-uco1`, `260717-6anu`, `260722-n2n4`)

**Two-tone prefix**: the `Window:` prefix (static in every lens) renders `text-text-secondary` as a **static sibling span outside the rename button/input** (`hidden sm:inline` — mobile keeps just the name) — clicking it never starts an edit, and the button's `aria-label` stays clean. The name keeps `font-semibold text-text-primary`. The prefix's frame states (dim ahead of cursor / typed behind) come from the boot sweep's `prefix` half. (`260704-pr0p`, `260714-uco1`)

**Three states** (one component):

- **display** — a `<button aria-label="Rename window {name}" title="Click to rename">` rendering the name (via `<SweepCells>` at rest → plain text) at `text-sm font-semibold text-text-primary` (weight-600 + primary color IS the "highlighted" rest state; the surrounding crumbs are secondary, so weight+color alone read as highlighted — no background). `max-w-[16ch] sm:max-w-[28ch] inline-flex items-center coarse:min-h-[30px]` (mobile-leaf touch target), with the name inside an inner `min-w-0 truncate` span — truncation MUST live on the span, not the button: `text-overflow` is inert on a flex container, and flex centering clipped long names on BOTH ends with no ellipsis (`riff-blustery-whale` → `iff-blustery-whal`). Left-anchored, so long names keep their head and cut at the tail; the button itself stays centered in the grid cell. Click enters edit.
- **boot sweep** — the JS sweep from `useBootSweep` (§ Hover-Animation Vocabulary → boot sweep). One inverse-video accent-green cursor at `DECODE_FRAME_MS = 28`ms/cell sweeps `Window:` + space + name; over the prefix it types (TypedLabel states), over the name it churns `DECODE_GLYPHS`. The heading container does NOT flip `text-accent-green` for the whole scramble — green lives ONLY on the per-cell churn/cursor spans inside `SweepCells`; each resolved cell settles to its own REST color (`restCellClass`: prefix → `text-secondary`, name → semibold `text-primary`), so the left-to-right two-tone reveal is visible instead of a uniform green flash. **Guards**: (a) a `DECODE_HOVER_INTENT_MS = 140`ms hover-intent delay before the sweep starts (cursor transit toward the right buttons doesn't replay it); (b) edit-start stops the sweep and the input binds to the real `name`/`draft` state, never the scrambled DOM text; (c) the sweep replays once whenever the **DISPLAYED name changes** — a `prevNameRef` **seeded `null`** effect keyed on the incoming `name` prop. The null seed plays the sweep ONCE on mount (first name resolution flips `prevNameRef` from `null`), so route navigation between page types animates — AND a committed rename, an SSE external rename, and window navigation all replay through the same name-change mechanism. This is why the instance is NOT remounted per window — a fresh mount would re-seed `prevNameRef` to `null` and replay every time / double-play. Skipped entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion` (`prefersReducedMotion()` reads `window.matchMedia`, guarded for jsdom — the rest state IS the reduced-motion state). (`260704-pr0p`)
- **edit** — an identically-styled inline `<input aria-label="Window name">` (monospace, `text-left font-semibold text-text-primary`, `border-b border-accent`; `text-left` so the name does not jump horizontally on edit entry given the left-anchored heading), width sized in `ch` and growing with content (`Math.max(draft.length + 1, 3)ch`), `autoFocus` + select-on-focus. **Enter commits, Escape cancels, blur commits**; empty/whitespace-only trim on commit = cancel (matches the sidebar/dialog trim guard); an unchanged name is also a no-op. Commit calls the shared `useWindowRename()` hook (§ Shared window rename below) — optimistic store rename + toast on error — then optimistically sets the display; the name-change effect replays the sweep when the store-driven `name` prop settles. (`260714-uco1`)

**Keyboard path (Constitution V)**: the command-palette `Window: Rename` action dispatches a `window-heading:rename` `CustomEvent` (mirroring `theme-selector:open`); a document-level listener in `WindowHeading` enters inline edit. There is no rename modal dialog — the inline edit (heading + sidebar) is the only window-rename surface; see § Keyboard Shortcuts (the palette rename action) and § Component Conventions (the shared `Dialog`). Only the SESSION rename dialog lives in `app.tsx`.

**External-switch cancel**: a `prevIdentityRef` effect keyed on `${server}:${windowId}` cancels an in-progress edit when the window identity changes out from under it (another client / tmux switching the route) — committing the typed name onto the newly-navigated window would rename the wrong window.

### Display-only page headings (`PageHeadingDisplay` in `top-bar.tsx`)

The board/server/host siblings of `WindowHeading` — a `PageHeadingDisplay({ prefix, name, solo?, ariaLabel })` that renders the same `PageType: name` two-tone identity via the SAME `useBootSweep`/`SweepCells` primitive, but with NO rename affordance (boards/servers have no rename API; the host mode has no instance). The static prefix span is `hidden sm:inline` (same mobile rule as terminal); the `solo` variant (host) renders the single word primary-medium and visible at all breakpoints. Hover replays the sweep behind the 140ms intent; mount + displayed-name-change replay via the same `null`-seeded `prevNameRef` effect; reduced motion skips. **A11y**: the identity carries a STABLE `aria-label` (the churn glyphs are `aria-hidden` inside `SweepCells`) placed on a `role="group"` wrapper — `aria-label` is prohibited on the implicit `role="generic"` of a bare `<div>`/`<span>`, so `role="group"` is what makes it a valid, screen-reader-stable accessible name. No `<h1>` is added to the top bar. (`260704-pr0p`)

**Tests**: `top-bar.test.tsx` covers the heading display/edit states (click → input, Enter commits via `renameWindow`, Escape/empty-trim cancel), the `window-heading:rename` CustomEvent entering edit, AND per-mode heading coverage (terminal `Window:` prefix sibling, board `Board:` + board switcher in the center, server `tmux Server:`, solo `Host`; board left nav shows only counts/hint — no `Board ▸`, no left board name; server left nav has no `aria-current` leaf). E2e `tests/e2e/window-heading.spec.ts` (+ sibling `.spec.md`, per the constitution's Test Companion Docs rule) covers, documented 1:1 in the `.spec.md`: the terminal heading renders the name once with a static `Window:` prefix sibling (NOT a trailing breadcrumb crumb); click → inline input → Enter commits (sidebar + heading reflect the new name); Escape cancels (window id unchanged); the palette `window-heading:rename` CustomEvent enters inline edit; the 375px bar stays single-line with no horizontal overflow; hover-treatment classes present and unchanged under `reducedMotion: "reduce"`; the board/server/host centered headings render with their prefixes; the `[ SESSIONS▊ ]` section-heading shape; a motion-opted-in **boot-sweep** e2e (hover the heading → an `.rk-typed-cursor` cell attaches → resolves to rest, since the units stub `matchMedia` to reduced-motion and never exercise the sweep behaviorally); and a reduced-context check that the sweep never starts (JS-gated). (`260704-pr0p`, `260714-uco1`)

### Shared window rename (`useWindowRename`, `hooks/use-window-rename.ts`)

`useWindowRename()` returns `execute(server, session, windowId, newName)` — the ONE optimistic window-rename action, used by BOTH the top-bar `WindowHeading` AND the sidebar inline rename (`sidebar/index.tsx:398`), so the two surfaces rename identically. It stamps the window store's `pendingName` (`renameWindow`) on `onOptimistic`, rolls back via `clearRename` on failure with a toast, and clears the pending marker on settle (SSE snapshot then becomes authoritative). The captured `(server, session, windowId)` is held in a `lastRenameRef` so rollback/settle target the right entry even if the caller re-renders between execute and resolution. Callers own their own trim/empty-name guards before calling (rename is unconditional inside the hook). Session rename is NOT part of this hook — it keeps its own dialog/inline path (§ Sidebar → Session rename). (`260703-5ilm`)

### Hover-Animation Vocabulary

A site-wide motion map where each treatment encodes **exactly one** category of element, so motion carries meaning instead of noise. The shared color cue: **the animated element turns `--color-accent-green` while it animates** (the brand chip on glitch-hover, the boot-sweep cursor + name-churn cells, both caret variants, the stepping brackets — user-requested — including the glint: the sweep strip is accent-green-tinted and the hovered chip's border + glyph flip green for the hover duration, the "green line"). **Scope note**: the boot-sweep green is PER-CELL — only the single cursor cell and the unresolved name-churn glyphs are green; the heading container does NOT tint green wholesale, so resolved cells settle to their own rest color and the two-tone reveal reads left-to-right (`260704-pr0p`). All CSS utilities live in `globals.css` (beside the `logo-chase` keyframes / `coarse` variant; Tailwind CSS 4).
