- **Data source**: the existing **`useBoards()`** hook (`plain /api/boards` fetch + the shared SSE pool — NO new API/endpoint; the same hook `board-page.tsx` and the sidebar consume). Returns `{ boards, isLoading }`.
- **Heading row**: a `<SectionHeading label="Boards" className="mb-2" />` (§ Section Heading). The board count / `loading…` is NOT in the SectionHeading `side` slot (reserved for the server-view stats) — it stays in the zone BODY, a `text-xs text-text-secondary font-mono mb-2` line right below the heading (`loading…` before the first fetch via `isLoading`, else `{N} board(s)`). (`260704-pr0p`)
- **Tiles**: one `<button>` per board (same tile idiom as the server tiles — `bg-bg-card border border-border rounded p-4 hover:border-text-secondary min-h-[60px]`) showing the board name + `{N} pin(s)`; clicking navigates to `/board/$name`. **Drag-reorderable**: tiles render `orderedBoards` from the shared `useBoardListReorder` hook (same instance seam as the sidebar) with `getTileProps(b.name)` spread onto each and the dragged tile dimmed `opacity-50` — see § Board-list drag reorder. (`260708-a2qd`)
- **Always visible**: the section renders even with zero boards — the empty state shows the sidebar's "Pin a window to start a board" hint (mirroring `BoardsSection`) instead of the section appearing/vanishing with the first/last board.
- **Not a shared component**: the tile markup is NOT shared with the sidebar `boards-section.tsx` (a parallel surface; the sidebar section drives the actual sidebar).

### SERVICES Zone on `/` (Host-page host-console home)

`HostOverviewPage` renders a **SERVICES** zone (`<section aria-label="Services">`) as the LAST zone, below the TMUX SERVERS tile grid, inside the same scroll container. It surfaces the host's listening TCP **ports** as tiles — a listening port is a HOST property (owned by no tmux window/session), so `/` (the box-level console) is its home, mirroring the HOST HEALTH rationale. The backend (`internal/ports`) passively enumerates and broadcasts **ALL** listening ports (procfs on Linux, lsof on darwin) with best-effort process attribution on BOTH platforms — no probing, no HTTP filter (see architecture.md § `internal/ports`). A tile is therefore **not guaranteed to speak HTTP**; the frontend does NO protocol guessing and applies no client-side denylist. Read-only awareness view; the only action opens a port's UI in a tmux window (Constitution IV — nothing mutates host state directly). (`260701-5d0z`, `260703-8y2a`, `260715-vfcz`)

- **Data source**: **`useHostServices()`** (its own `HostServicesContext`, separate from `HostMetricsContext` so the ~2.5s services stream doesn't cascade re-renders into metrics-only consumers). It rides the SAME server-independent broadcast as host metrics — the `event: services` frame arrives on every stream (the dedicated `?metrics=1` stream on a bare `/`, and each per-server stream's fan-out), deduped on the raw payload string (`applyHostServices`). Returns `[]` (never null) before the first tick, so consumers map over it unconditionally. See architecture.md § API Layer `/api/sessions/stream` (`event: services`) and § Server-independent host-metrics subscription.
- **Tiles**: each `Service` (from `@/types` — `{ port, process?, pid? }`) renders a row keyed by `svc.port` showing `:{port}` (mono) + the optional `process` name. **Process attribution is best-effort on BOTH platforms**: macOS populates `process`/`pid` from lsof; Linux joins lsof attribution onto its procfs port set and populates them where lsof can attribute the port, leaving them absent (bare tile) for ports lsof can't see (e.g. root-owned `sshd :22` under a non-root lsof) — see architecture.md § `internal/ports`. Empty state: a graceful `No services` affordance when the list is empty (pre-first-tick, nothing listening, or an unsupported platform where the collector returns `[]` — Windows/BSD; macOS and Linux both enumerate). (`260715-vfcz`)
- **"Open in window" action** (`handleOpenInWindow`): opens the port's UI in an `@rk_type=iframe` tmux window via the existing `/proxy/{port}/` reverse proxy (`createWindow(server, session, "port-{port}", undefined, "iframe", "/proxy/{port}/")` — the `rkType`/`rkUrl` args become the `@rk_type`/`@rk_url` window options), then navigates to `/$server` (the new window surfaces via SSE). The window **name** is `port-{port}`, NOT `:{port}` — tmux rejects colons and periods in window names (`validate.ValidateName`); the tile still *displays* `:{port}`. Since `/` is server-less, it resolves a **target (server, session)**: `servers[0]` + that server's first session from the SSE cache (`sessionsByServer`), else an authoritative `getSessions()` fetch (the per-server stream is usually not attached yet on a fresh `/` load, so the cache can be empty even when sessions exist — fetching avoids a duplicate-name 500 on `new-session`), else an instant `"services"` session created on the fly (`createSession`). An `openingRef` guard debounces a double-click into a single create flow. `servers[0]` reflects the infra-last sort applied in `session-context.tsx` `fetchServers` (see § Infra-server de-emphasis), so a real workspace is preferred over `rk-daemon` as the "first-listed server" target. (`260705-uh8f`)
- **Button gated SOLELY on server existence**: the "Open in window" button is `disabled` with `title="Create a server first"` ONLY when `servers.length === 0` (there is no target to host the iframe window); otherwise it is enabled regardless of port number. The tile still renders in the disabled case.
- **Well-known-port de-emphasis**: with the SERVICES zone showing ALL listening ports, well-known/system ports (`port < 1024`, the reserved range — `sshd:22`, `smtp:25`, …) are visually de-emphasized, mirroring the `isInfraServer` server-tile treatment: their `:{port}` renders grey (`text-text-secondary` instead of `text-text-primary`) and they sort **last as a class**. `isWellKnownPort(port) = port < 1024`; the `orderedServices` `useMemo` partitions `hostServices` into regular vs. well-known, sorts each sub-group by port ascending, and concatenates (regular first) — so regular tiles keep their port-ascending order and well-known tiles trail. **Presentation-only, applied at render in `host-overview-page.tsx`**: the backend snapshot stays port-sorted; this is a frontend concern (parallel to the `session-context.tsx` infra-last server sort — see § Infra-server de-emphasis). Every tile, well-known or not, renders with its "Open in window" button. (`260715-vfcz`)
- **No HTTP gate anywhere**: there is no client-side HTTP-likely heuristic (no `NON_HTTP_PORTS` denylist, no `isLikelyHttpPort(port)`, no disabled branch, no `"Not a web service"` tooltip, no `handleOpenInWindow` guard) and no denylist or HTTP gate on the server; **do not re-add one**. The backend broadcasts ALL listening ports and does no probing, so a tile is not guaranteed to be a web service; **opening a non-HTTP port yields a user-initiated failed iframe — the iframe load IS the on-demand probe** (visible and harmless; there is no ambient probe). The empty state is `No services` (meaning "no listening ports"). `types.ts` `Service`/`ServicesSnapshot` carry no `http:` flag — there is no HTTP verdict. (`260703-8y2a`, `260715-vfcz`)

### Color Tinting — Owned Palette + Axis Split

Session rows, window rows, and server tiles support an optional color assignment that applies a full-width background tint. The palette is an **owned set of 10 fixed OKLCH hue families adapted to the active theme**: each family has a stable hue angle, and hue identity survives theme switches (a window labeled "orange" reads orange on every theme). The row's visual channels are split so labeling and selection never collide: **hue = label**, **tint depth = selection**, **left-edge marker = an independent label axis** (§ Left-Edge Label Zone). On window rows both label axes (color + marker) are set from the single left-edge Label picker; session rows and server tiles keep a right-side color affordance and have no marker.

**Owned hue families** (`themes.ts` `HUE_FAMILIES`): 10 families in stable display order, each `{ name, hue, legacy, neutral? }`. Hue angles are placed non-uniformly — tight through the discriminable red→amber region, the large gap parked in teal→blue where human hue discrimination is weakest: red 25°, orange 55°, amber 90°, olive 120°, green 150°, teal 185°, blue 250°, purple 290°, magenta 330°, slate 250° (`neutral`). `FAMILY_BY_NAME` / `FAMILY_BY_LEGACY` are the two lookup maps.

**Two-shade axis (normal + dark), 20 picker values**: each of the 10 families spans a **normal** and a **dark** shade — a second color axis that doubles the label space for organizing many parallel windows. The shade is a **render-time lightness offset carried on `PickerColor` (`{ family, shade }`), not a parallel vocabulary of pseudo-families** — hue identity, legacy mapping, and theme adaptation stay properties of the 10 families, so dark variants derive for free with zero new palette data. `PICKER_COLOR_VALUES` is the 20 display values in **paired order** (`red, red-dark, orange, orange-dark, … slate, slate-dark`). `parseColorValue` / `formatColorValue` / `resolveFamily` / `colorValueToHex` accept the `-dark`-suffixed names; `formatColorValue` emits `"{family}-dark"` for dark shades.

**Theme adaptation (full adoption) + dark derivation**: `themeColorStats(palette) → { L, C }` computes the mean OKLab lightness and mean chroma over `palette.ansi[1..6]`, with a chroma floor `THEME_CHROMA_FLOOR = 0.05` (near-monochrome themes stay distinguishable). A family's **normal** source hex = `oklchToHexInGamut(L_theme, C_theme, family.hue)` (slate uses a near-neutral chroma `min(C_theme × 0.2, 0.025)`); its **dark** source hex renders at `L_dark = L_theme − 0.14` with the **same hue and same chroma** (slate keeps its near-neutral chroma rule — an intentional gray ramp), gamut-reduced via the same `oklchToHexInGamut`. Because the normal shade is the existing mean-L rendering, **every existing stored color maps to the normal shade untouched**. `colorValueToHex(value, palette)` resolves any stored value (family name, `-dark` suffix, OR legacy alias) to its adopted hex, returning `null` when it maps to no family.

**OKLCH → in-gamut hex, chroma-reduction not channel-clamp**: `oklchToHex(L, C, hueDeg)` converts polar chroma/hue to OKLab (`a = C·cos h`, `b = C·sin h`) then reuses `oklabToHex`. `oklchInGamut(L, C, hueDeg)` reports whether the triple maps inside sRGB without any channel being clamped, reusing the shared `oklabToLinearRgb(c)` matrix helper (extracted so its 18 coefficients stay in lockstep with `oklabToHex` — both consume it). `oklchToHexInGamut(L, C, hueDeg)` brings an out-of-gamut triple in by **reducing chroma stepwise** (`GAMUT_REDUCE_FACTOR = 0.92`, `GAMUT_REDUCE_MAX_STEPS = 20`) — L and hue are preserved; sRGB channel-clamping is never used because it shifts hue and would defeat the stable-hue goal.

**Storage vocabulary — three coexisting forms, zero migration**: a color value is stored as one of (a) a **legacy numeric/blend descriptor** (`"4"` / `"1+3"`) — the form pre-existing colored rows carry, valid forever on read and write; (b) a plain **family name** (`"blue"`); or (c) a **`-dark` family-name value** (`"blue-dark"`) — the only form for dark shades, which have no legacy equivalent. The normal-shade 1:1 legacy map: red←`1`, orange←`1+3`, amber←`3`, olive←`1+2`, green←`2`, teal←`6`, blue←`4`, purple←`1+4`, magenta←`5`, slate←`3+4`. `resolveFamily(value)` accepts a family name, a `-dark` suffix, OR a legacy descriptor (legacy normalized via the file-private `normalizeLegacy`, mirroring the backend `NormalizeColorValue` rule) and returns the same family for all shades/forms. **Write seam** — `familyToLegacy(name)` maps a **normal**-shade pick to its legacy descriptor exactly as before (zero migration — existing values untouched), while **dark** picks have no legacy form and pass through unchanged, written verbatim as `"{family}-dark"`; the helper is idempotent — an already-legacy, `-dark`, or unrecognized input passes through unchanged.

**Pre-blended row tints, multi-keyed for all 20 values**: `computeRowTints(palette) → Map<string, RowTint>` pre-computes the three blended state hexes for every shade of every family, keyed under **every stored vocabulary form** — a normal shade under BOTH its family name AND its legacy descriptor (both point at the *same* entry), a dark shade under its `"{family}-dark"` key (no legacy form exists) — plus the `"8"` uncolored-selected sentinel (10×2 normal keys + 10 dark keys + sentinel = 31 entries). All forms are keyed because consumers (`window-row`/`session-row`/`server-panel`) look up the RAW stored value and the backend emits legacy forms for pre-existing rows, so a family-name-only map would miss every legacy-stored row. Each shade's adopted source hex (`colorValueToHex`, dark included) is saturated ×1.5 (`TINT_SATURATE_FACTOR`) before blending into the theme background at three ratios:

| State | Constant | Saturated-source ratio |
|-------|----------|------------------------|
| Base | `TINT_BASE_RATIO` | 14% |
| Hover | `TINT_HOVER_RATIO` | 22% |
| Selected | `TINT_SELECTED_RATIO` | 40% |

Selection is carried by tint **depth** alone (no left border — § Row Anatomy), so the selected ratio is 40%. The uncolored-selected gray sentinel (`"8"`, `UNCOLORED_SELECTED_ANSI`/`UNCOLORED_SELECTED_KEY`) is NOT saturated (gray is near-zero chroma) and uses the deeper `UNCOLORED_SELECTED_RATIO = 0.5` so it beats the `bg-card/50` hover. Each state gets its own concrete hex — no stacked transparent layers. The `RowTint` type (`{ base, hover, selected }`) is exported. Hover is applied imperatively via `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` style mutations to avoid CSS-specificity issues with dynamic backgrounds.

**Contrast-guardrailed borders** (`computeRowBorders(palette, category) → Map<string, string>`): each shade's adopted source hex passed through a WCAG contrast guardrail, multi-keyed (family name + legacy descriptor for normal shades, `"{family}-dark"` for dark shades, + `"8"` sentinel — 31 entries) exactly like `computeRowTints`. `adjustBorderForContrast(border, bg, isDark, BORDER_MIN_CONTRAST)` nudges **only the OKLab lightness L** (lighten on dark themes, darken on light) in `0.03` steps until the border clears `BORDER_MIN_CONTRAST = 3.0` against the background or hits a 24-step cap; **hue and chroma are preserved** so the color keeps its identity. Window rows do not use these for a selection border; **server tiles** use them for their stripe/edge, **the left-edge marker stripe uses them as its guarded family color** (`markerColor`, § Left-Edge Label Zone), and **the Label picker's marker preview cells use them as the guarded stripe color of the live row preview** (§ Variant-C paired-grid Label picker). `sidebar/index.tsx` computes `rowTints` + `rowBorders` once per theme via `useMemo` and threads them to `WindowRow`/`ServerPanel`.

**OKLab + WCAG color-math helpers** (`themes.ts`): `hexToOklab` / `oklabToHex` (Björn Ottosson 2020 coefficients; `oklabToHex` and `oklchInGamut` share the `oklabToLinearRgb` matrix helper), the sRGB↔linear `srgbToLinear` gamma step, `relativeLuminance` (WCAG 2.x) and `contrastRatio(hex1, hex2)` (1..21), and `adjustBorderForContrast`. OKLab is the chosen lightness/distinctness space; WCAG is the separate legibility standard.

**SwatchPopover** (`app/frontend/src/components/swatch-popover.tsx`): one shared component, **one universal square style** — no rounded variant, no `square` prop. The color section renders the **20 paired values** from `PICKER_COLOR_VALUES` laid out 4-wide (5 color rows, `normal | dark` adjacent) plus the removal row: `Clear` (cols 1–3) beside the ✕ close cell (col 4). Props are STRINGS: `selectedColor?: string`, `onSelect(color | null)`, `onClose()`, plus the marker extension `selectedMarker?`, `onSelectMarker?(marker)`. **Color write seam** — every color emit funnels through `familyToLegacy` (the file-private `emit` callback: `onSelect(familyToLegacy(value))`): a **normal**-shade pick is stored as its legacy descriptor (`"orange"` → `"1+3"`, zero migration), a **dark** pick passes through verbatim as `"{family}-dark"` (no legacy form exists), and `null` (Clear) passes through untouched — the backend now accepts all three vocabularies (see architecture.md § validate closed sets), so no write 400s. **Uniform solid swatches**: each swatch is a single 18×18px solid fill = the value's `computeRowTints` `selected` tint (falling back to `colorValueToHex` then `theme.palette.foreground`), with a ✓ glyph + bright ring on the selected swatch so the selection is unambiguous between the two adjacent same-family shades. Selection is `-dark`-aware — matched by the picked value's `{family, shade}` (via `parseColorValue`), so a legacy-stored value highlights the right family's normal shade. The listbox **autofocuses on mount** (`containerRef.current?.focus()` in a mount effect, mirroring pin-popover) so arrow-key nav works immediately after the `Window: Label` palette action — the sole keyboard path to the marker section. **Dismissal contract**: selection NEVER dismisses (live combo toggling); the popover closes only via its ✕ cell, an outside click, or Escape — callers pass pure write handlers and close only from `onClose`.

**Universal square style**: every caller gets the same vocabulary — zero border radius on the container and all cells (no `rounded-md`/`rounded-sm`), a hard offset block shadow `3px 3px 0 rgba(0,0,0,.35)` (not `shadow-lg`), 3px gaps (`gap-[3px]`), 18px cells (`w-[18px] h-[18px]`), and 1px selection/focus outlines (`ring-1 ring-text-secondary`). There is a single style literal per axis — no dual class-string branches, no `SQUARE_GRID_COLS`/`DEFAULT_GRID_COLS` split. Session/server rows and the palette color actions render square like the window-row picker.

**Variant-C paired-grid Label picker**: when `onSelectMarker` is supplied (`showMarkers`), the popover renders two side-by-side sections divided by a **vertical hairline** (`w-px bg-border mx-1.5 self-stretch`) — markers LEFT (a 1-column `flex flex-col gap-[3px]` of 6 cells), colors RIGHT (the 20-swatch 4-wide paired grid). The layout pairs the two axes row-for-row:

```
∅   │  [   Clear   ]  ✕          ← row 0: removal row + close cell
∷   │  red  red-dk  or  or-dk    ← row 1: dotted  + colors 1–4
┆   │  am   am-dk   ol  ol-dk    ← row 2: dashed  + colors 5–8
▐   │  gr   gr-dk   te  te-dk    ← row 3: solid   + colors 9–12
▐▓  │  bl   bl-dk   pu  pu-dk    ← row 4: double  + colors 13–16
▐█  │  ma   ma-dk   sl  sl-dk    ← row 5: thick   + colors 17–20
```

- **Row 0 is the removal row**: the `∅` no-marker cell (marker column) beside a `Clear` button spanning color columns 1–3 (`col-span-3`) and the **✕ close cell** in column 4 (`aria-label="Close picker"`, an 18px `role=option` cell that is never `aria-selected` — an option-as-command matching Clear's pattern, keeping the `role=listbox` container's children ARIA-valid per Copilot's #452 review).
- **Dismissal contract — selection never closes**: picking a color, a marker, or Clear leaves the popover OPEN so color + marker combos can be toggled and compared live against the row; it dismisses only via the ✕ cell, an outside click, or Escape. Closing on select is the component's contract, not the callers' — every call site (window-row Label picker, session/server rows, HOST panel, settings dialog, the palette Set Color overlay) passes a pure write handler and closes only from `onClose`.
- **Rows 1–5** pair the dotted / dashed / solid / double / thick marker cells with the 5 color rows (each `normal | dark` adjacent — row 1: red, red-dark, orange, orange-dark; …).
- **Marker cells are live row previews of the selected color** — each non-∅ cell renders as a miniature window row for the currently selected color: background = that value's `tint.base` (gray sentinel when uncolored), the stripe via the shared `markerStripeStyle` helper in the **guarded border color** with a **2px left inset** (so the marker doesn't kiss the cell edge and the cell reads as a mini row), plus the paired row texture — the static `.rk-hazard` wedge on the thick cell and the static `.rk-scanlines` wash on the double cell (with per-cell `--rk-marker-color`), reusing the row classes so the texture vocabulary lives in one place. Picking a different swatch **repaints the marker column immediately**. The empty ∅ cell shows a glyph; the current state carries `aria-selected` + a selection ring. Selecting a cell calls `onSelectMarker` **directly with the exact state** (no cycling — any state is one click; `""` clears).
- **Preview cells NEVER animate** — an explicit user decision that motion belongs to real rows only: no scanline crawl even when the double cell is selected, the hazard wedge is static as always, and the dashed preview never carries the data-rain overlay. No cell ever carries `rk-scanlines-crawl` or `rk-dash-rain`.
- Marker cell order mirrors `MARKER_STATES` (`["", "dotted", "dashed", "solid", "double", "thick"]`) via `MARKER_CELLS` — `∅` in row 0, the five non-empty states beside the five color rows.
- **One-period stripe tiles make the previews correct**: the marker cells are 18px tall, so the fixed one-period `markerStripeStyle` tiles (§ Left-Edge Label Zone) are what let the dotted/dashed rhythm render correctly inside them — the previously element-height-dependent tile broke at that size.
- **Documented 1:1 invariant**: the marker-cell ↔ grid-row pairing is a deliberate `GRID_ROWS === MARKER_CELLS.length` invariant (6 marker cells ↔ 6 grid rows: 5 color rows of 20 swatches 4-wide + the Clear row), documented as such in a code comment. The 20-swatch grid and the 6-cell marker set are sized to pair exactly — the two extensions fix each other's layout, leaving no dead cells.

**Color-only callers get the same paired grid, marker-less**: with the marker props absent (`onSelectMarker` undefined) the left marker column and vertical hairline are simply not rendered — the color section stands alone with its `Clear` + ✕ removal row over the 20 paired swatches (5 rows of 4). Same square style; no separate layout branch.

**Keyboard navigation — conceptual 5-column × 6-row grid, no dead cells**: focus is a `{row, col}` `GridPos` pair (not a linear item index). `col` 0 = the marker column (valid only when `showMarkers`), cols 1–4 = the color columns; `row` 0 = the removal row, rows 1–5 = the five color rows. The 20 colors fill the 5×4 color region exactly, so there are **no dead cells** to clamp around anymore. ArrowLeft/ArrowRight cross the hairline (col 1 ↔ col 0) and step within the color columns; ArrowUp/ArrowDown move within a column. The `Clear color` button is a **single spanning focus target** occupying cols 1–4 of row 0 (canonicalized to col 1; `focusOnClear = row === 0 && col >= 1`). Off-grid moves clamp to the nearest valid cell; focus stays valid, no crash. Enter/Space activates the focused cell (`activate`): col 0 → `onSelectMarker(MARKER_CELLS[row])`, row 0 → `emit(null)` (Clear), else → `emit(PICKER_COLOR_VALUES[colorIndexAt])`. **Initial focus FOLLOWS SELECTION**: the selected color swatch, or the `Clear color` cell (row 0 — already `aria-selected` in that state) when uncolored, never an arbitrary swatch; the marker column is reached via ArrowLeft. **Focus-visible semantics**: the focus ring (`ring-text-secondary`, visually quieter than the `ring-text-primary` selection ring) renders only after the first arrow key (`keyboardActive` flag) — the listbox autofocuses on mount, so an always-on ring showed mouse users a phantom highlight. The selected item is marked with the brighter `ring-text-primary` (colors additionally show a ✓; the uncolored state rings `Clear color`). Color-only mode is the same grid minus column 0.

**Window-row left-edge trigger**: the combined Label picker opens from the window row's left-edge label zone (§ Left-Edge Label Zone) or the `Window: Label` palette action — it is not a right-edge hover indicator. Session rows and server tiles still surface their color picker via a right-edge hover palette icon (visible on hover on desktop, always on touch via `coarse:opacity-100`).

**Command palette actions**: "Session: Set Color" opens the color-only SwatchPopover. The window **label** action is `window-label` / `Window: Label`, which dispatches an imperative `label-popover:open` CustomEvent (`{ detail: { server, windowId } }`) for the current window — the matching `WindowRow` listens and opens its combined picker (mirroring the `pin-popover:open` pattern used by "Board: Pin Current Window") to set both color and marker in one picker. The color-only `window-set-color` / `Window: Set Color` action (which opens the color-only SwatchPopover via `setShowColorPicker("window")`) is a separate registered action. Every color write path flows through the `familyToLegacy` write seam (normal picks → legacy descriptor, dark picks → `"{family}-dark"` verbatim); "Clear color" sends `null`, and clearing the marker sends `null` through `setWindowMarker`.

**Storage**: a stored color value is a **legacy descriptor** (`"4"` / `"1+3"`, the form pre-existing rows keep), a plain **family name** (`"blue"`), or a **`-dark` family-name value** (`"blue-dark"`, the only dark-shade form) — all three valid on read and write. Frontend wire types are `WindowInfo.color?: string` and `ProjectSession.sessionColor?: string` (`src/types.ts`). Window and session colors are ephemeral tmux user options (`@color` per window, `@session_color` per session — survive session lifetime, not tmux-server restarts), set via `setWindowColor`/`setSessionColor` in `client.ts`. Server colors persist in `~/.rk/settings.yaml`. **No color storage/API migration** — the shade axis widens the accepted vocabulary additively (legacy values untouched); the backend closed set was extended to accept the family-name forms. See architecture.md § Data Model and § validate closed sets for the backend storage/validation contract.

### Instance Accent ("host color")

A per-instance accent color makes multiple run-kit instances (laptop, Mac mini, GCP server) viewed as visually-identical installed Chrome PWA windows tell each other apart at a glance. It is an **axis orthogonal to the owned-palette color tinting above**: server/window/session colors saturate the sidebar and mean "which tmux server"; the instance accent means "which run-kit instance" and owns surfaces the sidebar colors never touch (the top bar and the HOST panel). The accent is a property of the **instance**, not the viewer — stored authoritatively on the instance's host (`~/.rk/settings.yaml` `instance_color`, see architecture.md § `internal/settings` and § `/api/settings/instance-color`), so every device viewing that instance sees the same accent. (`260721-1etw-instance-accent-host-color`)

**Resolution chain** (frontend, in the `InstanceAccentProvider`): (1) the explicit `instance_color` setting from `getInstanceColor()` — authoritative; (2) the localStorage echo (`runkit-instance-color`) — a **paint cache only, never authoritative**, used as the first-frame seed before the fetch lands; (3) **none** — there is **no derived default**: a fresh instance renders no accent until the user picks one (an earlier hostname-hash default was removed — its color could coincide with a just-cleared explicit pick, making "Clear color" look like a no-op). An `authoritative` flag (the explicit fetch resolved) gates clearing the echo/meta on a null accent, so the paint seed is never wiped before the real resolution replaces it.

**Provider + consumers** — `InstanceAccentProvider` / `useInstanceAccent()` (`contexts/instance-accent-context.tsx`) mounts once in `RootWrapper` inside `ThemeProvider` > `ToastProvider` and above `ChromeProvider`, so it can read `useTheme()` for the theme-derived hexes and `useToast()` for the write-failure toast. It fetches `getInstanceColor()` + `getHealth()` once on mount (StrictMode-guarded via a `didFetchRef`), seeds the first paint from the echo, resolves per the chain, derives `{stripeHex, washHex, titlebarHex}` from the active theme, keeps the echo + theme-color meta in sync on every accent/theme change, and exposes all three on the context value (`{color, isExplicit, stripeHex, washHex, titlebarHex, setColor}`). `titlebarHex` is nullable like its siblings and has two consumers: the theme-color meta bridge effect, and the desktop shell's titlebar strip, which renders from it directly rather than re-deriving the same blend (§ Desktop-Shell Titlebar Strip). `setColor(descriptor | null)` updates state optimistically then POSTs via `setInstanceColor`, toasting on failure. Every rendering surface reads from this ONE instance — one fetch, one state, so a pick in the HOST panel repaints the top-bar stripe without a reload. A test seam `InstanceAccentValueProvider` injects a fixed value (mirroring the session-context value-injection pattern).

**Theme-derived hexes** (`deriveAccentHexes(value, theme)` in `src/instance-accent.ts`, never hardcoded) — a three-hex split, each a distinct accent surface: `stripeHex` is the **contrast-guarded** family hex — `colorValueToHex(value, palette)` then `adjustBorderForContrast(src, bg, isDark, BORDER_MIN_CONTRAST)` (the same guarded-hex derivation server tiles/stripes use, § Color Tinting) — used for the 2px top-bar stripe and the HOST hostname tint. `washHex` is a subtle `blendHex(src, bg, INSTANCE_WASH_RATIO = 0.065)` (~6.5%) accent-into-background blend for the top-bar background wash. `titlebarHex` is the strongest of the three — `blendHex(src, bg, INSTANCE_TITLEBAR_RATIO = 0.35)` (~35%) accent-into-background — and paints **every titlebar-class surface**: the theme-color meta content (the installed-PWA titlebar tint) and the desktop shell's page-drawn titlebar strip, in both cases a tinted band with the full-brightness `stripeHex` 2px stripe visible directly below it. `INSTANCE_TITLEBAR_RATIO` sits beside `INSTANCE_WASH_RATIO` (a taste constant). Blend descriptors (`"1+3"`) render through the same `resolveFamily`-based owned-family mapping as single indices — no new blend scheme. All three recompute on a theme switch (light themes get a light-background blend by construction); nothing hardcoded survives. An unrecognized descriptor yields `null` (no partial object).

**Top-bar stripe + wash** (`AppLayout`, `app.tsx`) — the persistent top bar's `shrink-0` wrapper (above `RootTopBar`) carries a 2px accent stripe (an `aria-hidden` `<div style={{height:"2px", backgroundColor: stripeHex}}>`) plus the `washHex` as its `backgroundColor` (the `TopBar` header has no background of its own, so the wash on the wrapper shows through). Both render only when their hex is non-null (no accent resolved ⇒ neither renders). This is the "which instance" channel living where the sidebar colors do not.

**HOST panel — accent hostname tint + swatch picker** (`components/sidebar/host-panel.tsx`) — the hostname in the panel's `headerRight` slot renders in `stripeHex` (the contrast-guarded accent) when resolved, else the default `text-text-primary`. A palette swatch button rides the `CollapsiblePanel` **`titleAction` slot** (immediately right of the HOST title — a sibling of the toggle button, since a nested button would be invalid markup; with `titleAction` set the header splits into `[toggle: chevron+title][titleAction][toggle click-region: headerRight]`, the trailing region keeping mouse click-to-toggle while keyboard toggling stays on the title button), hover-revealed with the touch/keyboard fallbacks `opacity-0 group-hover/panel:opacity-100 coarse:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100` (`CollapsiblePanel`'s header gained a `group/panel` class to drive it) and an `aria-label="Set instance color"`. Clicking it opens a **color-only** `SwatchPopover` (no `onSelectMarker`) portalled to `document.body` at fixed coordinates anchored at the button — left-aligned (the button sits beside the title at the sidebar's left edge), with a **flip-above** `useLayoutEffect` heuristic (the HOST panel sits at the sidebar bottom, so flip-above is the norm) — escaping the panel's overflow clip. This is the x4sf ServerGroup-header picker precedent (§ Server-group header action cluster). A pick calls `useInstanceAccent().setColor` (which POSTs and repaints every surface); `Clear color` sends `null`, removing the accent entirely (the no-color default). The HOST header carries **no connection dot**: the top-bar dot already reflects the same current-server subscription health (`setChromeConnected(slice.isConnected)` in session-context), and the removed dot's "SSE" title predated the `/ws/state` socket.

**PWA titlebar bridge — single theme-color meta writer** (`src/instance-accent.ts`) — the `<meta name="theme-color">` tag is accent-aware through ONE shared writer holding module-level state (`currentAccentHex` + `lastBackground`, content = accent hex when set, else the theme background): (a) the blocking pre-paint script in `index.html` (§ PWA Meta Tags & Theme Color) reads the echoed `hex` from `runkit-instance-color`, validates the `#rrggbb` shape, and applies it **verbatim** as the initial theme-color (falling back to the per-mode defaults) so an installed PWA window opens already tinted with no flash; (b) at runtime the provider calls `setAccentThemeColor(titlebarHex)` (the ~35% titlebar blend, NOT `stripeHex`) on accent/theme change, and writes the same `titlebarHex` into the echo's `hex` field — so cold start and runtime both paint the subtle tinted titlebar, and the full-brightness `stripeHex` 2px stripe stays visible directly below it; (c) `theme-context.tsx`'s `applyThemeToDOM` calls `applyThemeColorMeta(theme.palette.background)` instead of writing the meta directly, so a theme switch cannot clobber the accent tint. Desktop Chrome retints installed-PWA titlebars live on meta changes, and the **Electron shell listens to the same meta** — `did-change-theme-color` is what repaints its Windows/Linux window-controls overlay, so the one writer serves both hosts with no shell-specific color API ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Hidden Titlebar & Accent Strip). `index.html` is unchanged — the pre-paint script applies the echoed `hex` verbatim, so swapping the echoed value retints cold start for free (an echo written by a pre-titlebar-blend build carries the full-hue hex and self-corrects when the runtime resolution rewrites both meta and echo — the same accepted self-correcting transient class as cross-mode loads).

**PWA install identity — Dock/Cmd-Tab icon + tab favicon** — the instance accent also tints the installed PWA's OS-level identity, so multiple instances are distinguishable in the Dock and Cmd-Tab, not only inside the window (`260722-eo8e-accent-dock-icon`). This is a **backend** concern: the Go server serves `/manifest.json`, the three `/generated-icons/icon-*.png`, and `/generated-icons/favicon.svg` dynamically, tinting the grayscale logo per the instance's `instance_color` (see architecture.md § PWA Identity Assets). No frontend component changes — `index.html`'s static `<link rel="manifest">` / `rel=icon` / `apple-touch-icon` hrefs are unchanged; the tint enters through the responses. Two behaviors matter to the UI surface:
- **Cache-bust contract**: the dynamic manifest appends `?c=<descriptor>` to each icon `src` (`+` encoded `%2B`) when an accent is owned; the icon PNG routes tint only on that owned `?c=`, serving stock bytes otherwise. The favicon route carries no `?c=` (its href is static) and resolves the tint from settings per request with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, so open browser tabs re-tint on their next load.
- **Chrome icon-refresh limitation** (accepted, documented — not engineered around): Chrome snapshots PWA icons at install and re-checks the manifest only on app launch with ~daily throttling, so an accent picked **before** install is immediate while a change **after** install updates the Dock icon eventually (reinstall forces it). The top-bar stripe/wash and PWA titlebar theme-color stay live regardless.

### Desktop-Shell Titlebar Strip (`components/shell-titlebar-strip.tsx` + `lib/shell-strip.ts`)

The instance accent's **third consumer surface**, and the only one that exists outside a browser: inside the Electron viewer shell the native titlebar is hidden, so the SPA draws the window's titlebar itself as a 28px full-width band above the top bar. The accent chain is therefore PWA titlebar meta → top-bar wash/stripe → **shell titlebar strip**, all three fed by the same `useInstanceAccent()` instance.

**Background is `titlebarHex`, read not re-derived.** The strip paints `titlebarHex ?? theme.palette.background` — the identical ~35% accent-into-background blend the installed-PWA titlebar carries, which is the point: the same window chrome should be the same color whether run-kit is installed as a PWA or as the desktop app. It consumes the context's exposed field rather than calling `deriveAccentHexes` again, so one derivation cannot drift into two (§ Design Decisions → `titlebarHex` is exposed, not re-derived). With no accent resolved the band falls back to the plain theme background and is still a drag surface.

**Drag region via a CSS utility class, with exactly one no-drag island.** The band carries `.rk-shell-drag` (`globals.css`: `-webkit-app-region: drag`); the host-switcher trigger and its open menu carry the sibling `.rk-shell-no-drag` (`-webkit-app-region: no-drag`) and are the band's **only** interactive island — everything else in the band drags the window. Both are classes because `WebkitAppRegion` is absent from React's `CSSProperties` and an inline style would need an `as` cast the code-quality rules forbid; `rk-*` is also where the utility vocabulary already lives. The classes are inert in browsers, so they cost nothing outside the shell (§ Design Decisions → Drag regions are a CSS utility class; → The strip's switcher trigger is the band's only no-drag island).

**The label names the window's host, and is the switcher trigger.** A centered, truncated `location.hostname`-fallback label showing the shell-registered **active** host's display name, fetched on mount via `listShellServers()` and reduced by the pure `activeShellHostName(servers)`. Text color is `stripLabelColor(bg)` — a `contrastRatio` pick between the two standard text hexes, the same WCAG helper the border guard uses. Horizontal insets keep it clear of the OS controls compositing over the band: a fixed symmetric 80px on darwin (traffic lights sit left; symmetry keeps the label visually centered) and `titlebar-area-*` env expressions elsewhere, which degrade to no inset where window-controls-overlay is absent. `stripLabelColor`, `stripInsets`, `activeShellHostName`, and the shared `SHELL_STRIP_HEIGHT_PX` / `SHELL_STRIP_MARKER_CLASS` constants live in `lib/shell-strip.ts` with a colocated vitest suite.

**Host-switcher dropdown — the mouse-secondary switch path.** When the bridge answers a **non-empty** host list (`stripSwitcherEnabled(servers)`, a type guard) the label renders as a trigger button — `<active-host-name> ▾`, `aria-haspopup="menu"` / `aria-expanded`, `aria-label="Switch host"`, with a subtle currentColor-tinted hover pill (`hover:bg-current/15`, which reads on any accent-blended strip background since the label color is already contrast-derived against it) — reusing the top bar's `▾` switcher vocabulary (§ Crumb Affordance Vocabulary). It is the mouse-secondary companion to the ⌥⌘1–9 / ⇧Ctrl+1–9 accelerators and the palette's `Server: Switch to` block, which stay the primary paths (Constitution V). Below the gate — an older shell exposing only `version`/`platform`, a denial, or an empty store — the label is a plain static span with no chevron, no pill, and no click behavior; the band still mounts, because its gate is `isShell()` alone (§ Design Decisions → Shell chrome mounts on `isShell()`).

**Row anatomy** (`shellHostMenuRows(servers, platform)` in `lib/shell-strip.ts`, list order preserved because that IS the native menu's binding order): a fixed-width ✓ marker column so names align, the active row in `text-accent`; the display name; the **dimmed origin** (`new URL(url).origin`, falling back to the raw string on a parse failure) — host display names are not unique because the shell's store never dedupes, so the origin disambiguates; and a trailing accelerator hint `⌥⌘{n}` on darwin / `⇧Ctrl+{n}` elsewhere, `null` past `MAX_SHELL_SWITCHER_HINTS = 9` (mirroring the native menu's `MAX_SWITCHER_ACCELERATORS` — a host with no binding gets no hint). Platform comes from the bridge's `shellInfo()?.platform`. Rows need no bridge change: `servers:list` already projects `{id, name, url, active}`.

**Refetch on open, guarded by a monotonic sequence.** Every open issues a fresh `listShellServers()` — the native `Hosts → Remove "<name>"…` menu mutates the store without a page reload, so a mount-time-only list goes stale. One `listSeqRef` counter guards *both* fetches: a resolution whose sequence is not the latest issued is dropped, so out-of-order resolutions can never leave a stale list rendered, and the mount effect's cleanup bump doubles as cancellation (the open-time fetch has no effect cleanup to cancel it). A `null` resolution — the never-throw wrapper's denial signal — keeps the last known list rather than blanking an open menu.

**Menu semantics copy `BreadcrumbDropdown`'s contract** (§ Breadcrumb Dropdowns): `role="menu"` with `role="menuitem"` rows, capture-phase keydown for Escape (close + refocus trigger) and ArrowDown/ArrowUp (wrapping roving tabindex; Enter needs no handler because each row is a native `<button>`), outside-`mousedown` close, and focus landing on the active row on open. The menu is a plain `absolute left-1/2 top-full` anchor — the strip has no `overflow: hidden` ancestor clipping below it, so no fixed-position machinery is needed — and it paints in theme surface colors (`bg-bg-primary`/`border-border`) rather than the strip's accent blend, because it overlays SPA content below the band. Three guards make an open-time refetch that changes the row count safe: the seed effect keys on the **open transition only** (re-running on a refetched set would yank focus mid-arrowing); a separate clamp effect keyed on `[open, rows.length]` re-seats the roving tabindex when a refetch *shrinks* the set below the current seat (an in-bounds seat is left alone, so an unchanged or growing list never moves focus); and the arrow branch returns before `preventDefault` when the row count is 0, so the capture-phase handler cannot swallow arrows app-wide during the one render where an emptied list has unmounted the rows but `open` is still true. A close-on-empty effect then releases `open` so the menu's effects tear down with it.

**Selection hands off to the shell, no optimistic UI.** Picking a row closes the menu and calls `switchShellServer(id)`; the shell's single `switchToHost` seam swaps the displayed host, so there is nothing to update optimistically and no SPA-side path handling exists. The swap is a detach/attach flip between per-host renderer views ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Host Views), so this component instance simply stops being displayed — it keeps running, with its state intact, and is shown again unchanged when the user switches back. A `false` resolution surfaces an error toast (the palette's precedent), and selecting the already-active row is a harmless no-op — the same behavior the palette action and the native menu radios have, so all three switch paths behave identically.

**Marker class + mount gating.** While mounted the component sets `rk-shell-strip` on `<html>` (a `useLayoutEffect`, removed on unmount) — the shell's version-skew fallback CSS is keyed on `html:not(.rk-shell-strip)` and self-disables against it ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Hidden Titlebar & Accent Strip). `AppLayout` mounts the strip gated on `isShell()`, as a **sibling above** the top-bar wrapper — the top bar itself is untouched, which is the explicit contrast with a titlebar merge. `isShell()` is false in every browser and in Playwright, so **no e2e sees the strip or its dropdown**: the whole surface is covered by two colocated vitest suites — `lib/shell-strip.test.ts` for the pure helpers and `shell-titlebar-strip.test.tsx` for the component (rendered inside `ToastProvider`, asserting the gate branch, the open-time refetch call count, row anatomy, shrunk/emptied-list focus behavior, out-of-order resolutions, selection, and Escape/Arrow keys) — plus manual verification in the real shell. The band persists in macOS fullscreen (traffic lights auto-hide over it; no fullscreen bridge flag exists).

### Desktop-Shell Waiting Badge Reporter (`components/shell-badge-reporter.tsx`)

A null-rendering subscriber, mounted in `AppLayout` beside the strip and gated on `isShell()`, that mirrors the waiting-agent count to the OS dock/taskbar badge — the attention tier escaping the page (§ Attention Surfacing). It is the fifth `lib/waiting.ts` consumer and the only one whose output leaves the browser.

The count is `countWaitingAcrossServers(sessionsByServer)` — a new helper composing the existing `countWaitingInSessions` over every entry in the streamed map, so the badge counts identically to the sidebar and tile badges. It derives from SSE state already in the client: no polling, no new endpoint (Constitution II). **Scope caveat**, inherited from `sessionsByServer` itself: only **attached** servers stream windows, so an unattached server contributes 0 — the same accepted constraint the Host-page and sidebar server tiles carry.

Reporting is change-only: the derived count is the effect dependency and a ref guards re-mount duplicates, so a 2 → 2 SSE tick makes no bridge call while 2 → 0 reports `0` explicitly (clears must propagate). The call goes through `setShellBadge(count)`, which never throws — a plain browser or an older shell without the `badge` bridge group simply resolves `false`.

### Row Anatomy — Selection & Board-Pin Cues (`window-row.tsx`)

The window row's visual channels are orthogonal: **hue = label** (family tint), **tint depth = selection**, **left-edge zone = the independent marker axis + the Label-picker trigger** (§ Left-Edge Label Zone). Selection and the board-pin cue are borderless — the sidebar window row carries no left accent border.

**Full-bleed row box**: the row `<button>` (`window-row.tsx:238`) spans the sidebar **edge-to-edge** — every box-anchored visual layer (family tint via `buttonStyle`, hover fill, 40% selection tint, the `isDragOver` box-shadow, and the `inset-0` scanline overlay) reaches the physical sidebar left edge with no gutter. The 12px hierarchy indent lives inside the button's left padding (`pl-[30px]` = 12px indent + 18px content padding), not as a group margin on the window-list wrapper — so the status dot and window name sit at a fixed 30px from the sidebar edge (dot leading edge) while the box itself extends to the seam. The wider scanline band on selected+double rows follows from the box reaching the edge and is intended.

**Selection = tint depth + typography, borderless**: a selected colored row deepens to the 40% family tint (`rowTints.get(color).selected`); an uncolored selected row uses the gray sentinel's deeper 0.5 tint. The name goes `font-medium` + `text-text-primary`. There is **no** left accent border on selection.

**Board-pin active-board cue on the pin glyph** (not a border): the persistent filled pin glyph — already `opacity-100` + filled whenever the window is pinned to any board (`isPinnedToAny`) — renders **accent-colored** when the row is pinned to the currently-viewed board (`isPinnedToActiveBoard`), and monochrome `text-text-secondary` otherwise. `isPinnedToActiveBoard` implies `isPinnedToAny`, so the accent branch is always the persistent (never hover-gated) glyph. This is load-bearing because link-based pinning keeps pinned rows visible in SESSIONS: the cue distinguishes "pinned to the board you're viewing" from "pinned to some board" with zero new elements, leaving the left-edge zone for the marker + Label picker. See § Active-Board Highlight in Sessions Tree, § Sidebar Pin Icon on Window Rows. (`260718-co9z`)

### Left-Edge Label Zone (`window-row.tsx` `LabelZone`)

The whole 26px to the left of the status dot on each window row is ONE interactive target that opens the combined **Label picker** (colors + marker). It consolidates both of the row's label axes on the left edge — hue and the independent 5-state marker — leaving the right hover cluster for actions (pin + kill). The marker is an abstract label axis, orthogonal to hue and selection; its states are deliberately unnamed in the UI (todo/doing/done for one user, priority for another), though `dashed` = "working" and `thick` = "completed" are the suggested conventions — **label conventions only, with no functional wiring** to `@rk_agent_state` or the status pyramid.

**One 26px target, opens the picker — never cycles, never selects**: clicking anywhere in the zone opens the combined Label picker anchored at the row's bottom-left (an `absolute left-0 top-full z-50` wrapper; the row root stays overflow-free so the `top-full` popover is not clipped even on a selected+double row). The click `stopPropagation`s so it does not select the row, and coexists with drag-reorder. Cursor is `pointer` (menu-opener semantics). The zone is enabled on non-ghost rows wired with both the color and marker write seams plus a `server` (`labelZoneEnabled`).

**Zone geometry**: an absolute `z-20` sibling positioned `left-0` spanning `LABEL_ZONE_WIDTH = 26`px (full row height). Because the row box is full-bleed (§ Row Anatomy), `left-0` IS the physical sidebar edge — the zone needs no negative offset, so the coordinate expression is a plain `left-0` overlay. The zone's absolute span (sidebar x = 0…26) is 26px of the true sidebar edge. The palette-icon home is a 12px band (`ICON_ZONE_WIDTH = 12`) inset `ICON_EDGE_INSET = 12`px off the physical edge (sidebar x = 12…24) — past the widest (double, 4+6=10px) marker stripe, so the hover icon sits beside the stripe rather than over it. The button content is padded clear with `pl-[30px]` (12px hierarchy indent + 18px content padding) so the leading StatusDot + name start 4px past the zone's inner edge — the dot keeps its click-to-select (and its coarse-pointer flyout tap), and the dot + name keep their exact x-positions (no content shift). Active on coarse pointers — touch gets direct label access (no `coarse:pointer-events-none`).

**Hover affordance — palette icon + two-stage zone glow**: at rest the zone is transparent with no icon. On ROW hover (`group-hover`) the shipped `PaletteIcon` (~11px, in the 12px icon zone at `left: ICON_EDGE_INSET = 12`px, beside the edge-anchored stripe rather than over it) fades to ~65% and the whole zone glows ~12% family color; when the ZONE ITSELF is hovered the icon reaches full opacity and the glow deepens to ~24% (`color-mix`). The icon is drawn in the row's guarded family color (`markerColor`) on colored rows and inherited monochrome on uncolored rows. The icon container keeps an explicit `z-10` (the stripe is a DOM sibling rendered before it) as a guard so any residual overlap layers icon-over-stripe deterministically inside the zone's own `z-20` stacking context, not by incidental sibling order. `aria-label="Set window label"` names the zone for pointer AT users and test selection.

**Marker vocabulary — 5 states, one display order**: `themes.ts` owns `MARKER_STATES = ["", "dotted", "dashed", "solid", "double", "thick"]` (empty first, then the display order dotted → dashed → solid → double → thick) and the `markerStripeStyle(state, color)` helper, shared by this stripe and the Label picker's marker cells so the stripe vocabulary lives in exactly one place. Rendering per state, all in the guarded family color (gray uncolored): `dotted` = 3px-wide column of 3px dashes / 3px gaps; `dashed` = 3px-wide column of 8px dashes / 4px gaps (12px period); `solid` = `border-left: 3px solid`; `double` = `border-left: 6px double`; `thick` = `border-left: 6px solid` (a continuous bar). Markers are available on all rows including uncolored ones.

**Marker semantics (user-owned, deliberately distinct from the status dot)**: markers and row colors are USER PREFERENCES — what the user thinks about a task — whereas status-dot states are DERIVED (activity/PR/agent signals, § Status Dot); the two axes never mix, by design. Three markers carry agreed meanings: `dashed` = **working** (an agent is actively working here — the data-rain pairing), `thick` = **completed** (done, taped off — the hazard-wedge pairing), `double` = **different** (this window IS different — what "different" means is the user's call; the scanline wash makes it the showcase mark). The remaining states — none, `dotted`, `solid` — are entirely free for per-user conventions (e.g. dotted = "still deciding whether to start an agent", solid = "made up my mind what to do"). None of this is wired to `@rk_agent_state` or any derived signal; the semantics are hand-set conventions only.

**Dashed data rain (always-on row overlay)**: a `dashed`-marked window row carries `.rk-dash-rain` — two sparse 1px dash tracks streaming LEFT→RIGHT across the row's full width in the guarded marker color: the ambient "agent is working" cue. Mounted on `isDashed` alone (`window-row.tsx`), in every state — "working" is inherently live, and the thinned two-lane rain proved quiet enough to run ambiently (a deliberate user call after watching the selection-gated version); the gutter stripe itself stays STATIC in every state. Same overlay discipline as scanlines/hazard: a dedicated clipped inner element (`absolute inset-0 z-[5] overflow-hidden pointer-events-none`), never the row root, reading `--rk-marker-color` from the root (set for dashed rows too). Each track has its own rhythm (7px dash/36px period at y 8px; 5px dash/28px period at y 16px) so the lanes drift at different speeds and read as rain, not a marquee. **Seamless loop rule**: each layer's tile is ONE FIXED PERIOD wide (`background-size: 36px 1px, 28px 1px` — never `100%`, which makes the tile element-width-dependent and snaps at the loop boundary), and the keyframes displace each layer by an integer multiple of its own period (72/56px over 2.8s), so the cycle point is invisible. `background-position` only, never transform (the drag-ghost rule). Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the rain hides entirely; the static label cue is the dashed stripe itself. The picker's marker preview cells never carry it.

**Marker stripe is display-only**: the marker renders as a left-edge stripe via `markerStripeStyle`, anchored near-flush at the sidebar's physical left edge — `left: STRIPE_EDGE_INSET = 4`px (`window-row.tsx`), `right: 0`. It is purely a display of current state — clicking it opens the picker; it does not cycle.

**One-period fixed stripe tiles**: the gradient cases (`dotted`, `dashed`) use a plain `linear-gradient` of exactly one period with `backgroundRepeat: "repeat-y"` — dotted `backgroundSize: "3px 6px"` (`${color} 0 3px, transparent 3px 6px`), dashed `backgroundSize: "3px 12px"` (`${color} 0 8px, transparent 8px 12px`) — NOT `border-left: dotted` (a dotted border distributes dots per-element and restarts at every row, so stacked dotted rows would show seams while solid/double merge) and NOT an element-height-dependent `repeating-linear-gradient` + `3px 100%` + `no-repeat` tile. Because each tile is exactly one period, the rhythm is correct at ANY element height (e.g. the 18px picker preview cells), not only at heights that happen to be period multiples; row rendering is pixel-identical because the 6px/12px periods divide the 24px (36px coarse) row heights exactly, so the rhythm carries across adjacent stacked rows.

**Marker/color plumbing**: `WindowInfo.marker?: string` (`src/types.ts`) carries the state from the `@rk_marker` window option (architecture.md § `@rk_marker`). `setWindowMarker(server, windowId, marker | null)` in `client.ts` delegates to `setWindowOptions({"@rk_marker": ...})` (empty/`null` clears). `sidebar/index.tsx` owns `onColorChange` + `onMarkerChange` handlers, prop-drilled through `ServerGroup` to `WindowRow`; the Label picker passes the **exact** picked color and marker state straight to these (no cycling — any state is one click). The `Window: Label` palette action opens this same picker via an imperative `label-popover:open` CustomEvent that the matching `WindowRow` listens for (`{ detail: { server, windowId } }`, mirroring the `pin-popover:open` pattern); the row registers both listeners in one `useEffect`. **Scope: window rows only** — session rows and server tiles get the owned-palette color affordance but no marker and no left-edge label zone.

### Easter Egg — Double-Marker Scanlines (`globals.css` `.rk-scanlines`)

A window row whose marker is `double` gets a static CRT scanline overlay across its full width, making `double` the one marker state readable from across the sidebar. Selecting such a row brings the scanlines alive.

**Static scanlines**: `.rk-scanlines::before` paints a `repeating-linear-gradient(to bottom, {marker color}@24% 0 1px, transparent 1px 3px)` reading the family color from the `--rk-marker-color` custom property (set on the row when `double`). The overlay is a **dedicated absolutely-positioned inner element** (`absolute inset-0 z-[5] overflow-hidden pointer-events-none`), NOT the row root — `overflow-hidden` on the root would clip the row's `top-full` pin/color popovers, so clipping lives on the overlay and the root stays free to overflow. The overlay sits above the tint/button background (`z-5`), below the `z-10` icon cluster and `z-20` gutter. Pure static CSS — it does not touch the status pyramid's animation channel (the waiting halo stays unambiguous).

**Selected + double retro animation**: when a double-marker row is ALSO selected, the class `rk-scanlines-crawl` is added, animating a slow downward crawl of the gradient period (`@keyframes rk-scanline-crawl`, `background-position-y` 0 → 3px, 2.4s linear infinite) plus an occasional CRT refresh band — a soft ~12px gradient band in the marker color rolling top→bottom via `::after` (`@keyframes rk-scanline-band`, 7s cycle with a rest phase then a ~2.8s sweep, `pointer-events: none`). Static again when deselected. **Both animations move `background-position-y`, never `transform`** — an animated translateY range grows the compositor layer beyond the row box (ancestor clips ignored), and the HTML5 drag-ghost snapshot of a selected+double row then carries a sliver of the neighboring row along with it; a background sweep cannot paint outside the element's own box. The band is a `no-repeat` `background-size: 100% 12px` gradient on an `inset-0` `::after`, parked at `background-position-y: -12px` (outside the box paints nothing) and swept to `calc(100% + 12px)` (band top at box bottom — fully exited).

**Reduced motion**: inside the existing `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block, `.rk-scanlines-crawl::before { animation: none }` and `.rk-scanlines-crawl::after { animation: none; display: none }` — the crawl and band are fully zeroed (static scanlines only, band hidden). The base rules PRECEDE the reduced-motion block so its equal-specificity overrides win by source order, mirroring the `rk-waiting-halo`/`rk-waiting-seam` discipline.

### Hazard-Wedge Row Texture — paired with `thick` (`globals.css` `.rk-hazard`)

A window row whose marker is `thick` gets a static "hazard wedge" background — the completed / "taped-off" cue that pairs with the thick marker exactly as the scanlines pair with `double`. It is **NEVER animated in any state** (rest, hover, selected) — an explicit user decision after animated variants were tried and rejected as distracting.

**Static hazard wedge**: `.rk-hazard::before` paints a 45° weave in the guarded marker color (read from the same `--rk-marker-color` custom property the scanlines use, set on the row when `thick`) at 18% alpha via `color-mix`, on a **12px square tile** (`background-size: 12px 12px`) so adjacent thick rows weld seamlessly. A `mask-image: linear-gradient(to right, #000 22%, transparent 55%)` (with its `-webkit-` twin) confines the weave to a **left-edge wedge** — full strength for the first ~22% of the row, fading out by ~55% — a taped-off corner, not full-row wallpaper (full-width was tried and rejected as louder than the selected row; an earlier 13% fade-from-zero-to-38% version tapered so aggressively it was hard to make out and was re-tuned by user request).

**Overlay-owns-clip, mirroring scanlines exactly**: the wedge is a **dedicated absolutely-positioned inner element** (`absolute inset-0 z-[5] overflow-hidden pointer-events-none`), NEVER `overflow-hidden` on the row root (which would clip the row's `top-full` pin/color popovers — the same must-fix the scanlines documented). It sits above the tint/button background (`z-5`), below the `z-10` icon cluster and `z-20` gutter. `window-row.tsx` sets `--rk-marker-color` inline for `thick` rows the same way it already does for `double`. No `prefers-reduced-motion` gate is needed (it is static), but it stays listed in the reduced-motion audit block beside the adjacent scanlines block.

### Braille Sparkline Renderer

`app/frontend/src/lib/sparkline.ts` — converts an array of float values (0-100 range) into a Unicode braille sparkline string. Uses 8 vertical levels from the U+2800-U+28FF braille range filling bottom-to-top: `⣀⣄⣤⣦⣶⣷⣾⣿` (level 0 = `⣀`, level 7 = `⣿`). Values linearly interpolated across 8 levels. Zero-filled buffer renders as repeated `⣀`. Exported as `sparkline(samples: number[]): string`.

### Memory Gauge Renderer

`app/frontend/src/lib/gauge.ts` — utilities for the memory gauge visualization:
- `gaugeBar(ratio: number): string` — builds a filled/empty block string (`█` filled, `░` empty) from a 0-1 ratio. Fixed width of 10 characters
- `gaugeColor(percent: number): string` — returns a Tailwind color class: `text-green-500` (< 70%), `text-yellow-500` (70-90%), `text-red-500` (> 90%)
- `formatBytes(bytes: number): string` — compact human-readable size (`3.1G`, `512M`, `128K`)
- `formatMemory(used: number, total: number): string` — compact `used/total` string (e.g., `3.1G/8G`)

### Clipboard Utility

`app/frontend/src/lib/clipboard.ts` — shared `copyToClipboard(text: string): Promise<boolean>` function. Primary path uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText()`; fallback uses `document.execCommand('copy')` for non-secure contexts (HTTP). All callers (terminal copy, Pane panel row copy) import from this module (§ Steady-state version surfaces for the boolean success signal).

CWD display (line 1) uses `shortenPath()` to shorten the active pane's `cwd` (falls back to `worktreePath`):
- Home substitution: `/home/<user>/…` → `~/…`, `/Users/<user>/…` → `~/…`, `/root/…` → `~/…` (exact home dir → `~`). Handles Linux and macOS conventions.
- Truncation: if the path (after home substitution) has more than 2 non-empty segments, it is truncated to `…/<second-to-last>/<last>`. Paths with ≤ 2 segments are not truncated.
- Examples: `/home/sahil/code/org/repo/src` → `…/repo/src`; `/home/sahil/code/org` → `~/code/org`; `/var/log/nginx` → `…/log/nginx`.
- The `title` attribute on the CWD element always contains the original unmodified `activePaneCwd` — hover to see the full path.

## Live Safe-Name Conversion

Every naming entry point converts a user-typed name to its safe/canonical form **live, in the input's `onChange`** — the user watches "My problem" become "My_problem" as they type (WYSIWYG), so the optimistic-update name is identical to the committed name. The conversion is **per name kind**, sourced from the shared pure module `app/frontend/src/lib/names.ts` (the established `src/lib/*.ts` + colocated `*.test.ts` home for shared pure logic), which exports one transform per kind plus a commit finisher:

| Export | Rule |
|--------|------|
| `toSafeSessionName(raw)` | spaces, **hyphens**, colons, periods, and the backend forbidden set (`; & | ` backtick ` $ ( ) { } [ ] < > ! # * ?` + control chars) → `_`; collapse `_` runs; strip leading `_`; **preserve case**; cap 128 (`MaxNameLength`). Hyphen→`_` is session-specific (session-group collision avoidance). |
| `toSafeWindowName(raw)` | same as session but **keeps hyphens** — `riff-*` windows use them legitimately and the group-collision rationale does not apply. |
| `toSafeServerName(raw)` | anything outside `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]` → `_`; collapse; strip leading `_`; cap 64 (`MaxServerNameLength`). Matches backend `ValidateServerName`. |
| `toSafeWorktreeName(raw)` | the window rule plus `/`→`_` and a leading `-` dropped (matching `ValidateWorktreeName`). |
| `finalizeSafeName(name)` | commit-time finisher — strips leading **and trailing** `_` runs. |
| `deriveNameFromPath(p)` | path-derived session-name suggestion — extracts the last path segment, composes the session transform + `finalizeSafeName` (both ends trimmed, matching the old `toTmuxSafeName`). |

Case is preserved ("My problem" → "My_problem", never lowercased). Chars outside a kind's unsafe set (e.g. `@`, `%`, `+` for session/window names) pass through unchanged — this is conversion, not stripping.

**Live-typing edges** (§ Design Decisions → Live transform + commit finalizer split): the live `onChange` transform strips **leading** unsafe chars as typed (a space pressed in an empty field produces nothing) and collapses `_` runs live, but a **trailing** `_` stays visible while typing — trimming it live would delete the separator the user just typed ("My " + "p" would become "Myp"). Commit/submit sites run `finalizeSafeName` to trim the trailing `_`. An input that is empty after conversion falls through to the existing empty-name submit guards — no new error surface. There is no caret-position management beyond React-controlled-input defaults; a mid-string length-changing edit may move the caret to the end (an accepted edge).

**Wired entry points** (each applies the kind's transform in `onChange` and `finalizeSafeName` at commit):

| Surface | File | Kind |
|---------|------|------|
| Create-session dialog typed-name field | `create-session-dialog.tsx` | session |
| Session rename dialog input | `app.tsx` (state in `use-dialog-state.ts`) | session |
| Sidebar inline session rename | `sidebar/session-row.tsx` | session |
| Top-bar `WindowHeading` inline rename (also the palette's "Window: Rename" CustomEvent path — § Window Heading) | `top-bar.tsx` | window |
| Sidebar inline window rename (commits via shared `use-window-rename.ts`) | `sidebar/window-row.tsx` | window |
| "New iframe window" name field | `app.tsx` (`handleCreateIframeWindow`) | window |
| Host Overview create-server dialog | `host-overview-page.tsx` | server |
| AppShell create-server dialog | `app.tsx` (`handleCreateServer`) | server |
| Spawn/riff worktree-name field | `spawn-agent-dialog.tsx` | worktree |

The create dialog's **window mode has no name input** (windows are created unnamed and tmux auto-names them — § Unnamed `+ New Window`), so there is nothing to wire there.

**Known gap — board-route create-server dialog is NOT wired.** The third server-name input, the board-route create-server dialog (`src/components/board/board-page.tsx`, input at `board-page.tsx:1179`), still does a raw `setCreateServerName(e.target.value)` with no live `toSafeServerName`, and its `handleCreateServerSubmit` skips `finalizeSafeName` — it only gates submit on the pre-existing regex disabled-guard (`/^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$/.test(trimmed)`). So on that one surface a typed space still produces the old hard-rejection UX (submit blocked) rather than live conversion. This diverges from the "every naming entry point converts" intent and is a tracked should-fix, not shipped behavior — the other two server inputs (host-overview + AppShell) do convert live. (`260722-ln4n-auto-safe-name-conversion`)

The backend enforcement side of this contract (the tightened `ValidateNewName` for created/renamed-to names, permissive lookups for existing names) lives in [tmux-sessions](/run-kit/tmux-sessions.md) § Name-Validation Charset Contract and [architecture](/run-kit/architecture.md) § API Layer.

## Session Creation Pattern

### Instant Creation (Primary)

All primary session creation entry points create a session immediately without a dialog. Implemented by `executeCreateSessionInstant` in `app.tsx`.

**Algorithm**:
1. Derive a name from the active window's `worktreePath` using `deriveNameFromPath(worktreePath)` (from the shared `@/lib/names` module — § Live Safe-Name Conversion). If the result is empty (CWD is `/`, `~`, or `worktreePath` is undefined), the name is `session`.
2. Deduplicate against `sessions`: if the name is taken, try `{name}-2`, `{name}-3`, … up to `{name}-10`. If all are taken, use `{name}-11` (best-effort).
3. Call `createSession(server, derivedName, worktreePath)`. If no active window exists, call `createSession(server, "session")` (no `cwd` — tmux defaults to server CWD).
4. The session appears in the sidebar via the existing optimistic/ghost mechanism.

**Entry points** (all call `onCreateSession` → `executeCreateSessionInstant`):
- Sidebar `+` button (session level)
- Sidebar empty-state `+ New Session` button
- `SessionTiles` "+ New Session" dashed-border tile (the density view on `/$server`)
- Top-bar breadcrumb session dropdown `+ New Session` item
- Cmd+K "Session: Create" action

**Name derivation utilities**: `deriveNameFromPath` and the per-kind safe-name transforms live in the shared `app/frontend/src/lib/names.ts` module (§ Live Safe-Name Conversion), imported by `app.tsx` and every naming surface. Name conversion is split per kind (`toSafeSessionName`/`toSafeWindowName`/`toSafeServerName`/`toSafeWorktreeName`) — there is no single `toTmuxSafeName`.

### Folder-Prompted Creation (Secondary, via Cmd+K)

Two secondary entry points open `CreateSessionDialog` for users who want to specify a starting directory:

- **"Session: Create at Folder"** — opens `CreateSessionDialog` (mode `"session"`, default). The dialog's path input is pre-filled with `currentWindow.worktreePath` via the `defaultPath?: string` prop added to `CreateSessionDialogProps`. If no active window, the field starts empty.
- **"Window: Create at Folder"** — opens `CreateSessionDialog` with `mode="window"` and `session={currentSession}`. In window mode: title changes to "Create window at folder", session name input hidden, confirming calls `createWindow(server, session, undefined, cwd)` — **no name**, so tmux auto-names the window to the chosen folder's basename via `automatic-rename-format` (see [tmux-sessions](/run-kit/tmux-sessions.md) § Managed Window Creation). This flow sets no optimistic ghost, so there is no ghost label to derive. (`260707-j66b`)

`CreateSessionDialog` gains three optional backward-compatible props:
- `defaultPath?: string` — pre-fills the path input
- `mode?: "session" | "window"` — controls dialog behavior (default `"session"`)
- `session?: string` — required in window mode to pass to `createWindow`

### Deprecated: Dialog-First Flow

There is no `showCreateDialog` / `openCreateDialog` / `closeCreateDialog` API in `use-dialog-state.ts`; `CreateSessionDialog` is not opened by the sidebar `+` button or the primary "Session: Create" palette action. Use "Session: Create at Folder" in Cmd+K for folder-prompted session creation.

### Unnamed `+ New Window` — Folder Auto-Naming

The `+ New Window` action does **not** pin the window name to `"zsh"` — it calls `createWindow` with **no name**, so tmux auto-names the window to its folder basename and live-updates it (the tmux-layer convention lives in [tmux-sessions](/run-kit/tmux-sessions.md) § Managed Window Creation). A hardcoded name would leave a sidebar full of windows as indistinguishable "zsh" rows. (`260707-j66b`)

**All three unnamed-window call sites:**

1. **Sidebar `+ New Window`** (`app.tsx`, the `create-window` "Window: Create" palette action) — `createWindow(srv, session, undefined, activeWin?.worktreePath)`. Its optimistic-ghost label becomes the **raw** (unsanitized) basename of the creation cwd via the local `rawBasename(cwd)` helper (falls back to `"window"` when no cwd). This is deliberately **NOT** `deriveNameFromPath` — that runs the session safe-name transform (`.`→`_` etc.), but tmux's `#{b:pane_current_path}` uses the unsanitized basename, so the ghost must match the raw form tmux will actually display.
2. **Palette "Window: Create at Folder"** (`create-session-dialog.tsx`, `mode="window"`) — `createWindow(server, session, undefined, cwd)`; this flow sets **no** optimistic ghost, so no label is derived.
3. **Board-route `+ New Window`** (`board-page.tsx`) — `createWindowApi(srv, sess)` (no name, no cwd — the backend resolves a default; this action registers no ghost). Identical behavior to the same button on `/$server`.

**Client contract**: `createWindow(server, session, name?, cwd?, rkType?, rkUrl?)` in `api/client.ts` makes `name` optional and **omits it from the JSON body when absent/empty** (matching the existing omit-when-absent handling for `cwd`/`rkType`/`rkUrl`). Explicit names are still sent — the deliberate-name paths are unchanged: **iframe/service windows** (`Window: New Iframe Window`, `createWindow(..., name, undefined, "iframe", url)`), the `port-N` "Open in window" service windows (`server-list-page.tsx`), and `rk riff`.

**E2E** (`app/frontend/tests/e2e/new-window-unnamed.spec.ts` + sibling `.spec.md`): asserts, via route interception on `POST /api/sessions/*/windows`, that the `+ New Window` request body carries **no `name` key** — a deterministic frontend→API seam. (The visual auto-rename is NOT asserted: the e2e tmux server is created with a bare `new-session` and `CreateWindow` never sources the config there, so `automatic-rename-format` isn't reliably applied on that server; the tmux-native naming is covered by the config + Go arg-builder tests instead.) (`260707-j66b`)

**Ghost TTL backstop** (`GHOST_WINDOW_TTL_MS = 15_000`, exported from `src/store/window-store.ts`): an optimistic ghost row is **claimed** only when a NEW windowId (one not in the ghost's `snapshotWindowIds`) arrives for its `(server, session)` via SSE (`setWindowsForSession`, oldest-ghost-first). Ordinary creates confirm within one SSE tick (~1-2s), and a *failed* create's caller rollback calls `removeGhost`. But if a create **succeeds somewhere else** — e.g. a tmux target ambiguity routes the window into a different session (exact-match session targets prevent this at the backend, see [tmux-sessions](/run-kit/tmux-sessions.md) § Exact-Match Session Targets) — no rollback fires and no claim ever arrives, stranding a permanently greyed, pulsing, unclickable row. `addGhostWindow` therefore arms a `setTimeout(() => get().removeGhost(optimisticId), GHOST_WINDOW_TTL_MS)`: the unclaimed ghost self-clears after 15s (comfortably past any legitimate claim). `removeGhost` is **idempotent** (filters by `optimisticId`), so a timer firing after the ghost was already claimed or rolled back is a no-op. This is a generic frontend backstop for the "no rollback, no claim" strand, independent of the backend root-cause fix. Two fake-timer unit tests cover TTL expiry and the claimed-ghost no-op. (`260717-hikh`)

## Bottom Bar (Shell-level, Shared Across Routes)

Single row of `<kbd>` styled buttons, rendered at shell level via `<Shell>`'s `bottombar` grid area on every route that mounts `<Shell>` — single-terminal, board, and tmux Server (no-window) alike. Width tracks the right grid column (page width minus sidebar). Styled with `border-t-[3px] border-border` (the 3px structural seam — see § Border-Width System) and `px-1.5 h-[48px]`. Layout: `Tab Ctrl Alt Fn▴ ArrowPad | >_ ⌘K ⌨`. The hostname is shown in the sidebar Host panel, not the bottom bar; Escape lives in the Function key dropdown's extended-keys section. The `>_` button (conditionally rendered when `onOpenCompose` is provided) is an `aria-pressed` accent toggle for the docked compose strip (§ Docked Compose Strip), not a modal trigger. (`260509-17m3`, `260702-6m46`)

`BottomBarProps` contains no `wsRef`. Input handlers read `focused?.wsRef` from `FocusedTerminalContext` and send ANSI escape sequences through it. The component is byte-identical across single-terminal and board routes (same JSX, same callbacks: `onOpenCompose`, `onFocusTerminal`, `onScrollLockChange`) — board panes are terminals, so the input toolbar applies identically. When `focused === null` (Dashboard route, no active terminal), input handlers natural-no-op via the existing `wsRef.current?.readyState !== OPEN` guard — no error, no toast.

**Modifier toggles** (Ctrl, Alt): Sticky armed state with visual indicator (`accent` bg). Click to arm, auto-clears after next key is sent. Click again while armed to disarm. Multiple modifiers can be armed simultaneously. No Cmd (`⌘`) toggle — on desktop users hold the real Cmd key; on mobile Cmd combos aren't used in terminal workflows.

**Armed modifier bridging**: When modifiers are armed, a capture-phase `keydown` listener intercepts physical keypresses and translates them to terminal escape sequences (Ctrl+letter → control characters, Alt → ESC prefix). Sends via WebSocket, preventing xterm from receiving the unmodified key. Ignores real Cmd/Ctrl/Alt held by the OS.

**ArrowPad** (`arrow-pad.tsx`): Combined directional pad replacing individual arrow buttons. Sends ANSI escape sequences (`[A/B/C/D`). With modifiers, use xterm parameter encoding (`[1;{mod}X`). Modifier parameter: 1 + (alt?2:0) + (ctrl?4:0).

**Function key dropdown** (F▴): Opens a combined popup above the button. Top section: F1-F12 in a 4-column grid. Divider (`border-t border-border`). Bottom section: Esc, PgUp, PgDn, Home, End, Ins, Del in a 3-column grid (3x3, 7 items). Escape uses `sendSpecial` (preserves Ctrl re-arm semantics); other extended keys use `sendWithMods`. Closes after each selection, on outside click, or on Escape.

**Special keys** (Tab in bottom bar, Esc in Fn menu): Direct send via `sendSpecial`. Ctrl is not consumed for Esc/Tab (Esc IS Ctrl+[, Tab IS Ctrl+I in terminal semantics) — Ctrl stays armed for the next key. Alt prefix with ESC (Meta convention).

**All buttons**: 33px min-height / 35px min-width on fine pointers (`min-h-[33px] min-w-[35px]` — reduced from 36×36 to give the row air), full 36×36 touch target on coarse pointers (`coarse:min-h-[36px] coarse:min-w-[36px]`). Row gap is `gap-1.5` (6px) on fine pointers, `coarse:gap-1` (4px) on touch — so the 375px single-row budget is byte-identical to before. `text-xs`, `<kbd>` element styling. The row's vertical padding is asymmetric — `pt-1.5` with the bottom arm reading `max(var(--bottom-bar-floor,0.375rem),env(safe-area-inset-bottom))`: 6px everywhere except coarse pointers with the keyboard collapsed, where the `globals.css` floor rises to 1rem to clear the phone's corner-arc/home-indicator zone (§ Mobile Responsive → Safe-Area Insets).

**Chip tooltip chords** (`260801-mqim`): the `>_` Compose and `⌘K` palette chips carry **registry-resolved** `kbd` chips in their tips — `BottomBar` consumes `useKeybindings()` once and a local `chordFor(actionId)` helper applies the shared enabled-else-`undefined` derivation (§ Tier-1 `Tip`) for `compose-toggle` and `command-palette`. So the palette tip reads ⌘K on a mac and `Ctrl+K` on Windows/Linux, and both reflect overrides. **The ⌘K chip's own button FACE is the literal `⌘K` glyph on every platform** — the glyph is the chip's identity, not a chord claim; only the tooltip resolves.
