The remaining non-registry `kbd` strings in this area are the compose/chat submit (`composeSubmitKeycap()` → `⌘Enter` / `Ctrl+Enter`) and the per-surface Insert chips (`Enter` on the strip, `Alt+Enter` in chat), editing chords the registry does not own.

**Focus preservation**: All bottom bar buttons that send terminal input or toggle modifier state have `onMouseDown={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` via a shared `preventFocusSteal` handler. This prevents the browser from shifting focus away from xterm.js's hidden textarea when buttons are tapped, keeping the on-screen keyboard visible on iOS/touch devices. The CmdK button is excluded (it intentionally opens a dialog that takes focus). The ArrowPad handles focus preservation independently via its own `onMouseDown` handler.

### Docked Compose Strip

`ComposeStrip` (`components/compose-strip.tsx`) is a single **global**, sticky text-input surface docked at the bottom of the terminal area, immediately **above** the bottom-bar keys. It is a plain `<div>` in the shell footer — no backdrop, no `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal`, no focus trap, no Escape-closes, no close-on-send, no terminal dimming. A real `<textarea>` gives mobile autocorrect/IME (xterm is a `<canvas>` with neither) and a stable home for pasting large text blocks over a laggy relay.

**Two-row stack.** The input surface below the `→ {target}` header row is a `flex flex-col gap-1.5` **stack**, not a single button-flanked row: **row 1** is the `<textarea>` alone at `w-full`, **row 2** is a `flex items-center gap-1.5` button row holding 📎 at the left and an `ml-auto` group carrying Insert + Send at the right. The textarea's default is **`rows={2}`** on every device (desktop included), so the strip opens at two lines. The bounded auto-grow (`MAX_TEXTAREA_ROWS = 6`, scrollHeight-based `resize()`) treats that box as its **floor**: the `height = "auto"` measurement resolves to the `rows` attribute, so growing past two lines and deleting back settles at two rows, never one. The right group is an `ml-auto` container rather than a spacer element, which leaves the Tip-wrapped Insert/Send buttons untouched. (`260724-2bmy`)

**Two distinct send paths** (do not conflate): the compose strip rides the **relay WebSocket as raw bytes** — a submit sends `text + "\r"`, an insert-line sends `text + "\n"`, a raw insert sends bare `text` (no trailing byte), over the focused terminal's `wsRef` (the muxed RelayMux stream, same path as BottomBar keystrokes), no backend endpoint. The **chat-send** path is entirely separate — `POST /api/windows/{id}/chat/send` with named-buffer paste + NOVELTY echo probe + probe-and-submit-gated Enter + per-`(server,paneID)` lock (see [chat](/run-kit/chat.md) § send path). Compose does NOT use that machinery. The two surfaces DO share the keydown layer — one surface-parameterized `classifyComposeEnter` Enter policy plus one `handleReadlineKey` editing layer — but not the delivery mechanism, and not plain Enter's meaning (§ Send semantics).

**Toggle + persistence.** The strip's enablement is the `composeStripEnabled` chrome preference (ChromeContext, `localStorage["runkit-compose-strip"]`, `"true"` sentinel — the `terminalFontSize`/`fixedWidth` pattern; see § Terminal Font Size). Four parity affordances flip it via the SAME `toggleComposeStrip()` action: the ⇧⌘E / Shift+Ctrl+E `compose-toggle` chord (§ Keyboard Shortcuts — `ignoreInputs`, so it also closes the strip from inside the strip's own textarea; mounted on both AppShell and the board twin), the bottom-bar `>_` chip (`aria-pressed={composeStripEnabled}` + the ctrl/alt pressed-state vocabulary `bg-accent/20 border-accent text-accent` when on, `text-text-secondary` off; `preventFocusSteal` preserved), the `View: Text Input` palette action, and an **on-strip × close button** in the strip's own header row (`data-testid="compose-strip-close"`, `aria-label="Close compose strip"`). The × is a **pointer convenience** only — the chip and palette already satisfy Constitution V, so it adds no new keyboard shortcut. It consumes `toggleComposeStrip` from `useChromeDispatch()` *inside* `ComposeStrip` (not threaded as a per-route prop), so both footer mounts inherit the close affordance with zero per-route work. It sits at the **far right** of the `→ {target}` header row, grouped with the conditional "Uploading…" status in a single `ml-auto` flex container so the × stays right-aligned whether or not the upload status renders (uploading immediately left of it). It follows the strip's secondary-button vocabulary (`rk-glint`, bordered, `text-text-secondary`, hover `border-text-secondary`, compact `text-xs`/`px-1.5 py-0.5 leading-none`), carries `onMouseDown={preventFocusSteal}` like every other strip button, and takes the coarse-pointer touch treatment on **both** axes (`coarse:min-h-[36px] coarse:min-w-[36px]` — a bare × has no horizontal padding, so it needs `min-w` too, unlike the wider padded buttons that use only `min-h`; NOT the tiny 16px attachment-remove `×` pattern). Closing needs **no confirmation** — it is lossless because the draft (text + attachments) lives in the module store under its target's key and survives toggle-off/on (see **Drafts are per-target and survive refresh**). The caller gates the mount (`{composeStripEnabled && <ComposeStrip />}` in `app.tsx` and `board-page.tsx` footers). Toggling grows/shrinks the footer grid row by the strip's height, which the terminal container's existing `ResizeObserver` picks up → `fitAndSync` (FitAddon `fit()` + tmux resize) — the same reflow class as a font-size change; no dedicated refit effect.

**Live target.** The strip sends to the **currently-focused** pane's `wsRef` from `FocusedTerminalContext`, read live at send time — never a target snapshotted at open. A persistent surface that outlives many focus changes would, with a frozen target, silently send to a stale/closed pane. The wrong-pane risk is instead mitigated by an always-visible `→ {window}` target label, resolved in layers: window-store name by `server:windowId` (live — tracks renames) → the `windowName` the registrant carried into `FocusedTerminalContext` (board entries have a server-derived `windowName`; the store only covers servers whose sidebar group has delivered sessions, so board panes from other servers would otherwise label as `@N`) → raw windowId. With `focused === null` (e.g. the `/$server` tiles route) the strip renders **disabled** with a "no target" label and never sends.

**Send semantics — Enter transmits a line, Cmd/Ctrl+Enter submits.** The keydown routes through the shared pure `classifyComposeEnter(key, surface)` (`lib/compose-keys.ts`) — the SAME classifier the chat send form uses, called here with `surface: "strip"`. One classifier remains the single authority for both surfaces' Enter policy, but the two **deliberately diverge on plain Enter**, and the divergence is declared inside the classifier per surface, never forked at a call site (see § Design Decisions → Enter transmits a line in the strip, composes in chat, and [chat](/run-kit/chat.md) § Send-form input box). The classifier takes key state + surface alone — Enter policy is pointer-independent, so it reads no pointer hook — and stays text-agnostic (empty-text policy lives at the call sites). `send(mode)` takes `Exclude<ComposeEnterAction, "default">` and delivers over the focused terminal's `wsRef`, guarded on `draftKey !== null` and `wsRef.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN`:
- **Enter = insert line** — `ws.send(text + "\n")` and clear that target's draft. The strip overlays the VISIBLE terminal, so the transmitted line lands in the pane's own composer where the user can see it (Claude Code treats a raw `"\n"` as newline-insert), and consecutive Enters stage sentence-per-line exactly like typing into the pane. On an **empty or whitespace-only** textarea Enter is a **full no-op**: the keydown is consumed (no local newline appears) and nothing is sent.
- **Shift+Enter = newline** — the ONLY local multi-line compose in the strip.
- **Cmd/Ctrl+Enter = submit** — `ws.send(text + "\r")`; on an **empty or whitespace-only** textarea a bare `ws.send("\r")` ("press Enter in the pane"), which completes the stage-then-submit loop from the keyboard. Whitespace-only counts as empty: the whitespace is discarded, never transmitted, and the draft still clears.
- **Alt+Enter = raw insert** — chord-only. `ws.send(text)` with **NO trailing byte** (byte-exact, for completing a partial line already in the agent's input); empty/whitespace-only is a no-op.
- Precedence (first match wins): non-Enter/IME-composing → default; meta/ctrl → submit; alt → insert; shift → default; plain Enter → insert-line (strip) / default (chat).

Enter is guarded against IME composition (`e.nativeEvent.isComposing`). A `"default"` action falls through to the textarea; every other action gets `preventDefault()` + `stopPropagation()` and is handed to `send(action)` as-is. Clear-on-delivery is uniform across modes — a delivered send clears that target's text + attachments (and revokes its preview URLs) and the strip stays open, including a delivered whitespace-only submit. A **guard-blocked** send (no target / stream not open) early-returns WITHOUT clearing, so the draft is preserved. Terminal-conventional `"\n"` semantics are **accepted and documented in the send-path code comment, not guarded**: on a plain shell pane the transmitted `"\n"` EXECUTES the line — which is exactly what Enter does in a terminal — and an embedded `\n` inside a raw insert executes per line there too, so raw insert is only truly Enter-free for single-line text on non-TUI panes.

**Readline editing chords** (`lib/readline-keys.ts` — shared with the chat send form, the consistency mirror of the shared Enter classifier). The textarea keydown routes through `handleReadlineKey(e.nativeEvent, e.currentTarget)` **before** Enter classification (after the strip's Escape branch), intercepting exactly five chords that browsers/macOS leave unbound: **Ctrl+U** (kill from cursor to line start — readline unix-line-discard, not a whole-line wipe), **Ctrl+W** (delete word backward), **Alt+B / Alt+F** (move cursor word backward / forward), **Alt+D** (delete word forward). Matching is on `KeyboardEvent.code` (`KeyU`/`KeyW`/`KeyB`/`KeyF`/`KeyD`), never `key` — on macOS Alt+B/F/D compose `∫`/`ƒ`/`∂` into `key`, so key-based matching would miss exactly the chords the layer exists for. Modifier matching is exact (Meta or Shift anywhere → unhandled; Ctrl chords reject Alt and vice versa) and IME-composing keydowns are never intercepted, so the natively-bound Cocoa chords (Ctrl+A/E/B/F/P/N/D/H/K/T/O/Y, Opt+arrows, Opt+Delete) pass through untouched. Word boundaries are whitespace-delimited (the readline default), crossing newlines; `lineStart` keys on the previous `\n`. Motions use `setSelectionRange`; deletions go through `document.execCommand("delete")` after selecting the range so the native undo stack (Cmd+Z) survives, with a controlled-component-safe fallback (prototype value setter + a bubbled `input` event) when `execCommand` is missing or returns false (jsdom, future removals) — that fallback keeps React state in sync at the cost of undo for that edit. A handled chord calls `preventDefault()` + `stopPropagation()` (so it never reaches global chord listeners) and returns `true`; an **empty** edit range (Ctrl+U at line start, Ctrl+W at position 0) still consumes the chord but performs no edit, because a collapsed-selection `execCommand("delete")` would eat a character backward. Conflict safety: app chords are suppressed while a textarea has focus (`shouldSuppressChord`), the `ignoreInputs` punch-through set (⌘K, ⇧⌘E, ⇧⌘/, ⇧⌘,) does not collide, and Alt chords are excluded from every registry tier (§ Keyboard Shortcuts). The one near-miss is `KeyW`: `kill-window` claims it on the `cmd` (⌘W) and `shifted` (⇧Ctrl+W) tiers — never plain Ctrl+W — so the exact-modifier rule (Meta or Shift → unhandled) is what keeps the two from overlapping rather than luck. **Platform caveat**: on Windows/Linux *browsers* Ctrl+W is browser-reserved (closes the tab before the page sees it) — that binding works on macOS and in the desktop shell only; win/linux web users keep native Ctrl+Backspace. Ctrl+U (view-source) IS interceptable and works everywhere. The pure classification + cursor math (`classifyReadlineKey`, `wordLeft`/`wordRight`/`lineStart`, `readlineMotionTarget`, `readlineDeleteRange`) are exported for mount-free unit tests (the `palette-move.ts` extraction pattern).

**Buttons + truthful `enterkeyhint`.** The two chips mirror their chords, so their enable conditions **split** rather than sharing one flag. A secondary **Insert** house-chip (border-styled, `data-testid="compose-strip-insert"`, `aria-label="Insert line"`, `preventFocusSteal`, `coarse:min-h-[36px]`) renders left of Send and fires `send("insert-line")` — it follows Enter, so it is disabled when there is no target or the text is empty/whitespace-only (`canInsert`), matching Enter's empty no-op. **Send** fires `send("submit")` and is enabled whenever a target exists (`hasTarget`) — an empty click sends the same bare `"\r"` its chord does, because a button that advertises a chord and then diverges from it on empty would be a lying affordance. Tips: Insert reads `label="Insert line (Alt+Enter: raw insert)"` with `kbd="Enter"` — the chord-only raw insert stays discoverable in the label text, with no native `title` (titles are retired for Tip chips); Send keeps the platform-formatted submit chord from the shared `composeSubmitKeycap()` (`⌘Enter` on mac, `Ctrl+Enter` elsewhere, via `formatCombo({code:"Enter",tier:"cmd"}, detectPlatform())`), so both surfaces render the identical chip. None of these chords are registered in the command palette (focused-textarea editing chords like the unregistered Shift+Enter; the palette steals focus from the textarea it would act on), and Constitution V's keyboard reach is met by the chords themselves. The textarea's `enterKeyHint` is **`"send"`** unconditionally — the truthful hint, since Enter transmits the text to the pane and clears the draft. `compose-strip.tsx` consumes no pointer hook at all. (`260802-lj98`)

**Drafts are per-target and survive refresh.** Every draft belongs to its **addressee**, not to the user: the store (`lib/compose-draft-store.ts`) is a map keyed by the send target's `entryKey(server, windowId)` — the same key the strip's `focusedKey()` computes from the live focused target each render. Switching targets (route change or board pane-focus cycling) swaps the visible draft; each window keeps its own unsent text + attachments, and a delivered send clears only that target's draft. With no target the key is `null`, the store yields a single frozen empty draft, and the key-bound setters no-op (the strip is disabled anyway). The store lives at module scope — not component state — because the mount is conditional (toggle-off unmounts it) AND per-route (the AppShell and BoardPage footers are distinct React subtrees), so component-local `useState` would destroy unsent drafts on toggle-off and on terminal↔board route changes. It is exposed through the same module-slot + listener-set + stable-snapshot pattern as the window-switch pending mask (`window-transition.ts`), read as `useSyncExternalStore(subscribeComposeDraft, () => getComposeDraft(draftKey))`: one **global** listener set (any commit notifies — at most one strip is mounted) over **per-key** stable snapshots, so `useSyncExternalStore` sees an identical object reference while a key's draft is unchanged. Commits notify only on an actual change. (`260718-dhdj`, `260801-cyth`)

**Text persistence — one localStorage key, pruned on every write.** Draft **text** survives a page refresh via `localStorage["runkit-compose-drafts"]`, a JSON map `{[entryKey]: {text, updatedAt}}` written through **synchronously** on every commit and hydrated at module load by the exported `hydrateComposeDrafts()` (also the test-reset seam: `localStorage.clear()` + re-hydrate). The read is tolerant in the `parseOverrides` posture of `keybindings.ts` — malformed JSON, non-object/array roots, and wrong-typed entries degrade to empty/skipped via an `isStoredDraft` type guard rather than an `as` cast — and every localStorage access is `try`/`catch` best-effort, never throwing. One shared `pruneDraftEntries()` helper runs on **both** the write and the hydrate path (no duplicated pipeline): empty-text entries are never stored, entries outside the `MAX_DRAFT_AGE_MS` (7-day) window are dropped — the check is two-sided (`Math.abs`) so a future-dated `updatedAt` from clock skew or corrupted storage ages out instead of sorting first and squatting the cap forever — and only the `MAX_PERSISTED_DRAFTS` (30) newest by `updatedAt` are kept. Pruning is not optional hygiene: tmux window IDs are **reused after a server restart**, so a stale draft can otherwise point at a stranger. An all-empty map removes the key rather than storing `{}`. Multi-tab is last-write-wins — no `storage`-event sync. (`260801-cyth`)

**Attachments are in-memory per key; blob URLs are per-mount.** `File` objects cannot reach localStorage, so pending attachments live only in the in-memory map under their target's key and a hydrated draft carries an empty attachments array. The loss is cosmetic: uploads are **eager** (already on the target worktree's disk at attach time) and their path lines live in the persisted **text**, so after a refresh the previews and × remove-chips are gone but the path lines and uploaded files remain and sending still works. Blob-URL previews stay component-local (a fresh per-mount `Map<File, string>` ref, recreated lazily via `getBlobUrl` from the retained `File` objects). They are reclaimed at three seams: on unmount, on a delivered send (scoped to the cleared draft's files only — other targets' previews recreate lazily when their draft is shown again), and by a `[files]` effect that revokes URLs for any file no longer in the **visible** draft, so a departed target's URLs cannot strand in the map while the strip stays mounted. (`260801-cyth`)

**Focus routing — focus on OPEN, never otherwise.** Opening the strip focuses its textarea; everything else leaves focus where the user put it. The seam is a module-level **focus-on-open flag** in `lib/compose-strip-events.ts` (`markComposeStripFocusOnOpen` / `consumeComposeStripFocusOnOpen`, the same module-slot shape as the attach queue and focus registry beside it): `toggleComposeStrip()` marks it on the **off→on transition only**, and ComposeStrip's mount effect consumes-and-clears it and focuses. Because every open path funnels through that one toggle — the `>_` chip, the `View: Text Input` palette entry, the ⇧⌘E chord, the terminal's drag-drop/paste auto-enable, and the board twin — one seam covers them all with no per-affordance wiring. The consume **always clears**, even when the effect declines to focus (the disabled "no target" state), so a stale flag can never grab focus on a later remount. A plain route remount with the strip already enabled never calls the toggle, so it never sets the flag and never steals focus. The rest of the no-steal contract is intact: **after-send never focuses**, and Escape in the textarea blurs it back to the terminal (**never closes the strip** — the × close button does not alter this; only the explicit toggle affordances close it). On mobile, opening summons the IME — desired, matching the ⌨ chip. `preventFocusSteal` (`onMouseDown` preventDefault) is on the Send/📎/Insert/× buttons. On touch devices the ⌨ keyboard button focuses the strip's real textarea when the preference is on, via the **focus registry** in the same module (`registerComposeStripFocuser`/`focusComposeStrip` — a module registry, not a `document.querySelector('[data-testid]')`, since test ids are test-only here; the focuser returns `false` in the disabled "no target" case so `bottom-bar.tsx` falls back to `onFocusTerminal`). (`260801-sm6g`)

**Uploads — eager and worktree-scoped; an attachment never faces a foreign worktree.** The strip calls `useFileUpload(focused.session, focused.windowId, focused.server)` scoped to the **live** focused target, so uploads land in that window's worktree `.uploads/` (backend `upload.go` unchanged — see § File Upload). Because the draft — attachments included — is keyed by that same target, an attachment can only ever be shown alongside the worktree it was uploaded to: changing focus changes the whole draft, so there is **no re-upload, no path rewriting, and no error surface** on a target change (the strip carries no `role="alert"` error state at all). The retained `File` objects back previews only. Terminal drag-drop / clipboard-paste gestures (`terminal-client.tsx`) hand raw files to the strip via a document `CustomEvent` bridge (`COMPOSE_STRIP_ATTACH_EVENT` + a module hand-off queue in `compose-strip-events.ts`, drained on both the event and mount), enabling the preference first if off — no prop-drilling of upload results. The `File[]` overload on `uploadFiles` (`hooks/use-file-upload.ts`) exists for that queue. (`260801-cyth`)

**Board-pane focus cleanup.** `BoardPane` registers itself as the focused terminal while `isFocused`, and clears `FocusedTerminalContext` on **unmount** iff it is still the registered pane (`focusedRef.current?.wsRef === wsRef`, unmount-only `[]` deps, ref-guarded so a sibling's newer registration during a focus cycle is not clobbered). Without it, leaving a board for `/$server` would strand a stale non-null target — the enabled strip would render active with a stale label and upload into the stale worktree. With it, the strip reverts to the disabled "no target" state.

### File Upload

Entry points, all on the terminal page:
- **Clipboard paste** (`Cmd+V` / `Ctrl+V`) — document-level paste listener; files in `clipboardData.files` trigger upload, text-only paste passes through to xterm
- **Drag-and-drop** — drop files onto the terminal area; `ring-2 ring-accent` border highlight during drag-over; non-file drag content ignored
- **Compose strip upload button** (📎) — the left-most chip on the docked compose strip's button row (§ Docked Compose Strip → Two-row stack); opens native file picker via hidden `<input type="file">`

The paste/drop gestures live in `terminal-client.tsx` and hand raw files to the docked compose strip (enabling the `composeStripEnabled` preference first if off) via the `COMPOSE_STRIP_ATTACH_EVENT` document bridge; the strip uploads them to the **focused** target's worktree and inserts one path per line in its textarea (see § Docked Compose Strip). Server writes to `.uploads/{YYMMDD-HHmmss}-{sanitized-name}` in the target window's worktree. 50MB size limit. `.uploads/` auto-added to `.gitignore` on first use.

### iOS Keyboard Support

`useVisualViewport` hook (`app/frontend/src/hooks/use-visual-viewport.ts`) manages all viewport-related CSS side effects: adds the `fullbleed` class to `<html>` on mount (removed on cleanup), and listens to both `resize` and `scroll` events on `window.visualViewport`, setting `--app-height` (and `--app-offset-top`) CSS custom properties from `visualViewport.height`. The `scroll` listener catches iOS Safari viewport panning that doesn't trigger `resize`. **The hook is called once in `RootWrapper`** so the `--app-height` var is maintained on EVERY route — including the Host page and edge pages that mount no `Shell` — and owning the single idempotent effect at the root avoids the double-mount cleanup race a second copy in `Shell` would create. (`260707-4vq2`)

**Keyboard-open signal — `html.kb-open`.** The same rAF-coalesced `apply()` pass also derives a boolean keyboard-open signal and publishes it as the `kb-open` class on `<html>` (`syncKeyboardSignal`, called from `apply()` and from the initial sync — the hook's only two call sites). It registers **no additional listeners**: the derivation rides the existing `resize`/`scroll` pair, and `width` joins `height`/`offsetTop` in the change guard so a rotation reaches it. Removal is paired with `fullbleed`/`--app-height` in the existing cleanup, and the whole machinery is skipped when `window.visualViewport` is absent (jsdom, old browsers) — no class, no throw. The signal's sole consumer today is the bottom bar's raised coarse-pointer safe floor (§ Mobile Responsive → Safe-Area Insets); it is app-wide because the hook mounts at the root, so both bottom-bar render sites (app shell and the board twin) see it for free.

The heuristic: **baseline = the maximum `visualViewport.height` observed since the last `visualViewport.width` change**; the signal is on when `height < baseline − KEYBOARD_DELTA_PX`, with `KEYBOARD_DELTA_PX = 150` (exported constant, `use-visual-viewport.ts`). A keyboard shrinks height without touching width, whereas rotations and window resizes change width — so a width change is the baseline-reset trigger. 150px sits above iOS URL-bar chrome deltas (~50–114px) and below any real keyboard (~260px+). Two accepted limitations, both self-healing: **rotating or split-screen-resizing while the keyboard is open** re-baselines to the keyboarded height and clears `kb-open` until the next keyboard close/open cycle; **mounting with the keyboard already up** likewise reads as closed until the keyboard first closes (which grows the baseline). Both degrade to the 6px base floor, never to a wrong-direction pad. Unit coverage is `use-visual-viewport.test.ts` (mocked `visualViewport` + controllable rAF): baseline-off, keyboard-on, restore-off, sub-threshold chrome delta does not fire, rotation resets the baseline, cleanup removes the class, missing-`visualViewport` mount/unmount is safe, and the listener set stays exactly `resize` + `scroll`. (`260805-fi9m`)

In fullbleed mode, `globals.css` pins the **`.app-root` root layout container** (`position: fixed; inset: 0; height: var(--app-height, 100vh)`) to the visual viewport regardless of document scroll — the pin lives on `.app-root` (not the per-route `.app-shell` beneath it) because the persistent TopBar lives inside `.app-root` above the content region, so pinning that container keeps the header above the content. The per-route `.app-shell` is `position: relative; height: 100%` — it fills the root layout's `flex-1` region (its `overflow: hidden` contains xterm's canvas). (`260707-4vq2`) When the iOS keyboard appears, the bottom bar stays pinned above it, the terminal shrinks, and xterm refits via the existing `ResizeObserver`. The `fullbleed` class is also present in `index.html` as a static default (FOUC prevention); the hook takes over lifecycle management at runtime.

**Keyboard toggle** (`⌨` U+2328): Right-aligned button in the bottom bar, visible only on touch devices (`hidden coarse:inline-flex`). Bidirectional toggle: when terminal is focused (detected via `document.activeElement.closest(".xterm")`), tapping blurs to dismiss the keyboard; when not focused, tapping calls `onFocusTerminal` callback which chains through `app.tsx` → `TerminalClient.focusRef` → `xtermRef.current.focus()` to summon the keyboard. Dynamic `aria-label`: "Hide keyboard" / "Show keyboard". Uses `preventFocusSteal` to avoid stealing focus on the dismiss path.

**Scroll-lock mode**: Long-press (>= 500ms) on the keyboard toggle button activates scroll-lock — a mode that prevents the soft keyboard from appearing when the terminal area is tapped, allowing uninterrupted reading and scrolling. State is a `scrollLocked` boolean in `BottomBar` (default `false`), exposed to the parent via `onScrollLockChange` callback and passed down to `TerminalClient` as a `scrollLocked` prop. When locked, a capture-phase `focusin` listener on the terminal container immediately blurs any `.xterm` element that gains focus, preventing the keyboard from appearing. Touch scroll gestures (SGR mouse sequences) are unaffected — only focus is prevented. Activating scroll-lock while the keyboard is visible auto-dismisses it (`document.activeElement?.blur()`). Optional haptic feedback via `navigator.vibrate?.(50)` on toggle (graceful no-op if unavailable).

- **Long-press detection**: `touchstart`/`touchend`/`touchmove` handlers with a 500ms timer (`LONG_PRESS_MS` constant). Touch move > 10px (`LONG_PRESS_MOVE_THRESHOLD`) cancels the long-press. On timer expiry, scroll-lock toggles and subsequent `touchend`/`click` are suppressed via a ref flag. Desktop click behavior is unaffected (touch events only).
- **Tap in locked mode**: Tapping the keyboard button (< 500ms) when `scrollLocked` is `true` unlocks AND summons the keyboard in one action — matches user intent without requiring a double-tap.
- **Visual indicator**: When locked, the button shows `bg-accent/20 border-accent text-accent` (same armed-state pattern as Ctrl/Alt modifier toggles) and the icon changes from `⌨` (U+2328) to `🔒` (U+1F512). `aria-label` updates to "Scroll lock on — tap to unlock".
- **State lifecycle**: Session-scoped (component-local React state, not persisted). Resets on navigation (component unmount/remount). The docked compose strip is unaffected — it has its own input field outside the terminal container.

### Terminal Touch Scroll

The terminal container div has `touch-pan-y` (CSS `touch-action: pan-y`) — allows vertical swipe gestures for scrollback access on mobile/touch devices while blocking horizontal panning (prevents page-level overflow from tmux's ~80 column minimum). The xterm.js `.xterm-viewport` has `overflow-y: scroll` and `overscroll-behavior: none` (in `globals.css`), so the browser delegates vertical touch scroll to xterm natively without page bounce. In the single-view model, fullbleed is always active — `overflow: hidden` and `overscroll-behavior: none` are applied to both `html` and `body` (via `globals.css`), preventing iOS Safari elastic bounce scrolling. The docked compose strip and bottom bar are siblings of the terminal container, not children, so their touch behavior is preserved.

## Mobile Responsive

### Breakpoints & Container Width

All zones use `px-3 sm:px-6` — reduced horizontal padding on screens < 640px. No `max-w-4xl` constraint — terminal, top bar, and bottom bar all span full width. The mobile/desktop breakpoint for the `<Shell>` topology is **640px** (matches the existing `sm:` Tailwind breakpoint and `useIsMobile()` hook): `>= 640px` uses the 2-column grid with sidebar full-height; `< 640px` uses the single-column grid with sidebar overlay. Sidebar is drag-resizable on desktop (default 220px, min 160, max 400); terminal fills remaining space via the right grid column. Terminal container has `py-0.5 px-1` padding for breathing room against border lines. The bottom-bar toolbar row uses `pt-1.5` with a `--bottom-bar-floor`-driven bottom arm — 6px, raised to 1rem on coarse pointers while the keyboard is collapsed (§ Safe-Area Insets).

### Touch Targets

A custom Tailwind variant `coarse:` is defined in `globals.css` via `@custom-variant coarse (@media (pointer: coarse))`. On touch devices, interactive elements are upsized per surface — `coarse:` is the mechanism, not a universal size (there is no single 44px minimum; each surface documents its own target). Upsized elements include:
- Top-bar icon controls — the shared fixed-size token's `coarse:w-[30px] coarse:h-[30px]` arm (`TOP_BAR_BUTTON*`, § Right cluster → shared button-size token); content-width chips/segments share the height axis via `TOP_BAR_BUTTON_H` / `TOP_BAR_SEGMENT_H`
- Sidebar session/window row action buttons — `coarse:min-w-[32px] coarse:min-h-[36px]`, both axes (§ Sidebar Row Icon System explains why 32 and not 36 on the width axis)
- Breadcrumb name dropdown triggers
- `⋯` command palette trigger
- Hamburger icon (sidebar toggle — single state covers desktop column collapse and mobile overlay) (`260509-17m3`)

**Both axes or neither.** An icon-only control needs `coarse:min-w-*` as well as `coarse:min-h-*`: a bare glyph has no horizontal padding to grow into, so a `min-h`-only rule yields a tall-but-thin target that measures ~17px wide on touch. Wider padded chips (text buttons) can get away with `min-h` alone.

Bottom bar buttons use the full `coarse:min-h-[36px] coarse:min-w-[36px]` touch target on touch devices, `min-h-[33px] min-w-[35px]` on fine pointers (with a 6px fine / 4px coarse row gap). The row's own bottom padding also lifts on coarse pointers — via the `--bottom-bar-floor` custom property rather than a `coarse:` utility class, because the lift is additionally gated on the keyboard being collapsed (§ Safe-Area Insets).

### Viewport Zoom Prevention

The viewport meta tag in `app/frontend/index.html` includes `maximum-scale=1.0` and `user-scalable=no` to prevent iOS Safari from auto-zooming when text inputs (command palette, docked compose strip) receive focus. Without these directives, iOS zooms in on inputs with `font-size < 16px`, displacing the entire interface. Pinch-to-zoom is also disabled — acceptable tradeoff for a keyboard-first tool dashboard where zoom doesn't improve terminal readability. The `interactive-widget=resizes-content` directive (keyboard layout resizing) and `viewport-fit=cover` (§ Safe-Area Insets) ride the same meta tag and are unrelated to zoom.

### Safe-Area Insets

**The app is `viewport-fit=cover` and every edge-docked pad is a `max(<floor>, env(safe-area-inset-<edge>))` expression — but on this app `env()` is 0 in in-browser Safari, so the floor is what actually ships the clearance.** The viewport meta in `app/frontend/index.html` carries `viewport-fit=cover`, which is the precondition for `env(safe-area-inset-*)` resolving to anything but 0 on iOS. In practice it resolves to **0 in in-browser iOS Safari** for this app on **both** edges: the page is `position: fixed` and non-scrollable (`html.fullbleed .app-root` pinned to `--app-height`, § iOS Keyboard Support), so Safari never enters the chrome states where it reports insets. Non-zero values appear only in standalone PWA mode. Device-confirmed on the bottom edge 2026-08-05 (photo evidence: the bottom-bar chips clipped by the corner arc). The `max()` shape is retained everywhere so genuine inset reporting — standalone PWA, future Safari behavior — still wins where it exists. Two consumers: (`260724-2bmy`, `260805-fi9m`)

- **Bottom bar toolbar row** (`bottom-bar.tsx`): `pt-1.5 pb-[max(var(--bottom-bar-floor,0.375rem),env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]` — asymmetric padding whose bottom arm reads a floor custom property. `--bottom-bar-floor` is owned by `globals.css` beside the `html.fullbleed` block: `0.375rem` (6px) at `:root`, raised to **`1rem` (16px)** under `@media (pointer: coarse)` on `html:not(.kb-open)`. That raised coarse floor is what clears the corner-arc/home-indicator zone for the extreme chips (`⇥` left, `⌨` right) on iPhone-class devices — `env()` contributes nothing in-browser. Fine-pointer and desktop-shell layouts stay byte-identical at 6px (the `@media (pointer: coarse)` scope is the gate; the raised floor must not leak into contexts that don't need it — the same rule as the shell's `isShell()` top gate below). Accepted cost: flat-screen touch devices spend ~10px of terminal height they didn't need.
- **Top-bar `<header>`** (`top-bar.tsx`): `pt-[env(safe-area-inset-top)]` — a guard, because `viewport-fit=cover` in standalone PWA mode also extends the page under the status bar, which would tuck the bar beneath the clock. The class is `isShell()`-gated OFF inside the desktop shell (a conditional template literal — browser/PWA output stays byte-identical): the `ShellTitlebarStrip` already reserves the titlebar band in the SPA layout (§ Desktop-Shell Titlebar Strip), and macOS hidden-titlebar windows can report that band as `safe-area-inset-top`, so keeping the padding would reserve the band twice. `isShell()` is read once per render — the preload injects `window.runkitShell` before any SPA script runs, so the value is stable for the page's lifetime. (`260805-9hn1`)

**The raised bottom floor is gated by an explicit JS keyboard signal.** A static raised floor would waste vertical space on an already-short viewport when the on-screen keyboard is up and the bar rides above it on flat screen area. `useVisualViewport` therefore toggles **`kb-open` on `<html>`** (§ iOS Keyboard Support), and `html:not(.kb-open)` is what admits the 1rem floor — with the keyboard open, coarse pointers fall back to the same 6px floor fine pointers always use. **Do NOT rely on `env()` collapsing, or on any layout-viewport side effect of `interactive-widget=resizes-content`, to produce this behavior**: iOS support for that directive is unreliable, and on this app `env()` is already 0 in both keyboard states, so it carries no signal at all. JS toggles the class; the padding math stays entirely in CSS.

**Playwright/Chromium reports every `env()` as 0**, so e2e can honestly assert only the floor values and the class flip (`bottom-bar-safe-floor.spec.ts`: 16px coarse-emulated via `hasTouch: true`, 6px after adding `kb-open`, 6px on fine pointers). The `max()` arm itself is verifiable only from the compiled class, never from a computed style — no test may pretend to exercise a real inset.

**New chrome docked to a screen edge SHOULD use this convention** rather than a hardcoded pad: `max(<the pad you wanted>, env(safe-area-inset-<edge>))`. Treat `env()` as a bonus that fires only in standalone PWA mode — pick the floor as if it will never fire, and scope any coarse-pointer-only lift with `@media (pointer: coarse)`.

**Interaction rule: edge-docked safe-area padding and shell-reserved chrome must not stack.** Where the desktop shell already reserves an edge with its own chrome (today: the titlebar strip at the top edge), the safe-area pad for that edge is gated off with `isShell()` rather than layered on top — the OS-reported inset covers the same band the shell chrome occupies, and applying both reserves it twice. (`260805-9hn1`)

### Terminal Addons

Addons loaded in `init()` from **static top-of-file imports**, after `terminal.open()`, before `ResizeObserver` setup. Order: FitAddon (existing) → fit() → ClipboardAddon → WebLinksAddon → UnicodeGraphemesAddon → WebglAddon. The Unicode addon MUST precede WebGL so the renderer measures cell widths against the active Unicode 15 table on first paint.

**Static import bundling**: all six xterm-family value imports (`@xterm/xterm` `Terminal`, `addon-fit`, `addon-clipboard`, `addon-web-links`, `addon-unicode-graphemes`, `addon-webgl`) are static top-of-file `import` statements in `terminal-client.tsx`, NOT runtime `await import()`. (`260531-m3pl`) Because `terminal-client.tsx` is already router-lazy, the xterm family bundles into the already-deferred terminal-route chunk and loads **once when that chunk loads** — never as a per-pane-mount runtime module-graph fetch. This removes all six chunk fetches from the browser's per-origin connection budget at terminal-init time, fixing the board-route E2E hang: on the plaintext dev/test origin (no HTTP/2 — only negotiated over TLS), the browser caps ~6 persistent connections per origin, and long-lived streams (Vite HMR socket, pooled SSE `EventSource`, one `/relay/<wid>` WS per visible pane) already saturate the slots, so a 7th xterm chunk fetch hung pending forever → `setTerminalReady(true)` never ran → blank pane → E2E poll timeout. Masked in production over Tailscale HTTPS (h2 multiplexes). The CSS side-effect import (`@xterm/xterm/css/xterm.css`) and type-only `import("@xterm/...")` annotations are static too.

| Addon | Purpose | Notes |
|-------|---------|-------|
| `@xterm/addon-fit` | Auto-resize columns/rows | Existing — loaded first, `fit()` called immediately |
| `@xterm/addon-clipboard` | OSC 52 clipboard sequences | Custom `ClipboardProvider` accepts both `""` (empty/default, tmux's format) and `"c"` (explicit) selection targets. Rejects `"p"`, `"s"`, `"0"`–`"7"`. Provider exported as `clipboardProvider` for testability |
| `@xterm/addon-web-links` | Clickable URLs in terminal output | Constructed **with an explicit activation handler** — `new WebLinksAddon((_event, uri) => window.open(uri, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer"))`. The handler-less default is not viable (§ WebLinksAddon Explicit Handler) |
| `@xterm/addon-unicode-graphemes` | Unicode 15 + grapheme-cluster width tables | Requires `allowProposedApi: true` on the Terminal constructor; `terminal.unicode.activeVersion = "15-graphemes"` set after `loadAddon()`. Must load before the WebGL addon |
| `@xterm/addon-webgl` | GPU-accelerated rendering | The `new WebglAddon()` / `loadAddon(...)` construction is wrapped in try/catch — silently falls back to canvas renderer on GPU/WebGL-context failure. The *module load* is static (resolved at chunk load, cannot fail at `init()` time), but context creation can still throw at runtime, so the runtime guard is required (`260531-m3pl`) |

#### WebLinksAddon Explicit Handler

`WebLinksAddon` is constructed with an explicit activation handler that calls `window.open(uri, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")` — the codebase's universal external-open idiom (`app.tsx`, `board-page.tsx`, `buildOpenPrAction`). **The handler is load-bearing for the desktop shell, not a stylistic preference.**

The addon's default handler opens a window with **no URL** (to get a blank window whose `.opener` it can clear), then assigns `location.href = uri` on it. In a browser that works — `window.open()` succeeds on the click gesture. In the Electron shell it cannot: the main process's `setWindowOpenHandler` sees `url: "about:blank"`, which is not http(s), so the intent is denied (see [desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Security Wiring); `window.open()` returns `null` and the addon bails with a console warning ("Opening link blocked as opener could not be cleared"). The failure mode is a dead link with nothing but that console warning as evidence, and the shell cannot fix it from its side: `about:blank` carries no URL by the time main sees it.

Passing the URI through `window.open` directly makes the two environments agree: identical browser behavior (new tab, opener severed by `noopener,noreferrer` rather than the addon's manual `.opener = null` dance), and the shell's window-open policy receives the real URL and routes it to the system browser. Covered by a unit test in `terminal-client.test.tsx` asserting the addon is constructed *with* a function and that invoking it calls `window.open` with the three-arg idiom; there is no e2e — xterm renders links on a canvas, so link-region clicks are not reliably automatable. (`260730-e9lz`)

### Terminal Font Size (user-controllable global preference)

Terminal font size is a **global** user preference owned by `ChromeContext`, not a per-pane or per-window value (`260613-oo89`). The context exposes the **effective** size as `terminalFontSize: number` (what `TerminalClient` reads and the top-bar control displays); the internal preference is stored as `number | null` (null = unset) and persisted to `localStorage["runkit-terminal-font-size"]`. Effective = preference ?? device default — 11px mobile / 13px desktop via the shared `isMobileViewport()` rule (narrow-width OR coarse-pointer, NOT a CSS media query). Bounds 8–24px, step 1, exported as `TERMINAL_FONT_BOUNDS` (`{ min: 8, max: 24, step: 1 }`); all stored values clamped into `[8, 24]` on read. Helpers `clampTerminalFont`/`deviceDefaultFontSize`/`readTerminalFontSize` mirror the `fixedWidth`/`SIDEBAR_WIDTH_BOUNDS` shape.

**Mutators** (`ChromeDispatch`): `increaseTerminalFont` / `decreaseTerminalFont` step `effective ± step`, clamp, persist (try/catch noop); `resetTerminalFont` = **forget preference** (`localStorage.removeItem` → preference back to `null` → effective reverts to the device default), NOT a fixed value. First step from the unset (`null`) state operates on the device default and persists a concrete adjacent value (desktop 13 → 14).

**Applied to xterm** (`terminal-client.tsx`): `terminalFontSize` is passed as the `fontSize` option on construction (and to the `document.fonts.load` preloads); an effect keyed on it sets `term.options.fontSize = N` + calls `fitAddonRef.current?.fit()` on change (guarded against an uninitialized terminal). `fit()` recomputes rows×cols and **resizes the PTY** (the correct xterm resize path — reflows running TUIs, not a bitmap-scaled canvas). Because the value is global, **every** live `TerminalClient` reacts on change — including all mounted board-route panes. The mobile touch-scroll `LINE_HEIGHT` fallback reads `options.fontSize` live, so it tracks runtime changes.

**Control surfaces**: (1) the top-bar chevron menu's `−`/`+` stepper row plus the settings dialog's `[−] {size} [+]` + reset combo (see § Terminal font (Aa)), and (2) three command-palette actions — `terminal-font-increase` ("Increase terminal font"), `terminal-font-decrease` ("Decrease terminal font"), `terminal-font-reset` ("Reset terminal font"), no `shortcut`, wired to the dispatch fns. The palette actions are present on **both** the AppShell palette (`app.tsx`) and the board-route palette (`board-page.tsx`) — the setting is global and Constitution V requires the palette reachable on every route. This is the same dual-mount duplication `refresh-page` follows (§ Command Palette View actions).

**Deliberate non-decision**: `Cmd +/-` is **NOT** intercepted — browser-native whole-page zoom is retained (no global keybinding registered), avoiding the brittleness of hijacking `Cmd +/-` / `Cmd 0`.

### Terminal Font Bundling

The frontend bundles JetBrainsMono Nerd Font (patched single-file variant) as a webfont so terminal rendering is deterministic across all viewers, independent of which monospace fonts the browser happens to have installed. Without bundling, per-glyph font fallback for Nerd Font private-use-area codepoints produces visible baseline wobble within a single terminal row.

**Asset layout**: Three `.woff2` weights served from `/fonts/` — `JetBrainsMonoNerdFont-Regular.woff2` (400 normal), `-Bold.woff2` (700 normal), `-Italic.woff2` (400 italic). All three `@font-face` rules in `app/frontend/src/globals.css` expose the same `font-family: "JetBrainsMono Nerd Font"` name and MUST declare `font-display: block`. `swap` or `fallback` would let xterm measure cells against system-font metrics that persist as misalignment — xterm measures the character cell grid exactly once at `terminal.open()` and does not re-measure when a deferred font arrives. `index.html` includes a `<link rel="preload" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin href="/fonts/JetBrainsMonoNerdFont-Regular.woff2" />` to overlap the Regular download with JS parsing (only Regular is preloaded — Bold/Italic are a smaller fraction of the initial paint and don't justify the extra critical-path bytes).

**Load convention before `terminal.open()`**: The init routine in `app/frontend/src/components/terminal-client.tsx` awaits a concurrent `Promise.all([document.fonts.load(...), document.fonts.load(...), document.fonts.load(...)])` for all three weights at the exact `fontPx` (`isMobile ? 11 : 13`) the Terminal will use, BEFORE `new Terminal(...)` / `terminal.open()` / `fitAddon.fit()`. Three explicit `document.fonts.load(size, family)` calls (not `document.fonts.ready`) scope the await to exactly the weights xterm will request. A fresh `if (cancelled || !terminalRef.current) return;` guard MUST follow the await. With all six xterm imports static, this font-load `await Promise.all([...])` is the **only** async boundary in `init()`, so it is the sole place a post-await unmount guard is required before `new Terminal(...)`; one post-construction dispose guard sits before `setTerminalReady(true)`. (`260531-m3pl`)

**Primary `fontFamily`**: `'"JetBrainsMono Nerd Font", ui-monospace, monospace'` — bundled webfont first, `ui-monospace` as the system-default monospace, generic `monospace` as final guard against total load failure. The older long tail (`JetBrains Mono`, `Fira Code`, `SF Mono`, `Menlo`, `Monaco`, `Consolas`) is dead code once `font-display: block` plus a successful load makes the webfont always win; do not reintroduce it. Non-terminal monospace surfaces pick up the same font automatically via Tailwind's `--font-mono` custom property (webfont-first). (`260417-hyrl`)

**Test caveat**: jsdom does not implement the FontFaceSet API. `src/test-setup.ts` stubs a minimal `document.fonts.load()` / `document.fonts.ready` surface (same pattern as the existing `ResizeObserver` stub) so unit tests that mount `TerminalClient` do not hang on the await.

### Terminal Unicode Width Handling

xterm.js defaults to Unicode 6 width tables, which classify many modern glyphs (most emojis, several Misc Symbols codepoints) as 1 cell. Modern tmux lays out its buffer using wcwidth with a newer Unicode table (typically 14/15), treating the same glyphs as 2 cells. Without alignment, subsequent characters in a row drift between tmux's intended column and xterm's rendered column, producing visible ghost/overlap artifacts — especially with the WebGL renderer.

**Resolution** (`app/frontend/src/components/terminal-client.tsx`):
1. Construct the Terminal with `allowProposedApi: true` — required to access `terminal.unicode` (a proposed-API surface in xterm v6).
2. After `terminal.open()`, `loadAddon(new UnicodeGraphemesAddon())` (statically imported) and set `terminal.unicode.activeVersion = "15-graphemes"`.
3. Load order MUST precede the WebGL addon so the renderer initialises against the Unicode 15 table on first measure.

The `addon-unicode-graphemes` package (v6-era) covers Unicode 15 and grapheme clusters (ZWJ sequences, flag emoji, skin-tone modifiers). The `unicodeVersion` Terminal constructor option is a no-op past `"6"` without the addon — the addon is what registers the newer width table. (`260418-xgl2`)

### Command Palette Mobile Trigger

The `CommandPalette` component listens for a `palette:open` CustomEvent on `document` (in addition to `⌘K`). The `⋯` button in Line 1 dispatches this event on mobile. This is the mobile equivalent of `⌘K` — physical keyboards aren't available on phones.

### Keyboard-Navigable List Scroll Pattern

Both `CommandPalette` and `ThemeSelector` use the same scroll-into-view pattern for arrow key navigation: a `listRef` on the listbox container plus a `useEffect` on `[selectedIndex, open]` that queries `[aria-selected="true"]` and calls `scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest" })`. This ensures the selected item stays visible when navigating past the `max-h-64` scroll boundary. New keyboard-navigable list components SHOULD follow this pattern.

The sidebar session/window tree is a **third consumer** (see § Sidebar → Keyboard Navigation & Tree ARIA): a `treeRef` on the `role="tree"` container plus a `useEffect` on `[rovingKey]` that resolves the roving row (`[data-window-id]`/`[data-session-row]`) and calls `scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest" })` + `focus()`. It reuses the scroll-into-view half but deliberately diverges from the `selectedIndex` numeric-state model — the roving cursor is a row-key STRING (robust to the variable-length tree's expand/collapse and SSE row churn, where a numeric index can desync); the focus call is gated by `focusMovedRef` so passive SSE re-renders never scroll/steal focus.

## Keyboard Shortcuts

Every app chord is **data in one declarative registry** — `app/frontend/src/lib/keybindings.ts` (pure, DOM-light, colocated `keybindings.test.ts`, the `window-view.ts`/`palette-*.ts` convention). The module owns the tier definitions, `DEFAULT_BINDINGS` with its per-platform default refinements, the tier-stamped claimed-key map, the per-device override layer, chord matching (incl. scope precedence and the terminal-seam refusal predicate), conflict detection, capture, per-platform formatting, the shared suppression predicate, and the palette-hint decorator. React integration is two hooks: `hooks/use-keybindings.ts` (the reactive effective map) and `hooks/use-keybinding-dispatch.ts` (ONE window-level `keydown` listener per route shell). (`260730-g40a`)

### Tiers and the `e.code` rule

Three tiers, each a modifier predicate in `matchesCombo` — **Alt is excluded from every tier** (macOS composes characters with it):

| Tier | Chord | Used by |
|------|-------|---------|
| `shifted` | `Shift + (Meta or Ctrl)` — ⇧⌘ on macOS, ⇧Ctrl on Windows/Linux | the Windows/Linux action tier in full, and the mac actions that cannot demote (H/L/A) |
| `cmd` | `(Meta or Ctrl)` without Shift | the legacy punctuation chords ⌘K ⌘\ ⌘., the board ⌘[/⌘] pair, and the macOS-demoted action defaults (§ Per-platform default tiers) |
| `ctrl` | plain `Ctrl` without Meta/Shift | the `` Ctrl+` `` chat toggle (⌘` is macOS window cycling and must never be bound) |

Matching is on **`KeyboardEvent.code`, never `e.key`** — layout-independent (`"KeyN"`, `"BracketLeft"`), and it sidesteps the shifted-accelerator character-resolution flakiness the shell docs flag ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Keyboard-Tier Menu Seam). **The letters are constant across platforms; only the modifier varies.** The shifted tier is what makes N/T/W/H/L coexist on Windows/Linux at all: **plain Ctrl must always reach the pane** there (Ctrl+W delete-word, Ctrl+L clear, Ctrl+T fzf, Ctrl+N/P history), and legacy TTY encoding cannot distinguish Ctrl+Shift+letter from Ctrl+letter, so claiming the shifted tier costs terminal apps nothing (the same tier every cross-platform terminal — Windows Terminal, GNOME Terminal, Konsole, kitty — uses for app actions). That pane-ownership constraint is **Windows/Linux-only**, and browser key reservation is **host-specific** — so macOS defaults demote to the unshifted ⌘ tier wherever nothing immovable blocks them (§ Per-platform default tiers).

### The default binding set

Twelve starter actions on the letter/punctuation tier — `actionId` doubles as the palette action id wherever one exists. The letter is invariant; the modifier is per platform and per host. All are `scope: "global"` except `open-last-used`, which is `terminal`:

| Key | actionId | Action | macOS shell | macOS browser | Win/Linux |
|-----|----------|--------|-------------|---------------|-----------|
| N | `create-session` | New session | ⌘N | palette only (browser-reserved even shifted) | ⇧Ctrl+N |
| T | `create-window` | New window (tab-analog in the current session) | ⌘T | palette only | ⇧Ctrl+T |
| W | `kill-window` | Close window (the confirm flow) | ⌘W | palette only | ⇧Ctrl+W |
| E | `compose-toggle` | Toggle the docked compose strip (`ignoreInputs`) | ⇧⌘E | ⇧⌘E | ⇧Ctrl+E |
| O | `open-last-used` | Re-run the last-used Open target (`scope: "terminal"`) | ⇧⌘O | ⇧⌘O | ⇧Ctrl+O |
| H | `window-prev` | Previous window | ⇧⌘H | ⇧⌘H | ⇧Ctrl+H |
| L | `window-next` | Next window | ⇧⌘L | ⇧⌘L | ⇧Ctrl+L |
| [ | `go-back` | Router history back | ⌘[ | ⌘[ | ⇧Ctrl+[ |
| ] | `go-forward` | Router history forward | ⌘] | ⌘] | ⇧Ctrl+] |
| A | `agent-next-waiting` | Next waiting agent (§ Attention Surfacing) | ⇧⌘A | ⇧⌘A | ⇧Ctrl+A |
| / | `shortcuts-overlay` | Toggle the cheatsheet overlay (`ignoreInputs`) | ⌘/ | ⌘/ | ⇧Ctrl+/ |
| , | `settings-open` | Open the settings dialog (`ignoreInputs`) | ⌘, | ⇧⌘, (⌘, is browser Preferences) | ⇧Ctrl+, |

**`settings-open` is the one comma chord** (`260801-mqim`): a chrome-level *opener*, not a toggle — a re-fire while the dialog is open is a React state bailout, matching the macOS ⌘, convention where Preferences never closes itself. It is the third `macShellOnly` demotion alongside N/T/W, but for a different reason: ⌘, is the **browser's own Preferences accelerator** on macOS (claimed data, § Claimed keys), so the browser default stays shifted while the shell — which binds no ⌘, accelerator — promotes it to the OS-conventional ⌘,. `ignoreInputs: true` mirrors `shortcuts-overlay`/`compose-toggle`, the other chrome-level chords. `mapLabel: "settings"` is carried for parity but unrendered: `Comma` has no keycap cell in the overlay's tier-map grids, the `Period`/`Backslash`/`Backquote` precedent.

**E and O stay shifted on every platform** — no `macTier` demotion. ⌘E is macOS browser "use selection for find" territory, and the letter choice for the compose toggle is what the surrounding tier left free: C is the win/linux terminal copy/paste claim, T is `create-window`, I is win/linux DevTools. `compose-toggle` carries `ignoreInputs: true` so the chord also fires from *inside* the strip's own textarea (the one place the strip is most likely to be closed from) — the same field ⌘K and the overlay toggle use. `open-last-used` names the **behavior**, not an app, which is why it can carry a static chord at all while the per-target `Open:` palette entries cannot (§ Open-in-App palette actions). (`260801-sm6g`)

Six further bindings cover the established punctuation chords — same registry, so they are per-device rebindable like everything else: `command-palette` ⌘K (`cmd`, global, `ignoreInputs`), `sidebar-toggle` ⌘\ (`cmd`, global), `view-cycle` ⌘. (`cmd`, terminal), `chat-toggle` `` Ctrl+` `` (`ctrl`, terminal), and the board pane-cycle pair `board-cycle-prev`/`board-cycle-next` ⌘[/⌘] (`cmd`, board). `scope` (`global | terminal | board | sidebar`) is descriptive — it drives overlay grouping and conflict scoping; actual applicability is handler presence at each dispatcher mount. `sidebar` is schema-reserved with no binding. `kind` is `"builtin" | "macro"` — the builtins are the shipped registry actions above; `macro` bindings are user-defined and enter resolution from the macro store (see § Macro bindings). (`260730-hbyh`)

`Cmd+C`/`Ctrl+C` in a focused terminal is **not** a registry binding — it is a special case at the head of `attachCustomKeyEventHandler` (copy selection when one exists, else fall through to xterm's SIGINT), using `navigator.clipboard.writeText()` with a `document.execCommand('copy')` fallback for non-secure contexts and clearing the selection via `.finally()`.

No single-key shortcuts (`j`/`k`/`c`/`r`) or `Esc Esc` — these conflicted with xterm.js terminal input. Every action is also reachable via `Cmd+K` or top-bar buttons.

### Per-platform default tiers (`macTier` / `macShellOnly` / `defaultComboFor`)

The per-key table above is **data on the binding**, not a resolver branch. `KeyBinding` carries the base (Windows/Linux) `tier` plus two optional refinements: `macTier?: BindingTier` — the tier this binding's DEFAULT combo uses on mac hosts, code unchanged — and `macShellOnly?: boolean`, which restricts `macTier` to `isShell()` hosts. The shipped data is `macTier: "cmd"` on `go-back`/`go-forward`/`shortcuts-overlay`, and `macTier: "cmd", macShellOnly: true` on `create-session`/`create-window`/`kill-window`/`settings-open`; every other binding (including the legacy chords and macro projections) carries neither field and resolves identically everywhere.

`defaultComboFor(def, host)` is the **single seam** where platform and shell are consulted for defaults — it returns `{ code, macTier }` when `host.platform === "mac"` and `macTier` is set and (`!macShellOnly || host.shell`), else `{ code, tier }`. Both `resolveBindings` (the no-override fallback *and* the `isDefault` comparison) and `applyCapture` (own-default detection, which therefore takes a required `host`) read defaults through it, so an effective map is always host-coherent: on a mac shell host ⌘[ *is* `go-back`'s default and shows no modified dot, while the same combo on Win/Linux is a user override.

The **stored override shape is untouched** — `{ [actionId]: { code, tier } | null }`, byte-identical. Overrides are per-device by construction (localStorage), so per-platform storage is inherent and no migration exists. Win/Linux resolution is byte-identical to the pre-demotion map.

Which keys demote is constrained by what the environment refuses to give up:

- **⌘[ / ⌘] / ⌘/ demote on macOS in both hosts** — all three are interceptable in a mac browser (the board pane-cycle already ran on the cmd tier), and ⌘[/⌘] match native browser back/forward convention.
- **⌘N / ⌘T / ⌘W demote in the macOS shell only** — a browser reserves them unshifted *and* shifted (incognito / reopen-tab / close-window), so a mac browser keeps the shifted default and resolves it `reserved` (palette-only). The shell deliberately leaves the whole unshifted tier unbound, which is exactly what these spend ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Keyboard-Tier Menu Seam; ⌘W is unbound-by-design there for this).
- **⌘, demotes in the macOS shell only, for a narrower reason** — the browser claims only the **unshifted** ⌘, (Preferences), not the shifted tier, so `settings-open`'s ⇧⌘, browser default is live rather than `reserved` (unlike N/T/W, which are palette-only in a mac browser). The demotion is therefore about reaching the OS-conventional ⌘, where the accelerator is free, not about escaping a shifted-tier reservation.
- **H / L / A / E / O stay shifted on every platform** — ⌘H is macOS Hide (OS/app-menu level, dead in both hosts; the shell's App menu binds `role: hide`) and ⌘A is browser select-all plus the shell's Edit-menu `selectAll` role. H and L stay shifted **as a pair**: demoting L alone would split the H/L navigation pair across two tiers. ⌘E is mac-browser "use selection for find" territory.

### Claimed keys

Claimed keys are registry data (`claimedKeys(platform, shell)`), each carrying a **`tier`** (a claim occupies one chord tier — the mac ⌘ page tier has its own claimed set alongside the shifted tier) and an `owner` that drives presentation and effect:

- **`shell`** — menu accelerators. Shifted tier: ⇧CmdOrCtrl+R (force reload, everywhere), plus ⇧Ctrl+1–9 (server switcher) and ⇧Ctrl+I (DevTools) on **win/linux only** — `SHELL_SWITCHER_DIGITS` carries `platform: "other"`. The mac switcher sits on ⌥⌘1–9, which no tier can express (Alt is excluded from every tier), so on mac the shell owns no shifted digits at all; that unrepresentability is the point of the mac accelerator choice, not a gap in the mirror ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Keyboard-Tier Menu Seam). Mac `cmd` tier, present only inside the shell (`MAC_SHELL_CMD_CLAIMS`, mirroring the exhaustive mac bound set in `app/desktop/src/menu.ts`): ⌘Q quit, ⌘H hide, ⌘M minimize, ⌘R reload, the Edit roles ⌘Z/X/C/V/A, and zoom ⌘0/⌘+/⌘− (`Digit0`/`Equal`/`Minus`). Display + capture-warning data; the menu accelerator consumes the key before the page sees it anyway.
- **`system`** — shifted ⇧⌘Q (macOS logout), the mac ⇧⌘3/4/5 screenshot trio (`MAC_SCREENSHOT_CLAIMS`), and ⇧Ctrl+C/V (the win/linux terminal copy/paste convention), plus mac-browser `cmd`-tier ⌘Q/⌘H/⌘M (OS-level). Display-only. The mac rows carry `platform: "mac"` with no `shell` restriction — like logout, screenshots are system-wide, so they claim in both hosts. Mac ⇧⌘1/2/6–9 carry **no** claim: unclaimed page real estate.
- **`browser`** — the one owner that changes resolution. Shifted N/T/W (incognito / reopen-tab / close-window) outside the shell on every platform; on a mac browser also the `cmd` tier's ⌘N/⌘T/⌘W (new window / new tab / close tab), ⌘L (address bar), ⌘, (Preferences — the reason `settings-open`'s browser default stays shifted, `260801-mqim`), and tab digits ⌘1–9. Reservation is **tier-aware** — `resolveBindings` keys its reserved set on `` `${tier}:${code}` `` — so a combo resolves `enabled: false` with `disabledReason: "reserved"` only when a browser claim matches *both* its tier and its code. In a plain browser `create-session`/`create-window`/`kill-window` are therefore inert chords that stay palette-reachable, and an override onto ⌘L or ⌘, is dead on a mac browser but live in the mac shell (⌘, being the mac shell's shipped `settings-open` default there). Inside the shell every action is live.

Win/Linux claim sets are unchanged: the unshifted Ctrl tier there belongs to the pane, so it carries no claims data.

### Override layer

`localStorage["runkit-keybindings"]` stores **diffs only** — `{ [actionId]: { code, tier } | null }`, `null` meaning unbound/disabled (the same persistence shape as `runkit-terminal-font-size`; no backend, no config file, Constitution II). `resolveBindings(defaults, overrides, host)` merges them into the effective map — defaults resolved host-aware through `defaultComboFor` (§ Per-platform default tiers) — marking each binding `enabled` / `isDefault` / `disabledReason`. `parseOverrides` is tolerant — malformed JSON, a non-object root, or garbage entries degrade to "no override" — and both storage wrappers are try/catch, so an unavailable localStorage falls back to defaults without throwing.

This is also the **only** combo store: a macro's key lives here as an ordinary diff entry keyed by its actionId, never inside the macro definition. Macros therefore enter resolution as **keyless defaults** — `resolveBindings` resolves an effective combo whose `code` is `""` to `enabled: false, isDefault: false, disabledReason: "user"`, the same state a steal victim lands in, so the overlay's `unbound` affordance covers both. Builtins always carry a code and are unaffected. (`260730-hbyh`)

Rebinding is **steal-with-warning** in a single capture press: `applyCapture(bindings, overrides, actionId, combo, host, defaults?)` gives the combo to the new owner, sets the previous owner's override to `null`, and reports it as `stolenFrom` so the overlay flags it until it is rebound or reset. Re-capturing an action's own **host** default (`defaultComboFor(def, host)` — so ⌘[ counts for `go-back` on a mac) is a **no-op that short-circuits before the victim search**: it deletes the diff entry, keeping the blob diffs-only, and steals from nobody. The ordering is load-bearing — the mac defaults ship global↔board shadow pairs on ⌘[/⌘], so a victim search running first would let a no-op re-capture of either partner unbind the other. The optional `defaults` parameter is what the own-default check reads, so callers holding a **macro-extended** default set (`use-keybindings.ts`, which also passes its `host`) get the same detection as the `DEFAULT_BINDINGS` fallback. Stealing is kind-blind: a builtin capture can unbind a macro and a macro capture can unbind a builtin, one mechanism in both directions. Reactivity is an in-module pub/sub in `use-keybindings.ts` (the `use-local-storage-enum.ts` pattern — the native `storage` event fires only across tabs, which covers cross-tab for free); every write funnels through one `persist()`, and `setBinding` recomputes against freshly-read storage — **both** stores, so a macro-owned combo is steal-detected — meaning rapid successive captures compose.

`useKeybindings` composes `useMacros` and resolves over `DEFAULT_BINDINGS ∪ macros.map(macroToBinding)`. Because macros ride that one effective map, the dispatcher, the terminal seam (`attachCustomKeyEventHandler`), palette hints (`withShortcutHints`), and the overlay key map (`custom` keycap state) pick them up with **zero** consumer changes. A macro-store change also re-reads override storage, since `removeMacro` drops the deleted macro's diff without a keybinding-store notification and the two stores must not drift in-memory. `reset all` writes `{}`, which unbinds every macro combo while leaving the definitions intact — rows return to `unbound`. (`260730-hbyh`)

### Macro bindings

A **macro** is a user-named keyboard route to an action that already exists — never a shell string. `app/frontend/src/lib/macros.ts` (pure, DOM-light, colocated `macros.test.ts`) owns the model; `hooks/use-macros.ts` is its React integration. (`260730-hbyh`)

```ts
type MacroTarget =
  | { type: "riff"; preset: string }              // → POST /api/riff
  | { type: "palette"; paletteActionId: string }; // → the palette action body
type MacroAction = { actionId: string; kind: "macro"; label: string; target: MacroTarget };
```

- **Two target kinds only.** A riff target carries the preset **NAME** alone; the pane commands and arguments live in the preset definition inside `fab/project/config.yaml` — the committed-config trust boundary ([rk-riff](/run-kit/rk-riff.md) § Security / Trust Boundary). Riff targets carry **no `args` passthrough**: `POST /api/riff` has no args field and its `task` REPLACES preset panes (`composePanes`), so per-macro dynamic args would need a backend extension. `isMacroTarget` narrows structurally, so a `{type:"shell",cmd}` blob planted in localStorage is dropped on parse.
- **Definitions in `localStorage["runkit-macros"]`** — a JSON array with the tolerant-parse posture of `parseOverrides` (malformed JSON, a non-array root, and invalid entries degrade to dropped/empty; valid entries are re-projected so unknown keys are stripped and `kind` is pinned) and try/catch storage wrappers. An empty list removes the key. The combo lives in `runkit-keybindings` (§ Override layer).
- **`actionId` is `"macro:<label-slug>"`** — lowercase, non-alphanumeric runs collapse to `-`, `-2`/`-3`… suffixes on collision (the riff `resolveWindowName` convention), empty slug falls back to `macro`. It doubles as the palette action id, so the `withShortcutHints` join is free. `isMacroActionId` is the guard that keeps macros out of target lists and out of palette-target resolution — no macro→macro recursion.
- **`macroToBinding(macro)`** projects a macro into a `KeyBinding`: `kind: "macro"`, `scope: "global"`, `tier: "shifted"`, `code: ""` (keyless — the unbound resolution in § Override layer), `description`/`mapLabel` from `macroCommandPreview(target)`, which renders `rk riff --preset discuss` / `palette: {actionId}`.
- **`useMacros()`** returns `{ macros, addMacro(label, target) → actionId, removeMacro(actionId) }` with the same in-module pub/sub + native `storage` reactivity as `use-keybindings.ts`; `addMacro` uniquifies against freshly-read storage so rapid adds compose. `removeMacro` also drops the actionId's `runkit-keybindings` entry, so a later same-slug macro can never inherit a ghost combo.

**Execution** is `executeMacro` in `app.tsx`, mounted on AppShell only. A `palette` target looks its action body up by id in the render-updated `paletteActionsRef` (the § Design Decisions "Chord handlers are the palette action bodies" convention) and invokes it in place; an absent action means no handler, so the chord falls through untouched. A `riff` target is **session-gated** (the `Agent: Spawn` precedent) and calls the existing `spawnRiff(server, session, { preset })` client: success surfaces an info toast and navigates via `navigateToWindow` when `windowId` is truthy (the spawn dialog's falsy-id guard preserved); failure — including a 400 for a preset gone from fabconfig — surfaces the error text as a toast. No new endpoint, no new exec surface, no fire-and-forget. `keybindingHandlers` spreads the macro handlers **first** so a theoretical id collision resolves builtin-first. The board route mounts no macro handlers, so macro chords fall through there.

Macros are also palette-reachable without their key: one `Macro: {label}` entry per macro (id = the macro's actionId) folded into `paletteActions`, riff macros filtered out with no session. `withShortcutHints` decorates them automatically; an unbound macro renders no hint.

`findConflicts` is a pure function over the effective map — two **enabled** bindings conflict when their codes match, their tiers collide (`tiersCollide`: equal, or the overlapping `cmd`/`ctrl` pair), and their scopes are **EQUAL**. A same-combo global↔scoped pair is a **shadow, not a conflict** (§ Scope precedence). It is the **test-only invariant guard** asserting the shipped defaults are conflict-free in every host — including the mac hosts, whose defaults ship exactly one such shadow shape; the capture UI takes its steal warning from `applyCapture`'s `stolenFrom` instead, and that search stays `scopesOverlap`-wide (`global` overlaps everything, equal scopes overlap, `terminal`/`board` are disjoint since those routes never co-mount).

### Dispatch seams

- **`useKeybindingDispatch(handlers)`** — one window-level bubble-phase `keydown` per route shell, keyed by `actionId`. Rules in order: skip `defaultPrevented` events (the overlay's capture-phase rebind listener claims its own); walk the **enabled** matches in `findMatches` order (§ Scope precedence) and fire the **first one that has a handler at this mount**; when **no** match has a handler the chord falls through untouched (no `preventDefault`), so a route without context for an action leaves browser/pane behavior alone; then `shouldSuppressChord` gates real text inputs unless the binding sets `ignoreInputs`. Bindings and handlers live in refs so the listener registers once per mount and SSE ticks never churn it.
- **`shouldSuppressChord(target)`** — the single shared input-gating predicate (`lib/keybindings.ts`): suppress only when a real text input owns focus, with two carve-outs — anything inside `.xterm` (the hidden helper textarea is the terminal's normal focus state) and `.rk-chat-input` (the chat lens's analog, which the `` Ctrl+` `` toggle must escape). jsdom-safe (`contentEditable` attribute fallback for the missing `isContentEditable` getter). ⌘K and the overlay toggle carry `ignoreInputs` and skip it entirely.
- **AppShell (`app.tsx`)** mounts the dispatcher with handlers derived from the **palette action bodies** — `fromPalette(id)` looks each up by id for `create-session`, `create-window`, `kill-window`, `agent-next-waiting`, `go-back`, `go-forward`, `settings-open` (the existing `Settings: Open` entry's body) — plus `window-prev`/`window-next` (a modulo cycle over the current session's windows in sidebar order, wrapping, through the rich `navigateToWindow` path; gated by `canCycle`, so the chord no-ops off a window route), the `shortcuts-overlay` toggle, `compose-toggle` (chrome-context's `toggleComposeStrip`, the same body the `>_` chip and `View: Text Input` run), `open-last-used` (registered only when `windowParam` is set — § Open-in-App palette actions), and one handler per stored macro (§ Macro bindings).
- **BoardPage (`board-page.tsx`)** mounts its own dispatcher with `board-cycle-next`/`board-cycle-prev` (both handlers are absent when the board has no panes, so the chords fall through untouched), `go-back`/`go-forward`, `shortcuts-overlay`, `compose-toggle` (the board twin mounts the same chrome-context body — the compose strip has a board footer mount too), and `settings-open` (`useSettingsDialog().openSettings` lifted above the handler memo for the dep — the board route renders no AppShell, so a handler registered only there would leave the chord dead here). `open-last-used` is deliberately unhandled here: the Open control is terminal-route-only, so the chord falls through. The two route shells never co-mount.
- **Four chords keep their own component-local listeners** rather than joining the dispatcher, because their enablement is local state: ⌘K in `command-palette.tsx` (palette open-state), ⌘\ in `shell.tsx`'s `useSidebarKeyboardToggle`, ⌘. in `app.tsx`'s view-cycle effect (lens availability), and `` Ctrl+` `` in `use-chat-view-shortcut.ts` (its chat-enablement gate). Each reads its binding through `byAction` and matches with `matchesCombo` + `shouldSuppressChord` — definition and suppression are the registry's, enablement is the seam's.
- **Terminal seam** — `terminal-client.tsx`'s `attachCustomKeyEventHandler` keeps the ⌘C copy case first, then delegates the refusal decision to the pure predicate `shouldRefuseTerminalChord(e, bindings, platform)`, reading both the bindings and the host platform through render-updated refs (the handler attaches once at terminal init, but overrides can change any time). Returning `false` means xterm neither handles nor transmits the key, and xterm does not `preventDefault` a refused key, so the event bubbles to the window dispatcher and the action fires while the terminal owns focus. Two refusal rules:
  - **Any enabled shifted-tier match, on every platform** — legacy TTY encoding cannot distinguish Ctrl+Shift+letter from Ctrl+letter, so xterm would emit the Ctrl-char; refusing costs the pane nothing.
  - **On macOS only, an enabled `cmd`-tier match pressed with `metaKey`** — ⌘ chords never reach the pane as control bytes, so refusal is loss-free and the demoted ⌘[/⌘]/⌘/ (plus shell-host ⌘N/T/W) fire under terminal focus. The `metaKey` gate is load-bearing: `matchesCombo`'s cmd tier also accepts plain Ctrl, and mac Ctrl+[ is ESC.
  **Plain-Ctrl chords are never refused on any platform** (Ctrl+W delete-word, Ctrl+L clear, Ctrl+T fzf, Ctrl+[ ESC). On Win/Linux the cmd rule never applies at all — cmd-tier combos *are* plain-Ctrl chords there — so the seam is byte-identical to its pre-demotion behavior.

The chords are SPA-renderer bindings, **not Electron menu accelerators** — the shell's documented seam is accelerator *avoidance*, so the letters work identically in shell and browser hosts; only which modifier is bound varies (§ Per-platform default tiers).

### Scope precedence — scoped beats global

`findMatches(e, bindings)` returns **every** enabled matching binding, ordered non-`global` scopes first (registry order within each class). This exists because the mac demotion makes two shipped defaults share a combo by design: the board pane-cycle pair `board-cycle-prev`/`board-cycle-next` (`scope: "board"`) and the global `go-back`/`go-forward` both sit on ⌘[/⌘] on mac hosts.

Resolution is **precedence, not conflict**: the board route keeps pane-cycle on ⌘[/⌘] (back/forward stays palette-reachable there), every other route gets history navigation, and a *paneless* board — where the cycle handlers are absent — falls through to the global back/forward at the same mount, because the dispatcher's walk skips matches with no handler. `findConflicts` therefore flags only **equal**-scope pairs, while `applyCapture`'s steal search deliberately stays `scopesOverlap`-wide (§ Override layer, § Design Decisions).

### Shortcuts overlay (`components/shortcuts-overlay.tsx`)

The cheatsheet is **THE single shortcuts surface** — app chords, user macros, and tmux keybindings in one sheet. It is a focus-trapped **dialog, not a route** (Constitution IV fixes the route set), opened by three affordances that all TOGGLE it: the `shortcuts-overlay` chord (⌘/ on mac hosts, ⇧Ctrl+/ on Win/Linux), the `Help: Keyboard Shortcuts` palette action, and the sidebar-footer Keyboard icon (via the `shortcuts-overlay:open` document CustomEvent — § Sidebar footer global-chrome row). Mounted on both AppShell and BoardPage (the § Design Decisions → "Palette actions are duplicated across the two mounts" convention). It uses `useFocusTrap`, the bracket-heading vocabulary, `rk-glint` buttons, accent-green states, and theme tokens; `data-testid="shortcuts-overlay"`. Rows, keycap states, and capture all read the **host-effective** map, so the per-platform tiers surface without per-consumer branching.

**No tabs — one scroll, one filter.** The core query is "what's the key for X?", and the user should not have to know which system owns X before asking. So a single filter input spans app rows + macro rows + tmux rows, and everything lives in one scroll container. Navigation is a **sticky jump-nav chip row** under the header instead (`<nav aria-label="Shortcut sections">`, `data-testid="shortcuts-jump-nav"`): `key map · global · terminal · board · custom · tmux`, each chip a `scrollIntoView` anchor onto a section ref. While a filter is active each chip additionally renders its **live match count** and dims (`opacity-40`) at zero hits, so matches below the fold stay discoverable. The `custom` chip renders only when that section does; the `key map` chip carries no count and dims while filtering (the map hides then). Contents in document order:
