- **ONE keycap grid with a modifier picker**, **foldable** via a `▾ collapse map` / `▸ expand map` toggle and **auto-hidden entirely while a filter is active** (a keycap grid cannot answer a text query, and the reclaimed vertical space keeps row hits above the fold on short viewports). The **tables are the authority** for effective chords; the map is a **discovery + rebind tool** — which is why one grid answers "what does my keyboard do while I hold these modifiers" rather than two grids re-answering per-action chords the rows already carry. The grid is three rows of keycaps where a key renders `bound` (accent-green, host default combo), `custom` (accent, overridden), `claimed` (amber, dimmed — claimed wins over bound so a browser-reserved N reads claimed) or `free`, annotated with the binding's `mapLabel`, all via one shared `keyCell(states, code, idx)` over a per-layer state map. The map stays **app-layers-only**: tmux prefix chords are sequences, not combos, and do not fit the model. The digit run collapses into a **decorative `…` ellipsis cell** standing for Digit3–Digit8, which takes its claimed styling from **`Digit3`** — the one digit carrying a claim on *both* displays (switcher-claimed on win/linux, screenshot-claimed on mac). Keying it off the run's first member is what keeps mid-run claims visible on the mac display, where `Digit1`/`Digit2`/`Digit9` have their own unclaimed cells.
  - **The picker IS the map label**: the header's left slot reads **"Holding"** followed by a segmented control (`role="group"`, `aria-label="Keyboard map modifier"`, `aria-pressed` per segment, the platform-toggle visual idiom) whose selected segment supplies the modifier caps — `⇧ ⌘` | `⌘`. Selecting a segment swaps which layer the single grid renders, feeding `tierKeyStates(activeMapTier)`.
  - **The `⌘` option is a macOS-display affordance only** — the Win/Linux unshifted layer is plain Ctrl and belongs to the pane, so that display renders a static **"Holding Shift Ctrl"** label instead (a one-option segmented control is dead chrome). Default selection is `⇧⌘`. The active layer is **derived** (`displayPlatform === "mac" ? mapTier : "shifted"`), so switching the display away from macOS falls back to the shifted layer while the `⌘` selection survives and reapplies on return.
  - **Session-scoped view state**, like the fold: `mapTier` and `mapFolded` are both deliberately excluded from the on-close reset effect — a chosen layer and a folded map are reading preferences, unlike the transient filter/capture state the close effect clears.
  - The map header also carries the **"plain Ctrl always reaches the pane"** note (it explains why there is no Ctrl layer to pick) and the fold toggle, all on one row.
  - **Legend** (four states): `bound` · `custom` · `claimed — taken by the OS / browser / app menu (the desktop app frees the browser ones)` · `free`. The claimed entry is where the browser-vs-desktop story lives in the map — one line, no per-map captions.
- **Platform display toggle** (macOS ↔ Win·Linux) — a **view** control initialized from the detected host: it restyles keycaps and gates the `⌘` picker option, never what this host must actually press. Capture and the header chord always read the physical `host.platform`; claim lookups combine the *display* platform with the *physical* host's `shell` flag.
- **Header chord hint** — `{chord} toggles this sheet` derives from the effective map (`byAction.get("shortcuts-overlay")`) formatted for the physical host, so it reads ⌘/ on a mac and reflects overrides; it is **hidden entirely** when that binding is unbound or disabled rather than advertising a dead chord.
- **Filter input** over label + description, and a per-scope **grouped row list** (GLOBAL / TERMINAL / BOARD) with `ScopeBadge` pills on non-global rows.
- **Shell-owned locked rows are a subgroup at the END of GLOBAL** (a `SubHead` reading "Shell-owned — accelerators live in the desktop shell menu"), not a top-level section — three flavors of locked top-level section was one too many once tmux arrived. Same 🔒 non-interactive idiom (accelerators are edited in the shell menu; the DevTools row is win/linux only), and the rows still count toward the `global` jump chip. Locked rows are **free-form caps arrays**, not tier-constrained, which is what lets the `Switch to server 1–9` row render `⌥ ⌘ 1…9` on the mac display — diverging from the shared `tierCaps` helper that still gives `Force reload` ⇧⌘R — while win/linux keeps `Shift Ctrl 1…9` on both rows. No registry or tier change backs the divergence, because the mac switcher accelerator lives outside every tier.
- **Click-to-rebind capture** — arming a row installs a capture-phase window listener that `preventDefault`+`stopPropagation`s (keeping the chord away from the focus trap, the dispatcher, and the legacy listeners); Esc cancels without persisting; a modifier-only or tier-less press keeps capturing. After a successful capture the row shows a steal warning naming the victim, or a claimed-key warning when the combo is spoken for — the claim lookup is **tier-aware** (`c.tier === combo.tier && c.code === combo.code`), so capturing ⌘R inside the mac shell warns that the shell owns it while ⇧⌘R warns separately.
- **Host-divergent rows carry the fact themselves** — a `desktop` pill (ScopeBadge/reserved-pill idiom) plus the OTHER host's chord as secondary text: `in browser: ⇧⌘T` inside the shell, `in desktop app: ⌘T` in a mac browser (there alongside the amber `browser` reserved pill). The gate is `host.platform === "mac" && b.isDefault && b.macTier != null && b.macShellOnly === true`, so exactly the `create-session`/`create-window`/`kill-window` trio qualifies — the only `macTier`+`macShellOnly` set in `DEFAULT_BINDINGS`. It is a **physical-host** fact, never the display toggle, and only at the host default: an override applies verbatim on both hosts and an unbound row has no chord, so either collapses the divergence and drops the badge. Host-invariant rows and win/linux hosts never render it. Most of the ⌘ layer (⌘K, ⌘., ⌘[/⌘], ⌘/) works in a browser tab too, so the layer as a whole is **not** labelled desktop-only. Derivation reuses existing seams: `macTier`/`macShellOnly` ride onto `EffectiveBinding` through `resolveBindings`'s spread, and the other-host chord is `defaultComboFor(def, { platform: "mac", shell: !host.shell })` over the base def read from `DEFAULT_BINDINGS` (the effective row's `tier` is overwritten by resolution, so the base def is the correct input). No registry schema change.
- **Per-row modified dot + ↺ reset**, an `unbound` button on user-disabled rows, a `browser` badge on reserved rows, and a footer with the localStorage note + **reset all**.
- **`[ CUSTOM ]` section** (`data-testid="macro-section"`) — the editable macro tier (§ Macro bindings). One row per macro: the label, a monospace `macroCommandPreview` chip, the same click-to-rebind combo button as a builtin row (an unbound macro shows the amber `unbound` button; a browser-reserved combo shows the `browser` badge), and a `✕` delete that drops the definition **and** its combo diff. A riff macro whose preset is absent from the fetched preset list renders a red `missing preset` badge — no silent fallback. The grouped GLOBAL/TERMINAL/BOARD lists filter macros out (`b.kind !== "macro"`), so each macro renders exactly once; the filter input searches macro labels and previews too.
- **`+ bind a key to a palette action or riff preset…`** — an inline add flow: a searchable target list (riff presets + the mount's palette actions, macros excluded), a name input pre-filled from the picked target's label, and `add + capture key`, which creates the macro and immediately arms capture on the fresh row — naming and binding in one gesture. Target data arrives via optional props `paletteTargets` / `riffPresetNames`; **`paletteTargets == null` gates the whole add flow off**, so a mount that passes none (the board route) renders the CUSTOM rows read/rebind/delete-only. `riffPresetNames == null` means "preset list unknown" — no badges, no riff targets offered.

- **`[ TMUX ]` section** (last, `data-testid="tmux-section"`) — the pane's own keybindings, **read-only**. tmux keys are pressed inside the pane and configured in `configs/tmux/default.conf`; run-kit only documents them, so every row is the same 🔒 locked, non-interactive idiom as the shell-owned subgroup (no capture, no reset, no rebind). Data is the existing curated `getKeybindings(currentServer)` client API (`app/backend/api/keybindings.go`'s whitelist — a read-only dependency, unchanged). Two `SubHead` groups: **Direct** for root-table rows (F2/F3/F4 new/prev/next window, Shift+F3/F4 prev/next pane, Shift+F7 copy mode) and **Prefix — Ctrl+S, then key** for prefix-table rows, rendered as a keycap **sequence** (`Ctrl` `S` *then* `\`) rather than a simultaneous combo. Both subheads hide while filtering (the group labels are navigation, not matches). Key-name formatting is `S-` → `Shift+`, `C-` → `Ctrl+`.

  The server comes from `useSessionContext().currentServer` read **inside** the overlay, and the fetch runs only while the overlay is open, cancel-flagged on close/server change. `null` bindings means loading; `[]` means no current server (board/host routes), an empty response, or a failed fetch — all three collapse to the one-line **"No tmux server running"** empty state, so there is no spinner deadlock and no crash path.

`riffPresetNames` comes from a best-effort `getRiffPresets(server, session)` fetch in `app.tsx` that runs only **while the overlay is open on a route with a session**, mapped to names; a failed fetch leaves it `null`. Detection is therefore advisory — `POST /api/riff` validates authoritatively at execution time either way (§ Macro bindings), so a macro is never silently inert.

Export/import of overrides is not offered.

### Palette hints

`withShortcutHints(actions, byAction, platform)` (pure, in `lib/keybindings.ts`) decorates a palette action array with each action's **effective** combo as its `shortcut` hint — `actionId` doubles as the palette id, so the join is free. It is applied to `paletteActions` in `app.tsx` and the board palette in `board-page.tsx`. Formatting is per platform via `formatCombo`/`comboParts`: mac symbol tiers join bare (`⇧⌘N`, `⌘.`), other platforms join with `+` (`Shift+Ctrl+N`), and the `ctrl` tier keeps the `` Ctrl+` `` spelling on both. Hints reflect overrides. A **disabled** binding (user-disabled or browser-reserved) contributes NO hint — advertising a dead chord would lie — while the palette entry itself stays reachable. Actions with no registered binding pass through untouched, so hand-set hints survive; `buildViewActions` (`lib/palette-view.ts`) takes the effective cycle/chat hint strings as parameters rather than hardcoding them.

The **page tier** — the unshifted `⌘<key>` tier on macOS, unshifted `Ctrl+<key>` on Windows/Linux — is left entirely unbound by the Electron desktop shell on every platform it packages for ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Keyboard-Tier Menu Seam). The SPA spends part of that budget on **macOS**: ⌘[/⌘]/⌘/ in both mac hosts and ⌘N/⌘T/⌘W inside the mac shell (§ Per-platform default tiers), alongside the legacy ⌘K/⌘\/⌘. chords that always lived there. Host-conditional demotion is the `isShell()` gate in `defaultComboFor` (`lib/shell.ts`, the `window.runkitShell` detection seam). Whatever the SPA does not take stays the browser's or the OS's: mac-browser ⌘N/T/W/L and ⌘1–9 are claimed data, and on **Windows/Linux the page tier remains untouched by the SPA** — plain Ctrl is the pane's.

### e2e — `shortcut-registry.spec.ts` (+ `.spec.md`)

A mocked-backend spec covering ⇧Ctrl+H/L window cycling, ⇧Ctrl+[ back navigation, ⇧Ctrl+/ overlay open/filter/Esc-close, the `Help: Keyboard Shortcuts` palette entry, click-to-capture rebind persisting the localStorage diff, palette hint rendering, and browser-reserved N inertness. Two cases cover the merged surface: the jump-nav chips + TMUX locked rows (fed by a mocked `GET /api/keybindings*`, prefix rows asserted on their `then` sequence separator) with one filter driving live per-chip counts across app and tmux rows, and the absence of a `Help: tmux Keybindings` palette entry. A **mac block** spoofs the platform with an init-script getter override on `Navigator.prototype.platform` (`"MacIntel"`) so `detectPlatform()` resolves `mac` and the demoted defaults apply: ⌘[/⌘] retrace history (with ⇧Ctrl+[ proven inert), ⌘/ toggles the overlay and the map's modifier picker offers the ⌘ layer (the `Keyboard map modifier` group; ⌘ starts `aria-pressed="false"` — ⇧⌘ is the default — and clicking it selects the layer, asserted by the mac-browser `address bar` claimed cell), and both ⌘N and ⇧⌘N stay inert in a mac *browser* host. Deep mac paths — shell-host N/T/W demotion, the claimed sets, the refusal predicate — are unit territory in `lib/keybindings.test.ts`, since e2e runs on Linux.

### e2e — `macro-riff-bindings.spec.ts` (+ `.spec.md`)

A second mocked-backend spec for the macro tier (`260730-hbyh`): the overlay add-flow end-to-end (pick `riff: discuss` from the mocked `GET /api/riff/presets*` preflight → name → `add + capture key` → ⇧Ctrl+D) asserting both localStorage stores and the preview chip, then the chord POSTing `{session, preset}` — **preset name only** — to a mocked `POST /api/riff*` with a toast + navigation to the spawned window; the `Macro: {label}` palette entry rendering its `Shift+Ctrl+D` hint and executing the same path; and a seeded macro targeting a preset the preflight does not define showing the `missing preset` badge with the spawn's 400 surfacing as an error toast and no navigation. The spawn window `@9` sits in the static `/ws/state` snapshot from the start so post-spawn navigation confirms rather than tripping the switch-confirm watchdog. Both route globs carry the trailing `*` the `?server=`/`?session=` queries need, and the spawn glob `**/api/riff?*` cannot match `/api/riff/presets` (glob `?`/`*` never match `/`).

### Command Palette Actions

**Shell server-switch palette entries** (`shellServerActions`, the first shell-gated palette block): one `Server: Switch to "<name>"` action per **shell**-registered rk server, active one suffixed ` (current)`, ids `shell-switch-server-<uuid>`. The **quoted** name is what distinguishes these from the unquoted tmux `Server: Switch to {name}` entries — a shell server is a whole rk instance addressed by URL, a tmux server is a socket on the current host. Composition is the pure builder `buildShellServerActions(servers, onSwitch)` (`lib/palette-shell.ts` + colocated vitest, following the `palette-view.ts`/`palette-pin.ts` convention); `app.tsx` feeds it `useShellServers()` (`hooks/use-shell-servers.ts` — one `listShellServers()` fetch per mount, resolving `[]` in a plain browser so no caller needs an `isShell()` pre-check) and folds the memo into `paletteActions` right after `serverActions`. Selecting an entry hands off to `switchShellServer(id)`: the **shell** then loads that server's URL — a full page swap, so no SPA-side navigation follows — and a denied or failed bridge call surfaces as an error toast. AppShell-only (not dual-mounted into `board-page.tsx`), mirroring where the tmux `serverActions` live. The mount-time snapshot has one known staleness window: removing a non-active server in the shell's native menu leaves its palette entry until the next page load. Scope is **switch-only** — Add/Remove Server stay in the shell's native menu + welcome flow. The keyboard paths to the same operation are the shell-side switcher accelerators (⌥⌘1–9 on mac, ⇧Ctrl+1–9 on win/linux) and these palette entries (Constitution V). (`260730-9lez`)

Command palette actions include: create/rename/kill session, create/rename/kill window, move up/down for the current server/session/window (`260705-bpnr` — see § Command Palette Move up/down), theme switching, "Reload tmux config" (targets the active server via `?server=` param), "Create tmux server" (opens name dialog, creates session "0" in $HOME), "Kill tmux server" (confirmation dialog, kills active server, switches to next available — the dialog carries a daemon-specific warning when the target is `rk-daemon`; see § Infra-server de-emphasis → kill guard), "Switch tmux server: {name}" (one entry per available server, current marked), `Server: Switch to "{name}"` (one **quoted** entry per desktop-shell-registered rk server, active marked — present only inside the shell; see § Shell server-switch palette entries above), `Help: Keyboard Shortcuts` (id `shortcuts-overlay` — toggles the registry-driven cheatsheet overlay, THE single shortcuts surface incl. its read-only TMUX section; dual-mounted on AppShell and the board route), "Copy: tmux Commands" (opens tmux commands dialog — only visible on terminal route when `currentWindow` is available), "Notifications: Enable push" / "Notifications: Enabled ✓" (the Web Push opt-in gesture — terminal-themed label, NOT a bell; see § Notifications (Web Push opt-in)), `Macro: {label}` (one per user-defined macro, id = the macro's actionId, hint = its effective combo; riff macros session-gated — see § Keyboard Shortcuts → Macro bindings), and terminal navigation (jump to any session/window).

**Session/window creation actions in the palette**:
| Action ID | Label | Behavior |
|-----------|-------|----------|
| `create-session` | "Session: Create" | Instant creation — no dialog (see Instant Session Creation) |
| `create-session-at-folder` | "Session: Create at Folder" | Opens `CreateSessionDialog` pre-filled with `currentWindow.worktreePath`; empty if no active window |
| `create-window` | "Window: Create" | Instant window creation — the sidebar `+ New Window` path. Creates an **unnamed** window (`createWindow` called with no name), so tmux auto-names it to the active window's folder basename; the optimistic ghost is labeled with the **raw** cwd basename (see § Unnamed `+ New Window`) (`260707-j66b`) |
| `create-window-at-folder` | "Window: Create at Folder" | Opens `CreateSessionDialog` in `mode="window"` (dialog title changes, session name input hidden, confirms via `createWindow(server, session, undefined, cwd)` — no name, so tmux auto-names to the folder basename); only shown when a session is active (`260707-j66b`) |
| `create-iframe-window` | "Window: New Iframe Window" | Opens dialog with name + URL inputs; creates iframe window via `createWindow(server, session, name, undefined, "iframe", url)`; only shown when a session is active. `@rk_type=iframe` seeds "default view = web" (§ Window Views (Lens Model)) (`260714-t97o`) |

**Window rename action**: the `rename-window` action ("Window: Rename") dispatches a `window-heading:rename` `CustomEvent` (guarded on `currentWindow`), which the centered top-bar `WindowHeading` listens for and enters inline edit (mirrors `theme-selector:open`), rather than opening a modal dialog. The action stays registered (Constitution V keyboard path); there is no window rename `<Dialog>` (the SESSION rename dialog is separate). See § Window Heading. (`260703-5ilm`)

**Window move actions**: "Window: Move up" (id `window-move-up`) and "Window: Move down" (id `window-move-down`) in the `windowActions` group — the sole window-move pair, matching the up/down vocabulary of the Session / Server / Board move actions (windows render as vertical sidebar rows). Only shown when `currentWindow` exists. "Move up" excluded when the current window is at the minimum index in the session; "Move down" excluded when at the maximum index (boundary exclusion, not disabled state). On select, calls `moveWindow(server, currentWindow.windowId, targetIndex)` (source by stable window ID, destination by position) then navigates to the **same** `currentWindow.windowId` with `search: (prev) => prev` — the window keeps its ID across the reorder (only its index changes) and the current view is preserved (`260714-r7rq`), so the user stays on their window and view after the swap. The target index is computed via the shared pure helper `computeWindowMoveTarget(index, delta, minIndex, maxIndex)` (returns `null` at a boundary — no wraparound). (`260705-bpnr`, `260719-p8pv-window-move-palette-consolidation`)

**Command Palette Move up/down (server / session / window)**: a keyboard/touch reorder path parallel to the two drag surfaces, giving all THREE hierarchy levels a keyboard-reachable reorder (Constitution V). Six `PaletteAction`s, each acting on the **current** route-context entity (the `Session: Move` / `Window: Move` current-entity semantics), grouped with their existing families in `app.tsx`, all with boundary = **hidden** (the entry is filtered out at the top/bottom of its list — no wraparound, no disabled state): (`260705-bpnr`)

- **`Server: Move up` / `Server: Move down`** (`server-move-up`/`-down`, `serverActions`) — move the current server one position within the REGULAR class (infra excluded). The new full regular-class order is computed and POSTed via `setServerOrder` (server-global `event: server-order` echo re-sorts every client). Hidden when the current server is infra (`currentRegularIdx === -1`) or at a class boundary.
- **`Session: Move up` / `Session: Move down`** (`session-move-up`/`-down`, `sessionActions`) — move the current session one position in the **effective** session order (`deriveEffectiveSessionOrder(liveNames, sseOrder)` — persisted `@rk_session_order` filtered to live names, un-ordered live sessions appended in natural order; the sidebar's transient drag override is component-local and NOT visible to the palette). Persisted via the EXISTING `setSessionOrder(server, order)` — the same primitive the sidebar session drag uses; no new backend.
- **`Window: Move up` / `Window: Move down`** (`window-move-up`/`-down`, `windowActions`) — move the current window ±1 index via `moveWindow(server, windowId, targetIndex)`, then navigate to the same stable window ID with `search: (prev) => prev` (view-preserving). The sole window-move pair. See § Window move actions above.

**`Board: Move up` / `Board: Move down`** (`board-move-up`/`-down`) — the same boundary-hidden / no-wraparound keyboard+touch path for the board list, mounted in `board-page.tsx`'s `boardRouteActions` (NOT `app.tsx`), because the board route does not render AppShell and "current board" only has meaning on a `/board/$name` route (the board owns its own palette). Each acts on the CURRENT board (the `/board/$name` param), computes the full new order via `computeMoveOrder(boardNames, currentBoardIdx, delta)` over the backend-sorted `boards`, and POSTs it via `setBoardOrder` (server-global `event: board-order` echo re-sorts every client — see § Board-list drag reorder). Hidden when the current board is first (`currentBoardIdx === 0` → only Move down) or last (only Move up); a `null` from `computeMoveOrder` is a defensive no-op. `onError` surfaces `useToast().addToast`. (`260708-a2qd`)

**Pure order-computation helpers** — the boundary gating, effective-order derivation, and swap arithmetic are extracted into `app/frontend/src/lib/palette-move.ts` (Vitest-covered `palette-move.test.ts`) so they are testable without mounting the shell; the palette action bodies are thin wrappers that call these then invoke the client mutation. Exports: `deriveEffectiveSessionOrder(liveNames, sseOrder)` (SSE-order-∪-natural), `computeMoveOrder(order, currentIdx, delta)` (insert-before splice, `null` on boundary/out-of-range — shared by Server, Session, AND Board Move), and `computeWindowMoveTarget(index, delta, minIndex, maxIndex)` (target index, `null` on boundary — used by the Window up/down pair). `MoveDelta = -1 | 1`.

**Cross-session move actions**: Dynamically generated "Window: Move to {sessionName}" actions (id `move-window-to-session-{sessionName}`) — one per session other than the current one. Only shown when `currentWindow` exists AND there are at least 2 sessions. On select, calls `moveWindowToSession(server, currentWindow.windowId, targetSession)` then navigates to `/$server` (server dashboard) because tmux auto-assigns the window index in the destination session and no `/$server/$session` route exists. Flat action list (not a sub-picker) — works well for typical session counts (2-5) and requires zero changes to the command palette component.

### Tmux Commands Dialog

`app/frontend/src/components/tmux-commands-dialog.tsx` — opened via command palette "Copy: tmux Commands" action (id `copy-tmux-attach`). Only available on terminal pages when `currentWindow` exists. Opens a `Dialog` with title "tmux commands" showing three copyable tmux command rows:

| Label | Command |
|-------|---------|
| Attach | `tmux [-L {server}] attach-session -t {session}:{window}` |
| New window | `tmux [-L {server}] new-window -t {session}` |
| Detach | `tmux [-L {server}] detach-client -t {session}` |

**Server-aware command generation**: Commands include the `-L {server}` flag only when the server is not `"default"`. When the server is `"default"`, the flag is omitted. This matches the `tmuxExecServer` convention in the backend (see `tmux-sessions.md`).

Each row has a label (`text-text-secondary text-[11px]`), a monospace code block (`bg-bg-inset border border-border rounded px-2 py-1.5 font-mono text-[11px] select-all`), and a copy button. Clicking the copy button writes the command to the clipboard via `navigator.clipboard.writeText` and swaps the copy icon to a checkmark for 1.5 seconds before reverting. Clipboard failure is silently caught.

Dialog state is a `showTmuxCommands` boolean in `app.tsx` (same pattern as `showCreateServerDialog` / `showKillServerConfirm`). Props: `server`, `session`, `window`, `onClose`.

## Visual Design

Three theme modes: **system** (follows OS), **light**, **dark**. Default: system. Linear/Raycast aesthetic.

Theme is applied via `data-theme` attribute on `<html>` (`"dark"` or `"light"`). CSS custom properties in `globals.css` switch values per `html[data-theme="dark"]` and `html[data-theme="light"]` selectors. The `@theme` block registers token names for Tailwind CSS 4 with dark palette as initial values.

### Border-Width System

A three-tier border-width vocabulary, all in `--color-border`: (`260702-6m46`)

- **1px hairlines** (`border` / `border-t` / `border-b`) — the default: card borders, tile borders, in-panel dividers, dropdown separators, the `SectionHeading` rule.
- **3px structural seams** (`border-t-[3px]` / `border-b-[3px]` / `w-[3px]` / `border-[3px]`) — the major chrome boundaries that partition the app frame: the topbar's bottom edge (`top-bar.tsx`), both bottombars (`app.tsx` + `board-page.tsx`), the sidebar's inter-panel separators + Sessions-section top (`collapsible-panel.tsx` + `sidebar/index.tsx`), the sidebar resize handle bar (`app.tsx`; see § Sidebar → Desktop), and the **waiting board-pane seam** (`border-[3px]` + `rk-waiting-seam`; a pulsing yellow attention border, static under reduced-motion — see § Attention Surfacing). The intent: seams read as deliberate structure, hairlines as content detail. (`260706-y1ar`)
- **8px selection accent** (`borderLeft: 8px solid`) — the contrast-guardrailed colored-row accent border (`computeRowBorders`; see § Contrast-guardrailed borders). The thickest tier — a selection/identity signal, not a structural seam. The **sidebar window row does not use it** (selection = 40% tint depth, active-board pin = accent glyph; § Row Anatomy); it is used on **server tiles** (stripe/edge).

### Color Tokens

| Token | Dark | Light | Usage |
|-------|------|-------|-------|
| `--color-bg-primary` | `#0f1117` | `#f8f9fb` | Page background |
| `--color-bg-card` | `#171b24` | `#ffffff` | Card backgrounds |
| `--color-bg-inset` | `#0a0c12` | `#e8eaef` | Fixed-width outer background |
| `--color-text-primary` | `#e8eaf0` | `#1a1d24` | Primary text |
| `--color-text-secondary` | `#7a8394` | `#6b7280` | Secondary text, labels |
| `--color-border` | `#454d66` | `#d1d5db` | Borders, dividers |
| `--color-accent` | `#5b8af0` | `#4a7ae8` | Active states, focus rings |
| `--color-accent-green` | `#22c55e` | `#16a34a` | Activity indicators |
| `--font-mono` | JetBrains Mono, etc. | (same) | Everywhere |

### Theme Switching

Preference persisted to backend API (`PUT /api/settings/theme` → `~/.rk/settings.yaml`) with localStorage key `runkit-theme` as synchronous cache (values: any theme ID or `"system"`). On init, ThemeProvider calls `getThemePreference()` from API; falls back to localStorage / `"system"` if API fails. `setTheme` writes localStorage immediately and calls `setThemePreference(id)` fire-and-forget. Three switching surfaces: (1) command palette (`Cmd+K` → "Theme: System/Light/Dark", current indicated with "(current)" suffix), (2) the sidebar-footer theme button (cycles system → light → dark → system via the shared `cycleTheme()` from `global-chrome.tsx`; § Sidebar footer global-chrome row) plus the settings dialog's theme-pair row (§ Settings Dialog), and (3) the Theme Selector modal (Ctrl+Click / Cmd+Click on the footer theme button, or command palette "Theme: Select Theme").

### No-Flicker Initialization

A blocking inline `<script>` in `index.html` `<head>` reads `localStorage("runkit-theme")`, resolves system preference via `matchMedia`, and sets `data-theme` on `<html>` before first paint. Static fallback: `data-theme="dark"` on the `<html>` tag. The same script also seeds the `theme-color` meta from the echoed instance-accent hex (§ PWA Meta Tags & Theme Color / § Instance Accent).

### PWA Meta Tags & Theme Color

`app/frontend/index.html` includes PWA-related tags in `<head>`:
- `<meta name="theme-color" content="#0f1117" />` — initial value matching dark theme background
- `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-capable" content="yes" />` — enables standalone mode on iOS
- `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style" content="black-translucent" />` — content renders behind the status bar
- `<link rel="apple-touch-icon" href="/generated-icons/icon-192.png" />` — homescreen icon for iOS
- `<link rel="manifest" href="/manifest.json" />` — a static link to the manifest route (the Go backend serves `/manifest.json` dynamically, accent-tinting the icon `src`s per instance — see § Instance Accent → PWA install identity and architecture.md § PWA Identity Assets)
- `<link rel="icon" type="image/svg+xml" href="/generated-icons/favicon.svg" />` — the tab favicon, likewise served dynamically and accent-tinted per request

**Theme-color synchronization**: The `theme-color` meta tag value is kept in sync via a **single shared writer** (`src/instance-accent.ts`, § Instance Accent) that resolves the content to the instance-accent hex when an accent is resolved, else the active theme background:
1. **Initial load** — the blocking inline script in `index.html` sets `theme-color` alongside `data-theme` before first paint; it first tries the echoed instance-accent hex from `runkit-instance-color` (validated `#rrggbb`), falling back to the per-mode default (`#0f1117` dark / `#f8f9fb` light) so an installed PWA window opens already tinted with no flash
2. **Runtime switch** — `applyThemeToDOM` in `ThemeProvider` calls `applyThemeColorMeta(theme.palette.background)` (NOT a direct `setAttribute`), and the `InstanceAccentProvider` calls `setAccentThemeColor(hex)`; both funnel through the one writer's module state so a theme switch cannot clobber the accent tint (§ Instance Accent → PWA titlebar bridge)

Theme color is per-theme (derived from `palette.background`) when no accent is set, else the accent's `titlebarHex` (the ~35% accent-into-background titlebar blend, § Instance Accent → Theme-derived hexes) — not a fixed dark/light pair.

**Icon set**: Canonical mark at `app/frontend/public/icon.svg` (grayscale hexagonal cube, transparent — its five gray fills are what the instance-accent tint replaces). Generated variants in `app/frontend/public/generated-icons/`:
- `favicon.svg` — copy of `icon.svg` (transparent, used as browser favicon)
- `icon-192.png` — 192x192, solid `#0f1117` background, ~20% padding (homescreen icon)
- `icon-512.png` — 512x512, solid `#0f1117` background, ~20% padding (splash screen)
- `icon-512-maskable.png` — 512x512, solid `#0f1117` background, ~40% padding (maskable, safe zone for adaptive icon shapes)

Generated by `scripts/generate-icons.sh` (Node + sharp). Run via `just icons`.

**Standalone display mode**: When installed via "Add to Home Screen" (Android or iOS), the app runs without browser chrome (no address bar, no toolbar). The `display: "standalone"` manifest property and `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` meta tag enable this on their respective platforms.

### ThemeProvider Context

`app/frontend/src/contexts/theme-context.tsx` — split context (ThemeStateContext + ThemeActionsContext) following ChromeContext pattern. Provides `useTheme()` (preference + resolved + theme object) and `useThemeActions()` (setTheme, previewTheme, cancelPreview). Listens to `matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)")` change events when preference is "system" for real-time OS theme tracking. On init: calls `getThemePreference()` from API, falls back to localStorage / `"system"` if API fails. `setTheme` writes localStorage immediately and calls `setThemePreference(id)` fire-and-forget. `applyThemeToDOM` computes CSS values via `deriveUIColors(theme.palette, theme.category)` and writes the `theme-color` meta tag through the shared `applyThemeColorMeta(theme.palette.background)` writer (§ Instance Accent → PWA titlebar bridge — the instance accent wins over the bare background when resolved).

Provider order: `ThemeProvider > ToastProvider > InstanceAccentProvider > ChromeProvider > SessionProvider > AppShell` (`InstanceAccentProvider` inside the theme so it can read `useTheme()`; § Instance Accent).

### xterm Terminal Theme

`terminal-client.tsx` uses `useTheme()` to get the active `Theme` object. Initial theme set at Terminal construction via `deriveXtermTheme(activeTheme.palette)` — all 22 colors (background, foreground, cursor, cursorAccent, selectionBackground, selectionForeground, and 16 named ANSI colors). Live updates via `xtermRef.current.options.theme = deriveXtermTheme(theme.palette)` in a `useEffect` — no terminal recreation needed. There is no `XTERM_THEMES` constant.

### Terminal Relay (frontend) — `RelayMux` singleton + `TerminalClient` port

The browser opens no `/relay/{windowId}` WebSocket per pane. A **`RelayMux` singleton** (`src/lib/relay-mux.ts`, mirroring `state-socket.ts`) owns the ONE `/ws/terminals` socket per tab; each `TerminalClient` consumes a per-stream handle. This mirrors the state-socket transport — same URL-builder shape (`terminalsSocketURL()` vs `stateSocketURL()`), same backoff-reconnect + re-open-live-streams lifecycle, same singleton-per-tab ownership — but carries bulk binary terminal I/O on a SEPARATE socket (see `architecture.md` § Terminal Relay). (`260716-qf3j`, `260717-803u`)

- **`openStream({server, windowId, cols, rows}) → { send, resize, close, onData, onOpened, onClosed, setWindowId }`** — allocates a fresh client-side u32 stream id and issues an `open` control op (once the socket is open, else re-issued in `ws.onopen`). `send` emits a binary `[u32 BE streamId][payload]` data frame (strings UTF-8-encoded); `resize`/`close` emit control ops; `onData` fires on inbound binary frames for that id; `onOpened` on the `opened` control event; `onClosed` on `closed` (carrying 4004/4001/1000). `onOpened` is the re-open signal seams 2/4 (below) need.
- **Backoff reconnect + re-open of live streams** — on a SOCKET-level drop the mux reconnects with exponential backoff (`RECONNECT_BASE_MS = 1000` → `RECONNECT_CAP_MS = 30000`) and, in `ws.onopen`, **re-issues `open` for every still-live stream** — the server re-attaches (fresh PTY) and each pane's deferred reset (below) repaints on the incoming first data frame, so a reconnect is flicker-free with NO per-pane reconnect loop. An idle tab with zero live streams lets the socket stay closed. Each live stream **REUSES its SAME stream id on the new connection** (ids are unique per-connection, so reusing them across a reconnect is valid); a closed stream's id is retired.
- **`setWindowId(windowId)` — the fresh-target seam** — a same-session windowId ride updates the stream's `opts.windowId` WITHOUT a wire op (the live PTY already tracks the new active window). This keeps the stream's re-open target CURRENT: without it, a transient socket drop would re-`open` the STALE open-time window and `SelectWindowInSession` would yank the pane back. `cols`/`rows` stay fresh the same way via `resize()`, so `s.opts` is the single current source the mux re-issues `open` from.
- **A stream-level `closed` retires the stream client-side** (does NOT re-open — unlike a socket drop): `onClosed` fires and the stream is deleted from the mux map.

**`TerminalClient` port — the four delicate seams.** `terminal-client.tsx:813`'s `new WebSocket('/relay/...')` is the ONLY socket-creation site. The stream handle is surfaced through the `wsRef` seam via a **WebSocket-shaped adapter** (`{ readyState, send, close }`) so the shell consumers (`BottomBar`, the docked compose strip, touch-scroll, `fitAndSync`) that read `wsRef.current.{send,readyState,close}` are UNTOUCHED (a `{type:"resize"}` JSON `send` routes to the stream's `resize` op, everything else to a binary data frame; the `adapter as unknown as WebSocket` double-cast is the documented tradeoff — a structural `wsRef` type would remove it). The four seams:

1. **Confirmation-gated window-switch receipt = the stream's first DATA frame** — `notifyFirstWrite()` fires at the top of the stream's `onData` handler (`handleInbound`), before the write/coalesce decision. The gate state machine is pure. See § Window-Switch Slide Transition. (`260715-38kg`)
2. **Connect-select alignment epoch anchors on `open`→`opened`** — the deferred reset is armed and the grid re-fit/re-synced in `stream.onOpened` (which fires on the initial open AND every transparent re-open). Because the server enqueues `opened` on the stream's own queue ahead of any data (`architecture.md` § Terminal Relay, M2), the epoch is soundly ordered in-band, closing the `onmessage`-races-the-select-POST hazard (memory `relay-connect-select-alignment`).
3. **IntersectionObserver pane suspension = stream `close`/`open`** (no socket churn) — see § Desktop Relay-Connection Suspension.
4. **Deferred per-stream reset** — the deferred reset (below) is per-stream, re-armed in `stream.onOpened`.

**Non-4004 `closed` self-heal.** RelayMux re-opens only on a SOCKET drop, not a stream-level `closed`, so the client owns per-stream recovery: a `closed` with code 4004 prints `[session not found]` and fires `onSessionNotFound` (redirect); a non-4004 close (4001 attach-failed, 1000 PTY-EOF/graceful) prints `[reconnecting…]` and **probes exactly ONE fresh re-open of the current window** (bounded by a `probedReopen` flag). A genuinely gone window then 4004s the probe → redirect; a transient one re-attaches. A second non-4004 close prints `[disconnected]` and stops (no spin on a hard-failing window). This is a per-pane self-heal without a per-pane reconnect loop.

**Tab budget.** The tab holds a fixed **2 WebSockets** (state + terminals) and zero SSE — the e2e connection guard (`connection-budget.spec.ts`) asserts exactly this. See `architecture.md` § Terminal Relay for the backend/protocol. (`260716-qf3j`)

### Terminal Write Batching (Adaptive Flush + Deferred Reset)

Inbound terminal data in `terminal-client.tsx` — a `RelayMux` stream's inbound binary frames — flows through an **adaptive flush** (PR #244, with WebGL-renderer confirmation + context-loss survival in PR #245): (`260327-cnav`, `260717-803u`)

- **Immediate path** — a chunk that is small (≤ `IMMEDIATE_WRITE_MAX_BYTES` = 64 **UTF-8 bytes**), arrives while **idle** (nothing buffered, no flush pending), and is the **first immediate write this frame** is written synchronously so a keystroke echo paints on the same tick (latency attribution showed the rAF tail dominated perceived input latency ~3:1 over the network hop; keystroke→echo went 40→10ms). The threshold is measured in UTF-8 bytes via `textByteLength` — `String.length` UTF-16 units would under-count multibyte input and weaken the flood guard; the helper only pays for a `TextEncoder` encode in the ambiguous middle band, keeping the tiny-ASCII hot path allocation-free. The one-immediate-write-per-frame guard is `wroteImmediatelyThisFrame`, set by `markImmediateWrite()` and cleared by a one-shot rAF — a program emitting one byte at a time still coalesces after its first byte of the frame.
- **Coalesced path** — everything else (large chunk, already buffering, or a second small chunk in the same frame) accumulates in buffers (string concatenation for text, `Uint8Array[]` for binary) and flushes once per `requestAnimationFrame` (`flushToTerminal`), batching floods (builds, log tailing, `cat largefile`) into one xterm render pass per frame.
- **Ordering guarantee** — terminal bytes are order-sensitive: the moment anything is buffered, every subsequent chunk buffers until the buffer drains; an immediate write only happens when the buffer is empty AND no flush is pending, so a synchronous write can never jump ahead of buffered bytes.
- **Close-time drain** — the stream's `onClosed` handler cancels pending rAFs and calls `flushToTerminal()` (try/catch for a disposed terminal) **before** the `cancelled` check, so a transient-drop reconnect never loses tail data; `cancelAnimationFrame()` also runs on effect cleanup. Under the mux, a socket-level drop is transparent — RelayMux re-opens and the pane repaints on the first data frame — so `onClosed` fires only for stream-level closes; the close-time drain runs before the non-4004 probe re-open (above).

**Deferred per-stream reset**: each (re)open arms an effect-scoped `pendingReset` flag — armed in the stream's `onOpened`; `consumePendingReset()` (check + clear + `terminal.reset()`) runs **exactly once per stream open, immediately before that open's FIRST chunk write** — in the same tick on the immediate path, at the top of the same rAF callback (same frame) on the coalesced path — so clear + repaint land in **one presented frame**, with no black-frame flicker (the old content persists until the new redraw paints). This holds on every re-open path (sidebar click, tmux status-bar click, palette, board pane churn, transient socket-drop re-open, non-4004 probe re-open). **Ordering dependency**: arming on `onOpened` is only sound because the server enqueues `opened` ahead of the first data frame (`architecture.md` § Terminal Relay, M2) — a data-before-`opened` frame would fire the reset AFTER the first repaint and wipe it. (`260610-qf25`, `260717-803u`)

**`onOpened` also wipes the carried-over flush state.** Alongside arming `pendingReset`, the `onOpened` handler clears `binaryBuffers` and cancels/nulls any pending `flushRafId` — the adaptive-flush state belongs to the connection that just died, and it must never replay into the fresh one. **Why it is load-bearing**: on a backgrounded tab rAF never fires, so inbound bytes pile up unflushed; RelayMux then re-opens on wake and the server re-attaches. Writing that stale backlog into the new stream repaints dead-connection content, and any terminal query sitting in it would be answered by xterm.js into the CURRENT tmux client — which already got its answer, so the extra reply is typed into the active pane as junk. The relay strips tmux's DA identification queries before they ever reach the browser (`tmux-sessions.md` § Terminal Relay), so this wipe is defense-in-depth against that specific symptom while independently closing the whole replay-dead-connection-bytes class. Discarding the backlog costs nothing — the deferred reset wipes the repaint anyway. Safe w.r.t. the handoff below — an empty flush neither consumes nor executes the freshly armed `pendingReset`. The per-frame immediate-write pacing state (`wroteImmediatelyThisFrame`/`frameResetRafId`) is deliberately left untouched: it holds no bytes and self-resets on the next rAF tick. (`260801-f715-relay-device-attributes-responder`)

**Handoff semantics**: an **empty flush neither consumes nor executes a pending reset** — `flushToTerminal` early-returns when both buffers are empty, so a zero-data stream's close-time drain is a no-op (resetting with nothing to repaint would recreate the flicker); the next open simply re-arms in `onOpened`, idempotently. The **effect cleanup additionally neutralizes the closure's pending write state** (`pendingReset = false; binaryBuffers = []`): a late-delivered `onClosed`/`onData` — which can arrive AFTER cleanup, and whose drain runs before the `cancelled` check so same-effect transient-drop tail drains keep working — can therefore neither reset the shared terminal nor write stale old-window content after a successor effect (e.g. a `windowId` change forcing a fresh stream) has taken over. Reset-ordering unit tests live in `terminal-client.test.tsx` (no receipt-time reset; exactly once per stream open; string + binary first chunks; re-armed on re-open; zero-data close; post-consumption tail drain; connection A's buffered-but-unflushed bytes never written after connection B's `onOpened` — asserting against the mocked `RelayMux`/stream handle).

**Relay connection identity is (server, owning session), NOT windowId**: the stream-open effect's deps are `[terminalReady, server, wsRef, connectionEpoch]` — `sessionName` and `windowId` are deliberately excluded (the `eslint-disable react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` comment explains why). A same-session windowId switch (sidebar click, palette, URL writeback after a tmux-status-bar click) **rides the existing stream**: tmux has already switched the active window in place (or will, via the REST `selectWindow` already fired by `navigateToWindow` and mount-time alignment in app.tsx) and the directly-attached PTY redraws by itself — no stream close/open, no `terminal.reset()`, and **xterm scrollback is preserved** across same-session switches (native `tmux attach` behavior). A dedicated `windowId`-keyed effect calls **`streamRef.current.setWindowId(windowId)`** on every ride so the mux's re-open target stays current. Any GENUINE reconnect — session change, server change, transient socket drop — attaches to the latest window: the epoch-bumping paths re-open a fresh stream reading `windowIdRef.current`, and a socket drop's re-`open` reads the stream's `setWindowId`-kept-fresh `opts.windowId`. (`260602-qn62`, `260610-9umy`, `260717-803u`)

Identity is tracked by `connectedSessionRef` (session the live connection serves; `""` = resolved server-side only) + `connectedServerRef` + a `connectionEpoch` state bumped by a **session-identity watcher** effect on `[sessionName, server]`. The watcher is declared BEFORE the connect effect — ordering is load-bearing: on a same-commit server+session change it must read the PRE-change server from `connectedServerRef` (the connect effect overwrites it) to skip its bump, since the connect effect already re-runs via its own `server` dep — bumping too would tear down and reconnect twice. Watcher direction semantics:

- **`""` → resolved: absorption only** (record, never bump). On a cold deep-link the SSE-derived `sessionName` prop is `""` until the first snapshot; the client connects immediately by windowId and the relay resolves the owning session server-side (`ResolveWindowSession`) — by construction the live connection is already attached to that window's owning session, so the resolution transition costs no reconnect.
- **resolved → resolved: bump** — cross-session navigation, or a window moved to another session. A session RENAME also lands here and reconnects — accepted tradeoff: the SSE snapshot carries no stable session id to tell a rename from a genuine session change, renames are rare, and the deferred reset keeps any reconnect flicker-free.
- **resolved → `""`: LOSS-OF-IDENTITY signal** — record `""` AND bump for a probe reconnect. `sessionName` is derived by locating the URL's `@N` in the SSE snapshot, so it goes `""` and STAYS `""` when the viewed window left the snapshot: killed externally, pinned to a filtered `_rk-pin-*` session (pin transitions present as resolved → `""`, never resolved → resolved), or a dead deep link. The probe (by `windowIdRef.current`) either re-resolves the owning session server-side (an X → `""` → X ghost gap during navigation costs one flicker-free reconnect) or the relay closes 4004 → the `onSessionNotFound` redirect fires. This is the ONLY reachable recovery path in that state — `computeKillRedirect` and the URL writeback are both gated on a non-empty `sessionName` — so the bump is load-bearing (review finding M1, fixed in rework cycle 1: the original "treat `""` as inert in both directions" wedged the UI on kill-while-viewing).
- **`""` → `""`: no-op** (cold mount, still unresolved).

Deferred-reset and adaptive-flush semantics apply on every stream open that IS established (cross-session switch, server change, transient drop re-open, non-4004 probe re-open). Connection-lifecycle unit tests live in the "TerminalClient connection identity" block of `terminal-client.test.tsx` (same-session switch keeps the stream + pushes `setWindowId`; cross-session switch closes old + opens exactly one new with the deferred reset before its first write; `""` → resolved no-reconnect with later genuine change reconnecting; server change reconnects exactly once — no watcher double-bump; resolved → `""` probe; 4004 recovery after an external kill; the non-4004 feedback + one-probe path). The **transient-drop-latest-windowId** assertion is split across the seam: `terminal-client.test.tsx` pins the same-session ride pushing `setWindowId("@5")` into the live stream, and `relay-mux.test.ts` pins the socket-drop reconnect re-issuing `open` with the ridden-to windowId (`relay-mux.test.ts` also unit-tests framing, id-demuxed data in/out, and `opened`/`closed` dispatch against a stubbed WebSocket).

### Window-Switch Slide Transition

A same-server window switch animates as a **vertical slide** of the terminal surface via the browser's [View Transitions API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/View_Transitions_API), keyed off the two windows' positions in the flattened sidebar order (macOS-Spaces-like, but vertical because the sidebar arranges windows vertically). It is **fire-and-forget** (a play-on-switch animation), NOT a gesture-scrubbed interactive drag. It is pure UX polish and layers ON TOP of the deferred-reset / relay-identity machinery above — a same-session switch still rides the existing socket with no reconnect; the slide just animates the in-place repaint. Progressive enhancement: any gate failure falls through to the instant switch. (`260703-l4nf`)

> **The slide is an EARNED signal.** The mechanics below (the one seam, the gate, direction, CSS, the first-paint gate) are the foundation; the slide plays ONLY on confirmed-fast arrival, with a pending spinner mask + a failure bounce-back layered on (§ Confirmation-gated motion below). `waitForFirstWrite()` resolves a `GateSettleReason` (`"first-write" | "timeout" | "superseded"`), and the wrapper `skipTransition()`s on every non-`"first-write"` settle rather than always playing the slide. (`260715-38kg`)

**The one seam — `navigateToWindow`.** The wrapper lives on `navigateToWindow` (`app.tsx`) alone — the single same-server switch seam shared by sidebar clicks, command-palette `Window: Switch to …` entries (keyboard path, constitution V), TopBar breadcrumb navigation, and SessionTiles clicks — so the animation applies identically to keyboard- and mouse-driven switches. The instant-switch body is factored into a `runSwitch()` closure (optimistic `navigate` + `selectWindow` POST + mobile sidebar close); the wrapper either calls it directly (fallback) or runs it inside `document.startViewTransition`'s async callback (see the timing caveat in § URL as Resumable Bookmark — on the animated path `runSwitch` runs ≥1 frame after the click). `runSwitch` **returns the `selectWindow` POST** so the gate can wait until tmux has been told to switch. **Direct `startViewTransition`, NOT TanStack Router's `viewTransition: true` option** — the polished first-paint gate (below) needs the async-callback seam to await a custom signal, which the router option does not expose.

**Gate (`shouldAnimateWindowSwitch`).** The switch animates only when ALL hold: View Transitions supported (`viewTransitionSupported()` — a plain-`boolean` `typeof document.startViewTransition === "function"` check, NOT a type predicate; the TS 5.7+ `dom` lib already declares the API, so no ambient augmentation and no `as` cast — code-quality type-narrowing), `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` NOT set (checked via a **guarded** `matchMedia` — `typeof window.matchMedia === "function"`, for jsdom/older-WebView envs), an outgoing terminal window is in view (`windowParam`/`currentWindowId` non-empty — a first switch from SessionTiles with no outgoing surface skips), and the slide direction is non-null. Any failure → `runSwitch()` directly (instant switch, unchanged). Cross-server navigation takes the separate `navigate` branch in `handleSidebarSelectWindow`, never `navigateToWindow`, so it falls out with no transition and no code change; board routes and the Host page are untouched.

**Direction — flattened SSE sidebar order → root data attribute.** `windowSwitchDirection(order, currentId, targetId)` (pure, exported, in `src/lib/window-transition.ts`, colocated `.test.ts` — the same pure-helper pattern as `resolveServerView`/`computeKillRedirect`) returns `"up"` when the target sits BELOW current (new content enters from the bottom), `"down"` when ABOVE, and `null` when either id is missing from the order or the ids are equal (→ skip). `order` is `flatWindows.map((fw) => fw.window.windowId)` read from a render-synced `switchTransitionRef` (so the click handler reads current order without re-creating the callback). The resolved direction is written to `document.documentElement.dataset.windowSwitchDirection = "up" | "down"` immediately before `startViewTransition` (the `data-theme` root-attribute pattern); the CSS pseudo-elements key off `:root[data-window-switch-direction=…]`. **Accepted divergence:** `flatWindows` is raw SSE order, while the sidebar's drag-reorder override (`orderOverrideRef`) is component-local, so during a transient drag-reorder window the slide direction may briefly disagree with the visual sidebar order until SSE confirms — rare and self-healing.

**Direction-attribute latest-wins guard.** The attribute is cleared in `transition.finished.finally()`. But `finished` **FULFILLS on skip** (a rapid second switch supersedes this transition) — it does not reject — so a superseded transition's `finally()` would otherwise delete the attribute its successor already set, dropping the successor's direction CSS. Guard: each switch mints a monotonic token via `nextDirectionToken()` and clears the attribute only when `isLatestDirectionToken(token)` — only the LATEST switch's cleanup may clear it (mirrors the gate's still-points-at-itself pattern; pure module state, no DOM).

**CSS (`globals.css`) — pure transform + containment + full UA-animation neutralization.** `view-transition-name: terminal-surface` is on the terminal content container `<div>` (the shared wrapper around the `TerminalClient`/`IframeWindow` branch, merged with the fixed-width style) so **only the terminal region slides** — sidebar, top bar, bottom bar stay static. `::view-transition-old/new(terminal-surface)` animate via pure `translateY` keyframes, **180ms ease-out**, up/down variants keyed off the root attribute. Transform-only means **no layout change** — the container's `ResizeObserver`/`fitAndSync` path never fires, so tmux sees NO resize churn during the slide (hard constraint). Two non-obvious rules, both probe-verified:
- **Containment:** `::view-transition-image-pair(terminal-surface) { overflow: clip; }` — without it the translating snapshots escape the terminal group and paint over the top bar / bottom bar / sidebar (the UA does no clipping on the pseudo-tree by default).
- **Full UA-default neutralization for LIFETIME, not just visuals:** `::view-transition-old/new(root)` AND `::view-transition-old/new(terminal-surface)` AND `::view-transition-group(root), ::view-transition-group(terminal-surface)` ALL get `animation: none`. The group rule is the load-bearing one: `animation: none` on old/new alone does NOT shorten the transition — the UA's default **~250ms `::view-transition-group(*)` animations** hold the whole pseudo-tree (and thus the pointer-dead window and `transition.finished`) alive ~250–290ms past the 180ms slide. Neutralizing the group animations makes the 180ms slide govern the transition's whole lifetime.

**First-paint gate (`SwitchGate` in `window-transition.ts`) — the polished variant.** The new-state snapshot is gated on the incoming window's first inbound bytes so the surface slides in painted, not blank, with a **~300ms budget measured from callback entry**. Mechanics, in one module:
- `beginWindowSwitchGate()` opens a per-transition gate (a distinct token) and **FIRES any prior still-pending gate on supersession** — so rapid switches never serialize: the VT spec queues a second `startViewTransition` callback behind the first's returned promise, and the first callback holds that promise open while awaiting its gate, so firing the prior gate lets the queued second callback run at once. This call is load-bearing on BOTH the terminal AND iframe paths (a terminal→iframe switch must fire an in-flight terminal gate), so it runs before branching on iframe-vs-terminal.
- The callback does NOT `await` the `selectWindow` POST — it **chains `openForNotify()` off it** (`void posted.then(() => gate.openForNotify()).catch(() => {})`) and awaits only `gate.waitForFirstWrite()`, whose timeout clock starts at callback entry. This hard-caps the callback (and thus document-wide render suppression) at ~300ms **regardless of the POST's fate** — a stalled `selectWindow` (no client fetch timeout) can no longer freeze the document up to Chromium's ~4s VT deadline, and a rapid second switch never queues behind a stalled POST. `openForNotify` is the identity filter: only writes arriving AFTER the POST resolves count, so a busy OUTGOING window's still-streaming bytes (a same-session switch rides the existing socket) can't release the gate before tmux has run `select-window`.
- `settleGate` resolves the pending promise, clears the timer, and relinquishes the module slot **only when it still points at that gate** (still-points-at-itself) — a late timeout from a superseded gate can neither resolve nor null a newer gate.
- **Release is at message-RECEIPT time — `notifyFirstWrite()` at the TOP of the stream's `onData` handler (`handleInbound`) in `terminal-client.tsx`, before the write/coalesce decision — NOT at write time.** The receipt source is the stream's first DATA frame (inbound frames are always binary under the mux, so there is one `onData` handler). This is the load-bearing subtlety: `startViewTransition` suppresses rendering while its callback runs, and **rAF callbacks do not fire during suppression**. The coalesced flush (`flushToTerminal`) is reachable only via `scheduleFlush`'s `requestAnimationFrame`, and a tmux redraw's first chunk (~4KB) always exceeds `IMMEDIATE_WRITE_MAX_BYTES` (64) — so a write-time signal is structurally unreleasable on the common path, and every animated switch would degenerate into the full 300ms freeze. `onData` is a macrotask and DOES run during suppression; releasing there is safe for capture correctness because the pending rAF flush runs at the first rendering opportunity after the callback resolves — the same rendering update in which the new state is captured, so the received bytes are painted into the captured frame. **Distinguish this receipt-time NOTIFY (the transition first-paint gate) from the deferred per-stream RESET (`consumePendingReset`, above)** — different mechanisms at different seams: the reset fires once per stream open before its first chunk write, the notify fires at receipt time. (`260717-803u`)
- **iframe targets** (`switchTransitionRef.iframeIds`) have no terminal receipt seam, so they use the **ungated capture** (skip the wait entirely). The predicate is the **effective resolved view** (spec R12), not raw `rkType && rkUrl`: a target is an iframe target iff `resolveView(undefined, readStoredView(server, fw.window.windowId), fw.window) === "web"` (the URL `?view=` param is unknown for a not-yet-navigated target, so it resolves from localStorage + the window's default hint only). This keeps a `web`-rendering target on the ungated path AND — critically — keeps an **iframe-typed window whose last-view is `tty`** (or one with no `rkUrl`) on the **gated terminal path**, exactly where it renders a `TerminalClient`. Getting this wrong reintroduces the blank-pane/stuck-transition class of bugs this section documents — hence its own R12 task + e2e switch coverage. Because both the render branch and this classification call the same `resolveView`, the two can't drift. (`260714-t97o`)

**Accepted trade-offs.** (1) During the ~180ms slide the VT snapshot pseudo-element hit-tests to `<html>`, so a click landing on the terminal region mid-transition is lost — a brief **whole-document pointer-input dead window**. Keyboard input is unaffected (it targets the focused element, not a hit-tested point), so keyboard-first flows (constitution V) never lose input. (2) The direction-vs-sidebar-drag divergence noted above.

**Testing.** `playwright.config.ts` sets reduced-motion globally so window-switch e2e specs run against instant switches (the product's own fallback). **Trap worth recording:** `reducedMotion` is NOT a top-level `use` fixture in Playwright 1.59.1 — it is **silently ignored** there and only reaches the browser context via `contextOptions`, so the config uses `use: { contextOptions: { reducedMotion: "reduce" } }` (the top-level form is a no-op AND tsc-unchecked, since the config sits outside the tsconfig include). One dedicated animated-path spec `app/frontend/tests/e2e/window-switch-transition.spec.ts` (+ companion `.spec.md` per constitution Test Companion Docs) opts back in via `test.use({ contextOptions: { reducedMotion: "no-preference" } })`, performs a same-session switch, and asserts the incoming window's content paints AND `data-window-switch-direction` clears within ~1s — guarding against a regression that re-introduces a systematic gate-timeout freeze. Colocated Vitest unit tests cover `windowSwitchDirection` (up/down/missing/equal cases), `shouldAnimateWindowSwitch` (each gate branch), `viewTransitionSupported`, the full gate API (open-for-notify filtering, timeout, supersession-fires-pending, stale-timer isolation, stalled-POST bounded wait), and the direction-token latest-wins guard.

#### Confirmation-gated motion — the slide is an EARNED signal

Each visual state is an **honest, distinct signal** — the slide plays only on confirmed-fast arrival, so it never animates unconfirmed content (which would show the OLD window's bytes on a slow/failed switch), and a silent `selectWindow` failure can no longer park the URL/heading in an indefinite limbo with keystrokes routed to the OLD window: (`260715-38kg`)

| Signal | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| slide | confirmed arrival (bytes within the 300ms budget) |
| spinner mask | in transit — don't type |
| spinner → content cut | arrived late |
| spinner → bounce + toast | switch failed |

Optimistic navigation is retained (heading/URL flip at click = acknowledged intent; sidebar highlight stays SSE-derived = confirmation); pessimistic navigation and a dimmed overlay are both rejected (§ Design Decisions → Window-switch feedback stays optimistic). Frontend-only — the `notifyFirstWrite()` call is the ONE arrival signal. The gate machine is pure; the receipt SOURCE is the RelayMux stream's first `onData` frame (see the first-paint-gate release bullet above). (`260715-38kg`, `260717-803u`)

**Gate settle reasons (`window-transition.ts`).** `waitForFirstWrite()` resolves a `GateSettleReason = "first-write" | "timeout" | "superseded"`. `settleGate(gate, reason)` carries the reason from its three callers: `notifyFirstWrite` → `"first-write"`, the gate timer → `"timeout"`, supersession by a newer `beginWindowSwitchGate`/`armGraceMask` → `"superseded"`. `FIRST_WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS = 300` is forced by VT render suppression — must not be extended. Three concurrency guarantees hold: supersession fires the prior pending gate immediately; only the INCOMING window's post-POST bytes release the gate; the still-points-at-itself guard means a stale timer never clobbers a newer gate or touches its mask. (`260715-38kg`)

**`skipTransition()` on EVERY non-`first-write` settle.** In `navigateToWindow`'s VT callback the wrapper awaits the reason and calls `transition.skipTransition()` whenever `reason !== "first-write"` (`app.tsx`). This covers `"timeout"` (cut to the masked new state — no motion into unconfirmed content) AND `"superseded"`, which has TWO cases: a rapid successor VT (the VT spec already skips this transition, so the explicit call is a harmless no-op) AND an **abandoned/bounced** switch — `abandonSwitchFeedback()` settles a still-pending gate `"superseded"` with NO successor VT, so WITHOUT the explicit skip the failed switch would animate alongside its own failure feedback (slide = confirmed arrival, so a failed switch must never slide). The `"first-write"` fast path is byte-identical to `l4nf`. Note SSE-confirm deliberately settles `"first-write"` (via `confirmSwitchArrived`, below): an authoritative arrival IS a confirmed arrival, so its slide is correct.

**The LogoSpinner pending mask — pure module state machine + a `useSyncExternalStore` seam.** The mask is expressed as pure, DOM-free, unit-testable module logic in `window-transition.ts` (NOT React `useState` in the wrapper — that would duplicate arm/lift/teardown and reintroduce gate↔mask drift): a `MaskState = "idle" | "masked"` with `getMaskState()` + `subscribeMaskState(listener)` (the `useSyncExternalStore` contract — snapshot stable while unchanged, listeners fire only on an actual transition). `app.tsx` reads it via `useSyncExternalStore(subscribeMaskState, getMaskState)` and, when `"masked"`, renders an absolutely-positioned full-cover `<div className="rk-window-switch-mask" role="status">` with a centered `<LogoSpinner size={48}>` over the `terminal-surface` container (which gained `position: relative`). It FULLY hides the stale bytes (a full mask, never a dimmed overlay) and sits at `z-index: 40` (below the `z-50` toast container).
- **Armed ONLY at the 300ms gate-timeout decision — NEVER at click time.** The mask arms as a side effect of `settleGate` when `reason === "timeout"` AND the gate was created `gated` (tty). Arming there, not in the wrapper after the await, makes the "never at click time" invariant structural (during the VT callback the screen is frozen on the old snapshot anyway, so early masking gains nothing). `beginWindowSwitchGate({ gated })` carries the tty flag; the wrapper passes `{ gated: !targetUngated }` (the `ungatedIds` classification, § Window Views → Window-switch transition classification — web/chat targets never mask and never reach a timeout settle).
- **POST-filtered lift via epoch + `liftAccepting`.** The SAME `notifyFirstWrite` receipt that releases the gate ALSO lifts the mask on late arrival ("one signal drives everything") — but the lift is FILTERED exactly like the gate release: a byte counts only once the switch's `selectWindow` POST has resolved. A module-level `liftAccepting` flag is `false` from switch start until the switch opens it (`openForNotify()` on the gate path chains off the POST; `openForLift()` on the grace path — see below), guarded by a monotonic `switchEpoch` minted per switch so a STALE switch's late POST resolution can never enable lifts for a NEWER switch's mask. Without the filter, a busy OUTGOING window's still-streaming bytes (a same-session switch rides the same socket) would un-mask stale content before tmux ever ran `select-window` — the exact hazard being fixed.
- **Input block while masked.** The overlay's `pointer-events: auto` swallows clicks on the old window; keyboard is blocked by a capture-phase `onKeyDownCapture` on the `terminal-surface` container that `preventDefault()`+`stopPropagation()`s keystrokes (dropped, NOT buffered/replayed — replay into the new window would be its own surprise). **Global chords are EXEMPT** via the pure `isMaskExemptKey(e)` predicate (constitution V — Cmd+K is the primary discovery mechanism, and the swallow could otherwise kill it for up to the 5s confirmation window): `Escape`; meta (Cmd) chords EXCEPT `Cmd+V` (xterm doesn't forward meta-modified keys to the pty, but the browser's default paste DOES land in xterm's focused textarea → the old pty, so paste stays swallowed); and the specific Ctrl-bound globals `Ctrl+K`/`.`/`\`/`` ` `` with `!altKey` required (AltGr reports `ctrlKey:true` and AltGr+char is typed INPUT, not a chord — same modifier discipline as `use-chat-view-shortcut.ts`). Plain typing and terminal control bytes (Ctrl+C) stay dropped.
- **Lift is a CUT (a fast fade-in only), never a second slide.** `.rk-window-switch-mask` has a 120ms `rk-window-switch-mask-in` fade-IN; the lift is a plain unmount. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the mask STILL shows (input safety is never motion-only) but the fade is dropped (`animation: none`). **Ordering matters (rework G4):** the base rule + keyframes sit BEFORE the reduced-motion block, so the equal-specificity `animation: none` override wins by later source order (the same discipline as `.rk-waiting-halo`).
- **`abandonSwitchFeedback()` teardown — settles the gate, not just the mask.** The failure/bounce and route-leave/unmount paths call `abandonSwitchFeedback()` (NOT a bare `tearDownMask()`): it settles a still-pending gate `"superseded"` AND tears the mask down. A bare unmask would leave the pending gate armed — a `selectWindow` POST that rejects INSIDE the 300ms budget bounces first, then the gate's timer fires `settleGate(gate, "timeout")` and re-arms a mask over the bounced-back window with NO lift path (`liftAccepting` stays false after a rejected POST), a permanently stuck input-blocking mask (rework G2). `confirmSwitchArrived()` is the sibling authority-confirmed teardown: it settles a still-pending gate `"first-write"` (so its slide is correct) and tears down any mask — closing the real gap where a same-session tmux redraw completes BEFORE `openForNotify` (those bytes filtered as outgoing), leaving no later write to lift and letting the gate arm a mask over a switch that DID land. The bare `tearDownMask()` primitive has ONE sanctioned direct production caller — `beginPendingSwitch`'s fresh-switch teardown (§ below) — which clears a leftover mask WITHOUT settling a gate; the abandonment (`abandonSwitchFeedback`) and arrival (`confirmSwitchArrived`) wrappers cover every other intent and both delegate here.

**Failure bounce-back — un-sticking the silent-failure limbo.** `CONFIRMATION_WINDOW_MS = 5000` (named tunable) arms a SINGLE `setTimeout` per pending switch (NOT a poll — SSE stays the confirmation source). The bounce fires on an explicit `selectWindow` POST rejection OR the confirmation-window timeout — **NEVER** merely because SSE still reports the old window mid-switch. `bouncePendingSwitch(target)` clears `pendingClickRef`, calls `abandonSwitchFeedback()`, explicitly `navigate`s (replace) to tmux's actual active window (a stale `activeWindow` won't retrigger the writeback effect), and `addToast("Window switch didn't confirm — back to the active window", "error")`.
- **Server-scoped identity at all FOUR seams (rework H1, cycle 3).** `pendingClickRef` is a `PendingSwitchTarget = { server, windowId }` compared via the pure `isSamePendingTarget(pending, server, windowId)` at: (1) the mount-alignment skip, (2) the writeback's `urlMatchesPending`, (3) the writeback's `sseConfirmed`, and (4) `bouncePendingSwitch`'s guard. tmux `@N` ids are only unique per server and `AppShell` persists across `$server` changes, so a window-id-alone match false-positives on a cross-server collision (serverA `@5` vs serverB `@5`) — see § Click-intent and URL-match above. `bouncePendingSwitch` additionally clears SILENTLY (no toast, no navigation) when the recorded server no longer matches the live route (`serverRef.current !== target.server`), and its navigate uses `target.server` only after verifying it equals the live route server — a stale-server navigation is structurally inexpressible.
- **Three teardown effects, each a distinct route topology.** (a) **Server-change** (`$server` param change) — declared BEFORE the alignment effect (effects run in declaration order within a commit) so the skip sees a cleared intent and serverB's alignment fires; prev-ref keyed so it no-ops on mount. (b) **Route-leave** (`!windowParam` — windowless `/$server` SessionTiles, where the writeback can early-return) so a straggler timer can't navigate the user back with a spurious toast. (c) **Unmount** (Host `/` or a board route unmounts AppShell, but the setTimeout closure + module mask state outlive the instance). All three call `abandonSwitchFeedback()`. These are NOT subsumed by one another.
- **`beginPendingSwitch(target, { posted, graceMask })`** is the one entry point that records the intent, supersedes prior tracking, **proactively tears down a leftover mask/grace timer** (a fresh switch owns ALL feedback), arms the confirmation timer, and (instant path only) arms the grace mask + wires `openForLift` off the POST. The proactive teardown is a **bare `tearDownMask()`** at the top of the callback (right after `clearPendingSwitchTracking()`): `clearPendingSwitchTracking` cancels only the tracked entry's own timer/handle, NOT the module mask, so without it a prior TIMED-OUT switch's armed mask survives into the new route until SSE confirmation. It is deliberately the BARE primitive, NOT `abandonSwitchFeedback` — on the animated path `beginPendingSwitch` runs (via `beginWindowSwitchGate → startViewTransition → runSwitch`) while the switch's OWN just-opened gate is current, and settling it would skip the earned slide (`260715-38kg` R8); it is idempotent on the gated paths (which already tore down via `armGraceMask`/`beginWindowSwitchGate`). This closes the gateless-path residual: the two gateless call sites — the deep-link mount-alignment effect (no grace mask — a cold deep-link mounts a fresh connecting pane, not stale bytes) and `navigateToWaitingTarget`'s same-server branch (no grace mask, `?view=chat`-preserving) — otherwise cleared nothing, carrying a stale input-blocking mask into a window the user just navigated to. Its explicit-rejection handler is keyed on the **tracked object identity** (`pendingSwitchRef.current === tracked`, the still-points-at-itself pattern), NOT the windowId — so a superseded POST's late rejection can't bounce a newer healthy switch (rework SF8). It is also called from the click path (`runSwitch`). The mount-alignment effect SKIPS re-tracking when `isSamePendingTarget(pendingClickRef.current, server, windowParam)` — a click already recorded the intent, and re-tracking would fire a DUPLICATE `selectWindow` POST and (via `beginPendingSwitch`'s supersession) cancel the click's just-armed grace mask, making the instant-path mask dead in the live app (rework G1, the cycle-2 must-fix that made the instant path actually work).
- **Accepted non-goal — a prior switch's still-PENDING gate re-masking after a gateless switch begins.** The bare teardown clears an already-*armed* leftover mask, not a still-pending prior gate: an animated switch to A followed within ~300ms by a gateless nav to B lets A's gate timer fire and re-arm the mask over B. Discriminating that needs gate supersession `beginPendingSwitch` cannot safely perform (own-gate ambiguity — it runs with the animated path's own gate current), so it stays a rare self-healing corner, settled on the next SSE confirm or by the ungated instant path's own `abandonSwitchFeedback()`.

**Non-VT / reduced-motion grace-timer parity (`armGraceMask`).** Browsers without View Transitions support or with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` take the instant-switch path (no render-freeze phase, so no gate to time out). `armGraceMask(timeoutMs = 300)` returns a `GraceMaskHandle { openForLift(), cancel() }` and arms `maskState → "masked"` after the same 300ms threshold UNLESS an early first write cancels it or a late one lifts it — same threshold as the gate, different mechanism, same POST-filtered lift (`openForLift` mirrors the gate's `openForNotify`; epoch-guarded) and same `abandonSwitchFeedback` teardown. It supersedes any prior grace timer AND any still-pending gate (a rapid animated→instant sequence must not let a stale gate's timeout mask the newer switch) and clears any leftover mask — one feedback machinery at a time. On the instant path `runSwitch(graceMask)` receives `!ungatedIds.has(windowId)` (a gated tty target masks; web/chat stay mask-less, matching the gated path); the animated path passes `false` (its gate's timeout arms the mask instead).

**Tests.** `window-transition.test.ts` (colocated Vitest) covers the three settle reasons, the mask state machine (arm-at-timeout gated-only, lift-on-late-write via `notifyFirstWrite`, teardown on supersession / via `abandonSwitchFeedback` / via `confirmSwitchArrived`, gated-`false` does NOT arm), the `subscribeMaskState` seam (notify-on-change, unsubscribe stops), grace-timer parity (arm-after-threshold / cancel-on-early / lift-on-late), the post-POST lift filter, the SF8 stale-rejection identity guard, `isMaskExemptKey` (Cmd+V NOT exempt, AltGr NOT exempt), `isRedundantSwitch`, and `isSamePendingTarget` INCLUDING the cross-server `@N` collision — plus all preserved `l4nf` gate/direction-token assertions. The e2e spec keeps the fast-path animated-completion guard and adds a deterministic **mask-not-stuck** poll (`toHaveCount(0)` within budget); a strict "never-flashes-on-fast-path" assert and the >300ms/failure paths are documented in the `.spec.md` as unit-covered (a >300ms first write can't be deterministically forced on localhost).

### E2E host-global filesystem state (`~/.rk/settings.yaml` snapshot/restore)

`scripts/test-e2e.sh` isolates the **tmux server** (dedicated `-L rk-test-e2e` socket) and the **port**, but NOT `$HOME` — so any e2e that exercises a feature persisting to `~/.rk/` (settings, VAPID keys, push subscriptions) POSTs against the **developer's REAL host file**. `board-list-reorder.spec.ts` therefore snapshots the developer's `~/.rk/settings.yaml` and restores it around the suite so a curated board order (or any other setting) is never clobbered by test residue:

- **`beforeAll`** raw-byte-reads `settings.yaml` (`readFileSync(path, "utf8")`). Absent detection is **ENOENT-only**: a `code === "ENOENT"` read error means "no file to restore" (afterAll then deletes any residue); ANY other read error (EACCES/EIO — the file EXISTS but couldn't be snapshotted) is **rethrown**, so afterAll never `rmSync`-deletes real settings on a failed snapshot.
- **`afterAll`** (always runs, even on test failure) restores VERBATIM: write the original bytes back if the file existed, else `rmSync({ force: true })` to delete residue — a byte-identical round-trip. Teardown errors are swallowed so they never mask a test failure.
